# ctxprune: token-saving ideas (scoped master list)

1061 ideas, one list. Scope: works in all harnesses, with all models, against cloud API models, running locally. Status: **scope** 817 (in scope), **eval** 218 (measurement/benchmark/safety tooling), **conditional** 26 (needs the tool call rewritten before it runs, so not universal). ★ = the finders rated it medium or large (156 in-scope ideas). 342 further ideas were dropped as harness-, model- or cloud-specific or off-scope; see dropped.md.

Tags: **L** lossless, **R** deterministic rule that drops information, **M** model-based, **X** measurement/infra. `MCP` = applies to MCP tool traffic only. `busts-cache` = rewrites already-sent messages, so provider prefix caches miss from that point. Every estimate is an unverified hypothesis; 'Measured'/'Verified here' means a finder ran a quick check that was not reproduced.

## Contents

- Measure first (125)
- Whitespace & terminal hygiene (33)
- Repetition & logs (70)
- Structured data (144)
- Tool-specific noise packs (154)
- Cross-call dedup (stateful) (48)
- Hook overhead & coverage (62)
- Fix it at the source (PreToolUse) (47)
- Model-side (175)
- Reversible substitution (legend) (12)
- Adjacent surfaces (27)
- Safety nets (66)
- Other agents and IDEs (23)
- Code-aware views (11)
- Prompt-cache management (8)
- Media, browser & documents (15)
- Compaction & session lifecycle (2)
- RAG and vector pipelines (15)
- Output tokens and thinking (6)
- Private corpus & telemetry (18)

## Measure first

- **#2** [X] `eval` **Output-size histogram** — What share of tool-result tokens sits below vs above the 6k-char threshold? Shows whether the threshold is the biggest lever.
- **#3** [X] `eval` **Tokenizer ground truth** — Compare count_tokens vs chars/4 vs tiktoken on ~500 real outputs per content type; tests the unit="chars" assumption.
- **#4** [X] `eval` **Cache-aware cost model** — A saved token is worth ~1.25x write plus 0.1x per remaining turn; rank savings in dollars, not tokens.
- **#5** [X] `eval` **Net savings accounting** — Net = saved - note - recovery (greps of the saved original, cat of it, command re-runs); measure the recovery rate on real sessions.
- **#6** [X] `eval` **Replay harness** — Feed recorded tool events through hook.run offline and print a per-rule savings table, so every idea can be A/B'd in seconds without an LLM.
- **#7** [X] `eval` **Task-success A/B** — Run 20-30 fixed repo tasks with the hook on and off; compare tokens, turns and pass rate. Catches 'saved tokens, cost more turns'.
- **#8** [X] `eval` **Regret log** — Whenever the original gets grepped, record which rule removed what was needed.
- **#157** [X] `eval, MCP` **Cache-miss forensics: idle expiry versus prefix mutation** — Classify turns with large cache_creation_input_tokens as an idle gap past the cache TTL or a prefix change (tool list, CLAUDE.md edit, skill listing, dates, MCP tool order). A cache write costs 1.25x versus 0.1x for a read.
  - Test: Parse usage and timestamps in transcript JSONL; flag gaps over 5 minutes; diff request prefixes via a logging proxy; verify that ENABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_1H changes cache_creation totals.
- **#171** [X] `eval` **Reader-free reconstruction proxy for selection algorithms** — Reader-LLM QA is slow and noisy for comparing selection tweaks. Proxy: how well a small LM reconstructs the dropped atoms from the kept ones (loss or masked fill-in accuracy). Validate by rank correlation with existing QA results before trusting it.
  - Test: Compute proxy for ctxprune, LLMLingua-2 and random deletion at 0.33/0.5; check it orders methods like the stored QA table, then sweep selection variants cheaply.
- **#174** [X] `eval` **Cluster-aware paired bootstrap, MDE and A/A noise floor** — Questions share chunks and sources, so per-question CIs can be too tight. Resample by chunk or repo, pair methods per question, and publish a minimum detectable effect before each A/B. Add A/A repeats (keep=1.0, 3 reader seeds) as the noise floor.
  - Test: Re-aggregate cached eval_qa answers with 10k chunk-level resamples; compare interval width to naive binomial; rerun reader 3 times at keep=1.0 to get spread.
- **#175** [X] `eval` **Closed-book reader control for QA contamination** — Run the reader with empty context and with an unrelated chunk. SWE-smith, Toucan, SQuAD and HotpotQA are public and may sit in reader pretraining, and the teacher wrote the questions. Report accuracy minus closed-book hits; drop questions answerable without context.
  - Test: Call eval_qa answer with context='' on all 378 questions; list closed-book correct ones by source; recompute headline accuracy on the remainder.
- **#176** [X] `eval` **Split QA errors into abstain versus confident-wrong** — Scoring merges 'unknown' with wrong answers. An abstention costs one recovery read of the saved original; a confident wrong answer silently corrupts the next action. Report correct, abstain and confident-wrong per method, source and keep rate, and gate on the last.
  - Test: Re-score cached reader outputs, bucketing exact 'unknown' replies separately; compare confident-wrong rate at keep 0.5 versus 1.0 and versus LLMLingua-2-large.
- **#178** [X] `eval` **Per-call randomized holdout inside normal sessions** — Hook hashes tool_use_id to leave ~15% of eligible outputs uncompressed and logs the arm. Compare re-runs, greps of the saved original, and turns after each result between arms, clustered by session. Causal evidence from daily use with no extra task runs.
  - Test: Verify that hook input carries tool_use_id; add CTXPRUNE_HOLDOUT=0.15 with arm logging; after a few hundred outputs compare follow-up-action rates by arm with a session-clustered bootstrap.
- **#179** [X] `eval` **Shadow mode: compute and log, return original** — New rules and thresholds run in the hook but pass the original through, logging before/after sizes, rule fired, and the would-be output. Builds a real-world corpus and a savings forecast with zero behavior risk; promote a rule after clean offline QA.
  - Test: Set CTXPRUNE_SHADOW=1 for a week with secret redaction on the log; report per-rule fire rate and forecast tokens saved; then replay logged originals through reader QA.
- **#180** [X] `eval` **Length- and depth-matched canary eval for hook-sized inputs** — Held-out chunks are at most 500 tokens, but the hook compresses outputs over 6,000 chars, often far longer. Stitch real outputs to 2k, 8k and 30k tokens, plant verbatim canary facts at 10/50/90% depth, and measure reader recall after compression.
  - Test: Build 3 lengths x 3 depths x 50 canaries from the golden corpus; compare canary recall at keep 0.5 by depth and length.
- **#181** [X] `eval` **Add count, existence, absence questions; stratify answers by class** — QGEN demands verbatim-span answers, favoring protected tokens that are kept free. Add programmatic-gold questions: how many FAILED lines, is any warning present, which file is NOT listed. Report accuracy by question type and by protected versus prose answer.
  - Test: Script 100 count/existence questions with regex-derived gold over logs and test outputs; score reader on original versus 0.5 kept versus collapsed-duplicates output.
- **#185** [X] `eval` **Claude-tier reader matrix: Haiku, Sonnet, Opus** — Existing readers are the Qwen teacher and DeepSeek. Claude Code users run Claude models. Re-run the same compressed contexts with each Claude tier to see whether method gaps and the best keep rate depend on reader strength; a weak reader may need gentler rates.
  - Test: Reuse cached compressed contexts; answer 378 questions with three Claude tiers; fit method x reader interaction; if real, pick the rate per model.
- **#187** [X] `eval, MCP` **Passthrough logging proxy: payload anatomy per agent** — Same base-URL proxy, log-only: split each request into system prompt, tool schemas, history, tool results and IDE auto-attached context (open files, diagnostics). Transcripts omit most of that; shows what each agent really sends.
  - Test: Point Aider, Codex or Cline at a 30-line logging proxy for one task; tokenise each request part and print a table of shares.
- **#213** [X] `eval` **Serial read-only call fusion detector** — Find runs of 3+ consecutive read-only calls (ls, cat, rg) that one command could replace. Each removed turn skips another cache read of the whole prefix, which may outweigh output trimming. context-mode's batch_execute targets this.
  - Test: Scan the transcript ledger for such runs; estimate saved turns times mean cached-prefix tokens, with no model calls needed.
- **#269** [X] _tiny_ `eval` **Elision marker ablation at deletion points** — detok drops runs silently, so the reader sees spliced words as adjacent and cannot tell absence from deletion. Compare no marker, '…', per-run '[-N words]' and per-line '(+14 lines)' at equal total tokens. Markers may cut confident-wrong answers, or cost more than they earn.
  - Test: 100 QA items at 0.33 and 0.5 with each variant; compare accuracy, tokens, and abstain versus confident-wrong split on questions whose answer was dropped.
- **#272** [X] `eval` **Next-turn output tokens as hidden cost of compression** — Output tokens cost the most per token (reportedly 5x input) and were the largest billed share in one local session. Gaps in compressed text may lengthen thinking or hedging. Net-savings accounting counts only input tokens.
  - Test: In a 15% holdout arm or recorded replays, pair next-turn output_tokens (incl. thinking) for compressed versus original on 40 oversized results; report mean delta.
- **#275** [X] `eval` **Survival-turn distribution of tool results; skip short-lived contexts** — A saved token earns 0.1x of input price per turn it survives, so value depends on remaining turns. Measure turns until compaction or session end per result, split main, subagent and headless runs. Short-lived contexts may not repay hook latency.
  - Test: Parse session JSONLs including sidechain files; per tool_result count later assistant turns before compaction or end; report median and p90 by context type and by turn index.
- **#276** [X] `eval` **Compaction-avoidance credit: price a compaction, count those avoided** — A compaction costs a summary call (cached input read plus output at the higher output rate), then a fresh cache write of the new prefix. Value saved tokens by compactions avoided and report 'headroom turns gained', not only raw tokens.
  - Test: Find compaction summary records in transcripts, sum billed usage of that turn plus the next turn's cache_creation; divide by tokens per compaction cycle to price one saved token.
- **#277** [X] `eval` **Latency break-even: hook milliseconds versus prefill time saved** — Every compressed call costs hook CPU (about 2 s without serve) but shortens API prefill on the next turn. Nobody has measured whether that nets positive for 6k-30k char outputs. Report ms spent per 1k tokens saved.
  - Test: Via a logging proxy, time next-turn time-to-first-token with 5k versus 10k extra uncached tokens; compare against hook wall time with and without serve.
- **#281** [X] `eval` **Leave-one-out and Shapley savings attribution across stacked rules** — Stacked rules (dedupe, noise packs, model) overlap: whichever runs first claims the savings, so a sequential table misranks them. Credit each rule its Shapley value over all subsets; drop rules with near-zero marginal value.
  - Test: On the replay corpus run all 2^6 rule subsets, compute per-rule marginal and Shapley savings, and compare the ranking with the order-dependent sequential table.
- **#287** [X] _small_ `eval` **Classify non-main-loop API calls by model, size and purpose** — Besides the agent loop, clients make small-model utility requests (titles, topic checks, subagents). Group by model, max_tokens and system-prompt hash; tally billed share. Candidate fixes: response cache by request hash, or env switches like DISABLE_NON_ESSENTIAL_MODEL_CALLS (reportedly).
  - Test: Run a 30-minute session through the logging proxy; print per-group request count, input and output tokens; flag groups with byte-identical repeated requests.
- **#297** [X] `eval` **Trivial-baseline arms: head/tail, uniq, stopwords, random drop** — Contrarian control: at equal Claude-token output, does the 140M model beat a head+tail cut, line dedupe, stopword-only deletion, or random deletion that keeps protected atoms? If a cheap arm ties on reader QA, the model's latency and 143 MB buy nothing there.
  - Test: Build four arms matched to ctxprune output token counts on the agent-text QA set; score with the same reader; report wins per content type.
- **#301** [X] `eval` **ACON-style failure-pair loop to refine noise-pack rules** — Replay tasks with full versus compressed output; for pairs where only the compressed run fails, a strong LLM names the dropped lines that mattered and proposes rule exemptions. ACON (Kang et al., Microsoft, 2025) refines compression guidelines this way, reporting 26-54% lower peak tokens.
  - Test: Take 15 regret-log cases or paired replays; let Sonnet propose exemptions; re-run, check fixes flip without raising output size over 5%.
- **#373** [X] `eval` **Injection-susceptibility A/B: compressed versus original output** — Deleting words may strip the framing that marks text as untrusted page content while a planted imperative survives, or the reverse. Plant instructions in fetched pages and issue bodies, compress at 0.5, count how often a Claude reader acts on them.
  - Test: 30 pages, 3 injection styles; reader sees original or 0.5 compressed; compare follow rates per arm with a bootstrap interval over pages.
- **#388** [X] `eval` **Benchmark against framework-native compressors at equal output tokens** — Compare the integrations with what RAG users already use: LangChain EmbeddingsFilter and LLMChainExtractor, LlamaIndex SentenceEmbeddingOptimizer, LongLLMLingua. Match output tokens across arms so the README can say where ctxprune wins or loses.
  - Test: One harness, one retrieval set, equal-token arms per method; report reader accuracy and added per-query latency, with paired bootstrap intervals.
- **#389** [X] `eval` **Break-even: cached compressed corpus versus per-query top-k retrieval** — For small static corpora, compress once and place everything in a cached prompt prefix instead of fetching chunks per query. Model the break-even from corpus tokens, queries per cache lifetime and Anthropic's published cache read/write multipliers (verify current figures).
  - Test: Spreadsheet model: measure a 50k-token corpus raw and compressed, compare against top-8 chunk retrieval at 1, 10 and 100 queries per cache lifetime.
- **#440** [X] _small_ `eval` **Add cold-rewrite term to per-token savings value** — Each cache expiry re-bills every surviving context token at 1.25x, so a saved token is worth 1.25 + 0.1 per turn + 1.15 per later cold rewrite. Users with habitual idle gaps benefit more; derive min_chars and rate from their gap history. Extends #4.
  - Test: From 20 transcripts count requests after gaps over 5 minutes per session; recompute break-even per tool result with and without the term; count sub-6,000-char outputs that flip.
- **#448** [X] `eval` **Measure output copy-tax: written lines already seen verbatim** — Write, Edit, Agent prompts and heredocs often retype text the model read earlier: code moves, extractions, pasted context for subagents. Count output tokens whose lines match prior tool_results; if large, steer to sed -n ranges, git mv and path references instead.
  - Test: For each Write, Edit, Agent and heredoc input, find the fraction of lines over 25 chars present verbatim in earlier tool_results; report tokens by tool, split move versus new code.
- **#470** [X] `eval` **Cost-weighted eval: weight items by tokens times surviving turns** — QA accuracy averages items equally, but the bill does not. Weight each source and size bucket by its transcript token-bill share (tool-result tokens times turns survived, cache-priced) and report the weighted headline plus the best keep rate under those weights.
  - Test: Reweight the cached per-source eval_qa results by shares from the transcript ledger; check whether headline accuracy and the best keep rate change versus the unweighted mean.
- **#487** [X] `eval` **Free ground truth for CI log packs from Actions API** — Job JSON lists steps[].conclusion and check-run annotations carry the error messages, so 'which step failed and why' has an answer key without an LLM judge. Build a corpus from public failed runs and score fold rules by whether those lines survive.
  - Test: Fetch 30 failed runs with gh api; store jobs JSON, annotations and log; script a survival check of annotation text and failing step name after each rule.
- **#495** [X] _tiny_ `eval` **Docs-as-tests: executable checks for README and AGENTS claims** — Claims can drift: README says plugin download ~180 MB, install prints 143 MB; ~2 s load, ~50 ms no-op and 'seq 1 3000 left alone' have no test. prune(), CLI and integrations default protect=False despite the 'without mangling IDs' headline.
  - Test: Put each claim in claims.toml with a command and tolerance; run in CI on a fresh venv and HF cache; fail on drift; test prune() defaults on an ID-heavy log.
- **#504** [X] `eval, MCP` **Head-to-head versus rtk, Headroom and similar agent-side compressors** — Item #388 covers RAG compressors only. Agent-side tools differ: rtk rewrites commands per tool, Headroom routes by content type with retrieval, other hook-based trimmers cut JSON and logs. Run each on one recorded Bash/MCP corpus at equal output tokens, scoring reader accuracy.
  - Test: Replay 100 recorded outputs through each tool's filters; match output tokens; ask 5 questions per output; report accuracy, savings and winners by command family.
- **#529** [X] **Shape-keyed format ladder: cheapest encoding per JSON shape** — Measure each rung per shape: pretty, minified, unquoted keys, YAML, dotted paths, TOON-like, TSV. On a nested single object, minified JSON (85 tokens) beat YAML (96) and dotted paths (104); unquoted array keys saved about 3% (o200k proxy). Convert only when a rung wins.
  - Test: Build 5 shapes (flat record, nested object, uniform array, ragged array, deep config) from captured output; count tokens per rung with the count_tokens API; run reader QA per rung.
- **#565** [X] `eval` **Scorer false-positive null: containment match accepts hedges and substrings** — eval_qa scores gold-substring-in-answer. Checked in a snippet: gold '1' matches 'unknown 10 files', 'in' matches 'main', hedged lists and 'not 404' also pass. Add a mismatched-gold null, word-boundary match, answer-length cap; report strict EM beside the headline.
  - Test: Score each cached reader answer against another question's gold from the same chunk; report chance-level EM per method, then rescore all cached answers strictly and compare gaps between methods.
- **#566** [X] `eval` **Gold-span survival ceiling splits QA misses: deleted versus misread** — Every gold answer is a verbatim span, so check deterministically whether it survives in each compressed context. Accuracy then splits into deleted (selection fault), kept but misread (reader or fragmentation fault) and guessed. Stratify survival by answer position and distance to window seams.
  - Test: Using cached contexts_*.json, regex-match gold per method with whitespace tolerance; cross-tabulate survival against EM; report survival by source and keep rate with zero LLM calls.
- **#567** [X] `eval` **Reader-prompt parity: eval framing versus the real hook note** — eval_qa tells the reader words were removed and offers 'unknown'. The live agent instead sees the hook note, which says IDs, numbers and paths are kept exactly, plus its command and task. Compare framings; the note may change over-trust or recovery.
  - Test: Same cached compressed contexts, three READ prompts: eval text, verbatim hook note plus command, no mention. Compare EM and unknown rate per arm with a paired bootstrap over questions.
- **#568** [X] `eval` **Recovery-augmented QA: accuracy against total tokens including refetches** — Give the reader a simulated grep-original tool returning matching lines from the uncompressed chunk. Score accuracy against context plus recovered tokens plus extra turns, per keep rate. The frontier shows whether deeper compression plus recovery beats gentler compression alone.
  - Test: For 100 questions at keep 0.33 and 0.5, allow one grep call; log hit rate and tokens fetched; plot accuracy versus total tokens beside the no-tool curve and original.
- **#569** [X] `eval` **Lockbox question set and look ledger against eval tuning** — Hundreds of rules, thresholds and rates will be tuned against the same 378 questions, inflating headlines through adaptive overfitting. Seal a fresh question set from repos and servers outside the corpus, open it once per release, and log the number of A/Bs for FDR correction.
  - Test: Mint 200 questions from groups absent from the corpus; hold until a release; compare the headline there against the tuned set and report the shrinkage.
- **#570** [X] `eval` **Always-valid sequential harm test on the holdout stream** — With the per-call holdout (#178) running, apply an always-valid sequential test (mSPRT or confidence sequence) per rule on rerun and refetch rates against a non-inferiority margin. A harmful rule can auto-disable within days instead of waiting for a fixed-sample readout.
  - Test: Simulate arms with 3 percent versus 6 percent refetch rates; compare days-to-detect and false-alarm rate of the sequential test with a fixed-N test at 500 calls.
- **#572** [X] `eval` **Exposure-conditioned task A/B: pick tasks where the hook fires** — Most small repo tasks emit no output above 6,000 chars, so an on/off A/B mostly measures nothing and dilutes effects. Pilot hook-fire counts per task, keep tasks with several firings, and regress outcome on compressed-token share (dose-response) beside the intent-to-treat result.
  - Test: Run 40 candidate tasks once in shadow mode, count would-be firings; report how many exceed 3; estimate the power of a 25-task A/B using only those tasks.
- **#573** [X] `eval` **Fresh agent tasks by bug injection, free of memorisation** — Public SWE tasks may be memorised by the agent model. Inject a mutation (flipped condition, renamed import) into a green commit of a verbose-test repo, use the failing test as grader, and mint unlimited fresh tasks whose failure logs exceed the hook threshold.
  - Test: Generate 30 mutants across 3 repos; confirm the baseline agent fixes at least half and logs exceed 6,000 chars in at least one third; use as #7's task pool.
- **#574** [X] `eval` **Question-writer independence: re-author QA with another model family** — Qwen writes the questions and also labeled the training data, so questions may target what that teacher keeps. Regenerate questions with a different family and with scripted templates; check whether the original-versus-compressed gap widens. Distinct from reader and label diversity.
  - Test: Write 5 questions per chunk with a second family on 100 held-out chunks; score the same reader on original and 0.5 kept; compare the gap to the Qwen-written set.
- **#576** [X] `eval` **Accuracy versus realised Claude tokens: frontier curves, not nominal-rate points** — Methods are compared at nominal rates, but realised ratios differ and are counted in the reader's tokenizer. Sweep five rates per method, count output with Anthropic's token-counting endpoint (reportedly free), interpolate accuracy at equal Claude tokens and report area under the curve.
  - Test: Reuse cached contexts at several rates for ctxprune and LLMLingua-2; recount tokens; compare accuracy at 1k, 2k, 3k output tokens and AUC with 10k bootstrap resamples.
- **#589** [X] `eval` **Eval parity with the shipped hook configuration** — eval_quick and eval_qa score arms with measure=reader tokens, with or without ids; none combines the character budget with force_protected as the hook does, and none includes the note or the 6000-char gate. Headline AGENTS.md numbers may not describe shipped behaviour.
  - Test: Run eval_qa through hook.run with the real note and character budget at 0.5; compare accuracy and achieved token ratio with the measure= arm.
- **#621** [X] `eval` **Run-length token price curves per character class** — Probe Claude's tokenizer via count_tokens for runs of 1..120 of each char. The o200k proxy shows 80 dashes or equals cost 1 token, 120 zeros 40, 120 block glyphs 30, CRLF nothing extra. Ranks rules and kills worthless ones.
  - Test: Script count_tokens over run lengths for 60 characters including whitespace, digits, glyphs; compare with o200k; list classes costing over 0.5 token per char.
- **#629** [X] `eval` **Session-hash arm assignment for whole-session cost A/B** — Per-call holdout (#178) measures local follow-ups, not whole-session cost. Hash session_id to an arm inside the hook, no state file, so whole sessions are compressed or untouched. Run A/A first to size noise; compare cache-weighted tokens per completed task.
  - Test: Log arm and usage per session for two weeks; run A/A first; report minimum detectable effect on cache-weighted tokens before drawing any A/B conclusion.
- **#655** [X] `eval` **Retrieval query-source ablation: user prompt, command, assistant preamble** — #89 and #209 leave the query source open. Hook input includes transcript_path, so the assistant's last text before the tool call (its stated intent) is readable. Compare it with the user prompt and the bare command as BM25 query.
  - Test: On 30 outputs with known answer spans, measure answer-chunk recall at equal tokens for each query source and their concatenation.
- **#657** [X] `eval` **Note-only placebo arm: append the note, delete nothing** — Separate behaviour caused by the note's mere existence (curiosity reads of the saved file, extra turns) from behaviour caused by missing content. The placebo arm returns the full original plus the real note; compare recoveries and tokens against real compression and no hook.
  - Test: Add CTXPRUNE_PLACEBO=1; run 20-50 paired claude -p tasks in all three arms; count Reads or greps of the saved path and total tokens per arm.
- **#659** [X] `eval` **Rate-limit headroom: peak uncached input tokens per minute** — Tool results are new tokens, so they weigh far more on input-per-minute limits than in a read-dominated bill (one local session: 428k cache-write versus 4.13M cache-read). Subagent fan-out bursts hit 429s first. Value compression in that currency too.
  - Test: Bucket transcript usage by minute (input plus cache_creation); subtract compressible result tokens; compare peak minutes against documented tier limits; check which usage fields the provider counts.
- **#660** [X] `eval` **Reconcile ledger claims against the official organisation cost report** — For API-key users, give hook-on and hook-off runs separate API keys or workspaces and read the provider's usage and cost report (Anthropic's Admin API, reportedly) as ground truth, instead of trusting self-computed savings.
  - Test: Run identical scripted claude -p task sets under two keys for a week; pull cost per key per model; compare the delta with the ledger's claimed dollars.
- **#670** [X] `eval` **Baseline arms: history-level reducers (middle-out, trim_messages)** — Gateways already ship lossy history reducers: OpenRouter's middle-out transform and LiteLLM's trim_messages (both reportedly). Add them as baselines at equal token counts, so ctxprune claims are measured against what a base-URL swap gives for free.
  - Test: On 50 recorded long sessions, shrink to ctxprune's 0.5 token budget with each reducer; score with the existing reader QA harness.
- **#673** [X] _small_ `eval` **Exact-match reader-call cache plus Message Batches for evals** — QA sweeps re-ask identical (model, prompt) pairs across reruns and rate sweeps. A hash-keyed on-disk cache at temperature 0, with misses sent through Message Batches (50% discount per Anthropic docs), cuts the cost of measuring.
  - Test: Re-run the QA sweep twice and count second-run hits; send 200 uncached prompts through batches, compare bill and wall time.
- **#674** [X] `eval` **Ledger of tokens billed on interrupted and retried requests** — Transcripts omit requests that ended without message_stop: Esc interrupts, mid-stream 5xx, client retries. Their input and partial output are billed and then regenerated. Only a proxy sees them; this sizes the waste and any prefill-resume fix.
  - Test: Log per-request terminal state in the passthrough proxy over a day; sum input and output tokens of requests lacking message_stop, grouped by cause.
- **#689** [X] `eval` **Census of files every new session re-reads on orientation** — Each new session may re-read the same README, manifest and entry files before real work; the claude-learn tool reportedly flags files read three or more times. Census the first 15 tool calls per session by repo; frequent paths become a skeleton, pointer or digest.
  - Test: Group first 15 tool calls per session by repo from ~/.claude/projects; rank paths by share of sessions and tokens; estimate savings if the top 5 became a 300-token digest.
- **#715** [X] `eval` **Token share of markup syntax classes in real outputs** — Before building markup rules, tokenise saved outputs and transcript results by syntax class: link targets, tags and attributes, entities, escapes, image embeds, diff signs, TOC lines. Measured here (o200k) on 107 markdown files: link targets 13%, emphasis 0.2%, images 0.9%, HTML tags 1.5%.
  - Test: Run a regex classifier over ~/.claude transcript tool results plus saved originals; emit per-class token share per tool; rank rules by share times firing frequency; drop classes under 1%.
- **#786** [X] `eval` **Public code-completion sets to size the source-code weakness** — AGENTS.md admits weaker results on source code. LongBench's lcc and repobench-p sets (also used by LongCodeZip, reportedly) give public prompts to score pruning at 0.5 and 0.8 against head-truncation and no-op, calibrating the #75 skip gate.
  - Test: Prune 200 lcc and 200 repobench-p prompts per arm; have one fixed reader complete them; compare edit similarity per arm against tokens kept, split by prompt length.
- **#810** [X] `eval` **Loghub dataset matrix: lossless-rule savings per log dialect** — Run the deterministic log rules (timestamp factoring, prefix hoist, dedupe, template mining) over the public logpai/loghub 2k samples (HDFS, BGL, Spark, Android, Apache and others). Report o200k and count_tokens savings per dataset per rule, catching rules tuned only to synthetic logs.
  - Test: Clone logpai/loghub, run each rule on every *_2k.log, tabulate token deltas, and verify L rules invert exactly.
- **#812** [X] _tiny_ `eval` **Randomised prefix nonce to stop cache contamination in cost A/Bs** — Trials sharing a system prompt or forked prefix read each other's cache entries, so later arms look cheaper. Add a per-trial nonce at the start of the system prompt, or price from token counts times fixed multipliers, not billed usage.
  - Test: Run the same 10-turn task twice back to back with and without a nonce; compare cache_read on run 2 and the cost gap between arms.

- **#1390** [X] `eval` **Audit gateway cost fields for 1h writes and premium tiers** — #1042 trusts gateway spend logs as billed truth. Gateways may price every cache write at the 5m rate, collapse write and read splits, or ignore premium tiers (reportedly in some versions). Recompute cost from raw usage and published rates first.
  - Test: Send one request with a 1h cache write and one over 200K input through the gateway; compare its logged cost with a hand computation from the raw usage JSON.

## Whitespace & terminal hygiene

- **#9** [L] **Strip ANSI and OSC escapes** — Colors, cursor moves, OSC-8 hyperlinks (which duplicate the URL).
- **#10** [L] **Collapse CR progress updates** — Collapse \r progress updates to the final frame (pip, curl, wget, npm, docker, git clone).
- **#11** [L] **Normalise line endings and invisibles** — CRLF to LF, strip trailing whitespace, BOM, zero-width characters, nbsp.
- **#12** [L] **Collapse blank-line runs** — Runs of 3+ blank lines to one.
- **#13** [L] **Dedent common leading whitespace** — Display-only blocks only, never text that will be edited.
- **#14** [L] `eval` **Whitespace tokenization study** — Tabs vs spaces and long space runs in aligned columns; measure which forms the tokenizer likes.
- **#15** [L] **Glyph substitution** — Smart quotes and dashes to ASCII, box-drawing borders to a plain pipe char, check/cross/emoji to ASCII, spinner frames removed; measure tokens per char per class.
- **#16** [L] **Drop stdout/stderr duplicates** — When both streams contain the same text (2>&1 plus tee).
- **#17** [L] **Dedupe repeated failure blocks** — pytest and similar tools print each failure in the body and again in the summary.
- **#18** [L] **NFC-normalise Unicode** — Combining characters inflate tokens; tiny win.
- **#226** [R] _tiny_ **Strip Unicode tag characters and bidi controls, with a flag** — Tag block U+E0000-E007F is invisible to humans but readable by LLMs (reportedly used for hidden prompt injection); a 49-char hidden payload tokenized to 189 tokens on an o200k proxy. Strip tags and bidi overrides, add a note, save original.
  - Test: Craft output hiding an instruction in tag chars; run the hook; assert it is stripped, flagged and the original saved; count tokens before and after with tiktoken.
- **#231** [R] _tiny_ **NFKC-fold ligatures, fullwidth forms and math alphanumerics** — PDF-extracted text carries fi/fl ligatures, CJK docs use fullwidth ASCII, some sites use mathematical bold letters. NFKC folds these; a stress string went 49 to 16 tokens on the cl100k proxy. Restrict to safe ranges, since NFKC also rewrites TM, circled digits and ellipsis.
  - Test: Run pdftotext on 5 PDFs and scrape 5 fullwidth CJK pages; count characters changed by the safe-range fold; tokenize before and after; check no digits or symbols changed meaning.
- **#232** [R] _tiny_ **Repair mojibake (UTF-8 misread as cp1252)** — Sequences like a-circumflex plus euro plus tm for an apostrophe appear in curl with wrong charset, Windows logs and CSV exports. ftfy (Python library) fixes them heuristically; a short sample went 18 to 8 tokens (o200k proxy). Gate on high-byte density; keep the original.
  - Test: Run ftfy over a mixed corpus plus clean UTF-8 files containing legitimate accented characters; measure the false-fix rate and the token delta.
- **#234** [R] _small_ **Reflow hard-wrapped prose paragraphs** — man pages, RFCs, READMEs and --help prose wrap near 78 columns with deep indent, so each line pays a newline plus indent. o200k proxy on a man paragraph: 64 tokens, 60 with dedent alone, 52 when also reflowed. Skip code, lists and tables.
  - Test: Pick 20 man, RFC and README outputs; reflow only runs of wrapped lines; tokenize; ask 10 factual questions per set against the original; verify no code block merged.
- **#235** [R] _small_ **Thin line-number gutters in Read, cat -n and nl output** — A padded number plus tab or arrow on every line costs tokens (40-line sample: 440 to 320 on an o200k proxy). Drop the padding, or use a range header with every-10th-line anchors. Read is skipped by the matcher; try updatedToolOutput, or a proxy as fallback.
  - Test: Verify that PostToolUse can rewrite Read output; unpad numbers on a logged tool_response, then run an Edit to confirm it still matches; measure gutter token share and line-citation accuracy.
- **#332** [L] _small_ **Decode UTF-16 text before the binary stub decision** — PowerShell redirects, Excel 'Unicode Text' exports, .reg files and SQL Server scripts are UTF-16LE with BOM. 500 chars with interleaved NULs cost 1000 tokens vs 120 decoded (o200k proxy). Detect BOM or alternating NULs and decode; otherwise the NUL-density stub hides readable text.
  - Test: Verify that NULs survive in tool_response: write a UTF-16LE file, run cat through a real hook payload; tokenize raw versus decoded; assert text round-trips.
- **#517** [L] _small_ **Replace tree, pstree and perf call-graph rails with indentation** — Rails like '├── ', '│   ' and perf report -g's '|--12.3%--' cost tokens at every level. An 8-line tree sample went 55 to 28 o200k tokens using two-space indents. Convert only when depth stays recoverable; also drop perf's leading '#' header block.
  - Test: Run tree, pstree -p, lsblk and perf report -g --stdio on real data; convert; compare tokens; ask parent-of and depth questions on both forms.
- **#613** ★ [R] _medium_ **Stub QR codes, figlet banners and terminal block art** — Runs of 4+ lines dominated by block, braille or ASCII-art glyphs (QR from expo start, wrangler login, aws sso login; figlet; neofetch logos) carry no text. Synthetic 29x58 QR: 1,426 vs 13 tokens (o200k proxy). Stub with dimensions.
  - Test: Generate QR with qrencode -t UTF8 or python qrcode, figlet text, neofetch; run detector; confirm prose, tables and trees never match; tokenise before and after.
- **#614** [L] _small_ **Same-length ASCII for progress bars, sparklines, braille meters** — Shade, block and braille glyphs cost 1-3 tokens each, while # and . runs cost about 1 token per 80 chars. Synthetic braille bar 56 vs 9, sparkline 35 vs 7 (digit levels), shade bar 20 vs 10. Preserve run lengths and levels; extends #15.
  - Test: Capture rich, tqdm unicode-bar and btop-style output; map glyph runs and eight sparkline levels to ASCII; assert levels round-trip; tokenise; ask percent-complete questions on both.
- **#615** [R] _small_ **Strip escaped and ESC-less ANSI residue in logs** — Raw ESC is handled (#9), but JSON-encoded logs carry literal \u001b[32m, \x1b[0m, \033[ or ^[[ text, and some captures keep bare [0;32m. Synthetic: 70 vs 6 tokens (o200k proxy). Strip with an anchored SGR regex.
  - Test: Collect docker json-file logs, pino or bunyan output, gh run view --log and cat -v dumps; apply regex; count false positives such as array [0m; tokenise before and after.
- **#616** [L] _small_ **Resolve backspace overstrike from man pages and spinners** — man or nroff without col -b (reportedly when piped), perldoc, ul and script typescripts emit X\bX bold and _\bX underline; old spinners write -\b\. Synthetic NAME header x5: 50 vs 10 tokens (o200k proxy). Apply backspaces like a terminal.
  - Test: Run man -P cat ls | cat -v where man-db exists, or printf overstrike samples; compare a strip-regex result with col -b output; tokenise; assert identical text.
- **#620** [R] _tiny_ **Lowercase shouted ALL-CAPS prose lines** — Capitalised prose merges less in BPE vocabularies: 32 vs 24 tokens for a 20-word shouted build error, 24 vs 20 for a pip PATH warning (o200k proxy). Lowercase runs of 4+ caps prose words only, never identifiers, constants, hex or level tags.
  - Test: Find lines with 4+ consecutive all-caps dictionary words in 100 saved outputs; measure token share; confirm ERROR and INFO tags are already one token; QA lowercased variants.
- **#808** [L] _tiny_ **Join multi-line pretty-printed struct dumps (Rust {:#?}, pprint)** — Rust assert_eq 'left:/right:' dumps and {:#?} output spend a line and indentation per field. If brackets balance and the result is under 160 chars with no raw newline in strings, join onto one line. Synthetic nested struct: 69 vs 53 o200k tokens.
  - Test: Collect 20 cargo test failures with struct diffs; join, count tokens, and verify bracket balance is unchanged.
- **#855** [R] _small_ **Drop SGR-8 concealed spans, not just the escapes** — Jenkins raw console logs reportedly hide ConsoleNote blobs as ESC[8m ha:////base64 ESC[0m. Stripping only the escapes (#9) leaves a visible base64 run on each annotated line. Synthetic 407-char random base64: 272 o200k tokens. Delete the text between conceal and reset.
  - Test: Fetch a Jenkins pipeline's raw console log; run current ANSI strip, count residual ha:// tokens; then drop concealed spans and diff. Confirm no visible text is lost.
- **#989** [L] _tiny_ **Fold Arabic-Indic, Devanagari and Thai digits to ASCII** — NFKC (#231) leaves non-Latin decimal digits alone, yet unicodedata.decimal maps them exactly and int() parses both forms. Targets LC_ALL=ar/fa/hi/th CLI output and RTL documents. Synthetic: 24 vs 11 tokens on o200k, 46 vs 11 on cl100k.
  - Test: Generate numbers in five Nd scripts; map with unicodedata.decimal; assert int() equality; tokenise before and after; confirm is_protected still flags the resulting atoms.
- **#990** [R] _tiny_ `MCP` **ASCII-fold accents in Latin-script prose, keep original saved** — NFD-strip combining marks on French, German, Polish, Spanish or Vietnamese prose lines from MCP issues and docs; never identifiers, paths or code. Mixed European sample: 28 vs 24 tokens (o200k), 38 vs 28 (cl100k). Lossy where accents distinguish words.
  - Test: Fold 30 non-English issue bodies; count tokens; run reader QA on folded versus original; check quoted strings and code spans stay excluded.
- **#991** [R] _tiny_ `MCP` **Cap combining-mark floods and collapse lone surrogate escapes** — Zalgo text in issue titles or chat MCP output stacks many marks per letter; Python reprs of undecodable filenames show \udce9 escapes. Keep 2 marks per base; collapse \udcXX runs. Synthetic: Zalgo 92 vs 8 tokens, escapes 17 vs 6 (o200k).
  - Test: Craft Zalgo text and os.listdir over invalid bytes; apply the cap; assert base letters survive in order; tokenise; verify U+FFFD runs (measured token-neutral) are left alone.
- **#1082** [R] _tiny_ **Drop icon-font private-use glyphs and variation selectors** — eza or lsd --icons, powerline prompts, Nerd Font git graphs and FontAwesome markup emit U+E000-F8FF characters; emoji add U+FE0F. They carry no meaning for an LLM. #15 covers emoji; #233 drops icon glyphs only inside binary stubs. Synthetic icon listing: 22 vs 11 tokens.
  - Test: Grep transcripts for U+E000-F8FF and U+FE0F in Bash output; count tokens; strip only in display output, never file reads; check CJK and source identifiers stay untouched.
- **#1335** [R] _small_ **Pack one-per-line short-item lists into space-joined rows** — BPE folds a space into the next word token but bills each newline separately. Re-flow lists of whitespace-free items (git ls-files, ls -1, pip freeze, rg -l) into space-joined rows. o200k proxy: 375 to 316 and 339 to 290 tokens; comma joins gained nothing.
  - Test: Re-flow 20 saved list outputs; compare count_tokens for per-line, 80-column and 160-column rows; ask membership, count and Nth-item questions on each form.
- **#1338** [R] _tiny_ **Collapse ZWJ, skin-tone and flag emoji; drop decorative emoji** — Measured o200k proxy: a ZWJ family emoji costs 11 tokens versus 2 for the base glyph; skin-tone thumbs-up 3 versus 1; flag 4 versus 'NL' 1; warning sign with VS16 3 versus 'WARN' 1. Collapse sequences, drop decorative heading emoji in bot-written Markdown.
  - Test: Grep 30 saved GitHub and Slack MCP outputs for U+200D, skin-tone modifiers and regional indicators; measure token share; collapse; confirm reader QA on status questions is unchanged.
- **#1339** [R] **Strip DCS, APC and PM terminal-graphics strings, not just CSI** — Sixel (DCS), kitty graphics (APC) and tmux passthrough wrap opaque payloads that CSI/OSC-only regexes may leave behind. Synthetic 710-char sixel: 530 o200k tokens; real image output from chafa or timg can be far larger. Delete through the ST terminator and leave a stub.
  - Test: Emit sixel via chafa -f sixel, kitty icat and a tmux passthrough; run the current ANSI strip; count surviving payload tokens; add ST-terminated string removal and diff text lines.
- **#1340** [R] _tiny_ **Decode cat -v meta notation and octal UTF-8 escapes** — cat -v and cat -A render UTF-8 as M-bM-^@M-^Y; ls -b, sed -n l and od -c use octal escapes. Extends #619 and #227. Typographic sample, o200k proxy: 54 versus 11 tokens (M- notation), 11 versus 3 (octal). Decode valid UTF-8 runs; keep ^I.
  - Test: Run cat -A and ls -b on files named with accents and curly quotes; decode runs forming valid UTF-8; assert re-encoding matches the bytes; compare count_tokens before and after.
- **#1342** [L] _tiny_ **Collapse double spaces after sentence-ending periods in prose** — Reportedly nroff man pages, RFC text and older docstrings put two spaces after a period; the second space costs a token (3 sentences: 20 versus 17 o200k tokens). Collapse only in prose lines of 8+ words, never code, tables or indentation.
  - Test: Count period-plus-two-spaces-plus-capital hits per 1k tokens in man, --help, RFC and docstring outputs; apply the guarded collapse; compare count_tokens; diff to confirm code and table lines are untouched.

## Repetition & logs

- **#19** [L] **Consecutive identical lines** — Collapse to 'line [xN]'.
- **#20** [L] **Non-consecutive exact duplicates** — First occurrence plus '[xN total]'.
- **#21** [R] **Template mining (Drain3)** — '<TS> INFO req <ID> ok in <N>ms' becomes one template plus a variable table; test the reconstructible form vs first/last example plus count.
- **#22** [L] **Periodic multi-line blocks** — Retry loops that repeat a k-line stanza, detected and folded.
- **#23** [L] **Timestamp factoring** — Hoist the shared date to a header; per-line times or deltas. A timestamp can cost 10-15 tokens x 1000 lines.
- **#24** [L] **Column-prefix factoring** — Hoist '[INFO] [worker-3] [mod.x]' when constant over a run.
- **#25** [R] **Homogeneous pass lines to a count** — '412 PASSED lines omitted', failures kept verbatim.
- **#26** [R] **Counter sequences** — 'Downloading 17/200' x200 becomes first, last and count.
- **#27** [L] **Tree-ify file listings** — find, ls -R, rg --files: print each directory once instead of the full path per line.
- **#28** [L] **Group grep/rg hits by file** — 'file:' then indented '12: ...' instead of repeating the filename per line.
- **#29** [L] **Strip git boilerplate** — (use "git add ...") hints, hint: lines, remote: banners, repeated CRLF warnings.
- **#30** [L] **Collapse redundant diff headers** — diff --git / index / --- / +++ on display-only diffs.
- **#126** [R] _small_ **Dedupe warnings across test files and worker PIDs** — pytest's warnings summary repeats the same DeprecationWarning under many test files; Node prints '(node:PID) [DEPxxxx]' per jest worker; React act() warnings carry component stacks. Normalise PID, path and line numbers, then fold to message, count and first location.
  - Test: Collect pytest and jest runs from five repos; normalise PIDs and paths; count duplicate warning blocks and the tokens they hold.
- **#219** ★ [R] _medium_ **Fold identical-stack threads in jstack, goroutine and gdb dumps** — Thread dumps list many threads whose stacks match apart from name, id and address (idle pools, parked goroutines, 'thread apply all bt'). Normalise the header, group by stack hash, print one stack with 'x37 threads: names'. Go frames also carry +0x offsets and hex args.
  - Test: Capture jstack of a Spring app, a Go deadlock dump and gdb 'thread apply all bt'; compare grouped versus raw tokens; check blocked-thread stacks still stand out.
- **#259** [R] _small_ **Per-line marginal deflate cost as format-free novelty score** — Stream lines through one zlib or zstd stream with sync flush; bytes added per line, relative to its length, flag near-duplicates with no regex or parser. Collapse cheap runs, keep costly outliers like errors. Synthetic log check: novel lines ~80 bytes, templated ~28.
  - Test: On 50 saved build and server logs, compare lines dropped against Drain3 templates; confirm error and traceback lines survive; chars saved at equal needle recall.
- **#265** [R] _small_ **MinHash near-duplicate blocks within and across outputs** — Shingle 3-5 line blocks or paragraphs, flag blocks with Jaccard at least 0.8 against an earlier block, and replace with 'similar to lines 12-18' plus only the differing atoms. Catches repeated docstrings, stack frames and license headers that differ in a year or address.
  - Test: On 50 saved outputs, measure chars in near-duplicate but not identical blocks versus exact duplicates, then review 20 replacements for lost meaning.
- **#319** [R] _small_ **Fold health-probe access-log lines into a count** — Access logs seen via kubectl logs, docker logs or aws logs tail can be dominated by kube-probe, ELB-HealthChecker, GoogleHC and Route53 health-check hits on /healthz or /ready. Fold them to a count with first and last time; keep non-2xx probes.
  - Test: Run nginx, loop curl -A kube-probe/1.28 against /healthz beside real requests; confirm only probe 2xx lines fold and failing probes stay; count tokens saved.
- **#320** [R] _small_ **Event streams: keep terminal state per resource** — CloudFormation describe-stack-events emits IN_PROGRESS then COMPLETE rows per resource, each repeating StackId; ECS service events reportedly repeat steady-state messages; kubectl rollout status prints progress lines. Keep last state per resource key plus first failure reason.
  - Test: Build a 40-resource stack-events sample and an ECS events list; fold by LogicalResourceId; ask which resources failed and why, versus raw; compare tokens.
- **#328** ★ [R] _medium_ **Collapse diff hunks for linguist-generated and -diff paths** — Ask git check-attr for linguist-generated, -diff and binary (GitHub collapses these in PR views), plus globs like dist/, *.min.js, *.snap, *.pb.go. Print 'path +N -M generated' instead of hunks. Extends the lockfile digest; needs relaxing the git diff verbatim skip.
  - Test: Run git diff --numstat over 30 commits; flag paths by check-attr and globs; sum tokens of their hunks versus total diff tokens.
- **#329** [R] _small_ **Fold whitespace-only, CRLF-flip and deleted-file diff hunks** — When - and + lines match after stripping whitespace and CR (formatter runs, EOL conversion), print '+N/-N lines whitespace-only' with the hunk header. Reduce wholly deleted files to 'deleted path, N lines'. Display-only diffs; needs git diff off the verbatim skip.
  - Test: Scan git log -p of repos with formatter or EOL-fix commits; fold hunks; assert folded lines match after normalisation; compare tokens with git diff -w --ignore-cr-at-eol.
- **#392** [L] _small_ **Factor git blame prefixes into one header per commit run** — Hook skips git blame as verbatim, so it saves nothing. Verified here: a 236-line blame cost 9242 o200k-proxy tokens; one header per same-commit run plus 'lineno TAB code' cost 3105 (time of day omitted). Code stays byte-exact.
  - Test: Run blame on 20 files; regroup; assert code columns equal the original after parsing; tokenize; confirm 'who last changed line N' answers match; leave --porcelain alone.
- **#394** ★ [R] _medium_ **Collapse ignored and vendored subtrees in find, ls -R, du** — Verified here: 4000 find lines under site-packages cost 153k o200k-proxy tokens; per-root 'dir/ (N files)' lines cost 69. Collapse subtrees matched by git check-ignore --stdin or a fixed list (node_modules, .venv, dist, target); complements tree-ify and rg rewrites.
  - Test: Run find ., ls -R, tree and du in a JS and a Python repo; collapse; confirm source files stay listed and the agent still finds its target; compare tokens.
- **#395** [R] _small_ **Fold per-path errno lines from find, du and grep -r** — Traversal tools print one stderr line per path for 'Permission denied', 'No such file', 'Is a directory', 'Too many levels of symbolic links'. Group by errno suffix into count, shared parent directories and first three paths, leaving stdout hits and exit status untouched.
  - Test: As a non-root user run find / -name x, du -a / and grep -r foo /; fold stderr; compare tokens; assert real matches and exit code are unchanged.
- **#397** [R] _tiny_ **Fold per-file echo lines from tar -xv, unzip, cp -v** — Bulk verbose commands (tar -xv, unzip, cp -rv, rsync -av, chmod -Rv, mkdir -pv) print one line per file that succeeded. Fold to total count, per-directory histogram and the first and last entry; keep any line with an error word.
  - Test: Extract a 3000-file tarball and zip with verbose flags; fold; verify the count equals tar -t; tokenize before and after; inject one failing entry and check it survives.
- **#410** ★ [L] _medium_ **Key-value metric lines with stable keys to header plus rows** — HF Trainer dict reprs, Keras 'loss: x - accuracy: y' epoch lines and logfmt repeat every key name on every row. Print the key list once, then values only; off-schema lines stay inline. 20 synthetic Trainer lines: 760 vs 406 tokens (o200k).
  - Test: Capture a real Trainer and a Keras fit log; convert runs of same-key lines to columns; tokenise; ask 'epoch of best val_loss' on both forms.
- **#411** ★ [R] _large_ **Decimate per-step training curves, keep extremes and anomalies** — Per-step loss, lr and grad_norm lines run to thousands. Keep first and last, running min and max, NaN/inf, spikes past k sigma, eval and checkpoint lines; fold the rest into 'steps 100-900: loss 2.1 to 1.4'. Unlike counter folding (#26). Original saved.
  - Test: Take a 5k-step Trainer or Lightning log; ask final loss, best eval step, whether it diverged; compare answers and tokens, decimated versus original.
- **#417** ★ [R] _medium_ **Fold identical lines across distributed ranks and workers** — torchrun/DDP '[rank3]:', Ray '(pid=1234, ip=...)' and mpirun '[1,3]<stdout>:' prefixes yield N copies of each line. Strip the prefix, fold identical lines to '[ranks 0-7]', keep rank-specific lines attributed. Not the pytest warning fold (#126).
  - Test: Run a 4-process gloo torchrun script that logs and warns; measure duplicated token share; confirm a rank-only NaN or OOM line stays attributed to its rank.
- **#423** [L] _small_ **Folded-stack profiles: rewrite as prefix tree with counts** — Flamegraph input ('a;b;c 17' from stackcollapse scripts, py-spy --format raw) repeats shared frame prefixes on every line. Print a trie with indentation and sample counts. Synthetic 6 stacks: 107 vs 34 tokens (o200k proxy).
  - Test: Capture py-spy or perf on a busy program; convert folded lines to tree; assert the tree expands back to identical lines; tokenise; ask for the hottest leaf path.
- **#425** ★ [R] _medium_ **Severity-run folding: collapse DEBUG/INFO runs between warnings** — Detect level tokens (log4j, Python logging, glog I/W/E, syslog PRI, pino numeric). Collapse runs of TRACE/DEBUG/INFO to 'N lines, time span, top 3 templates'; keep WARN and above verbatim plus 2 context lines. Ceiling: 20 DEBUG lines, 759 vs 24 tokens (o200k proxy).
  - Test: Run on LogHub Spark, HDFS and OpenStack samples plus a CI log; count tokens dropped; verify every WARN/ERROR line and context survives; ask what happened before the first error.
- **#430** [R] _small_ **Regroup interleaved logs by request, trace or thread id** — Concurrent services interleave many requests. Group lines by correlation key (req-uuid, trace_id, thread, pod), print the key once as a header, keep order inside each group, add per-line time offsets. A 32-hex id costs 21 tokens per line (o200k proxy).
  - Test: Use LogHub OpenStack samples (request ids) and a multi-worker server log; measure id token share and savings; check an agent reconstructs one request's failure sequence.
- **#478** ★ [R] _medium_ **Fold identical failures across CI matrix legs** — gh run view --log prefixes lines with job names, so a 6-leg matrix prints the same traceback and setup six times. After normalising timestamps and paths, print identical blocks once with 'also in: leg names'; legs with different failures stay in full.
  - Test: Pull a failed run with a 6-leg matrix via gh; fold; assert every distinct failing test name survives and each leg is listed; compare tokens against raw.
- **#507** ★ [R] _medium_ **lsof: fold per-thread duplicate rows or inject -Ki** — Linux lsof repeats each process's fds once per thread, differing only in TID and TASKCMD. Measured here: 507 of 1,285 rows were repeats, about 22k of 49k o200k tokens. Fold into 'x N threads' rows, or rewrite to lsof -Ki.
  - Test: Run lsof on a multithreaded server (java, node) raw, folded and with -Ki; compare tokens; confirm the same files, ports and PIDs remain visible.
- **#510** [R] _small_ **Coarsen dmesg and journalctl monotonic stamps; elide repeats** — dmesg prefixes like '[    0.123456]' cost 3,080 of 10,410 o200k tokens in this VM's 385-line dmesg. Truncate to milliseconds or whole seconds and drop stamps equal to the previous line's. Differs from wall-clock hoisting: the microsecond digits are the cost.
  - Test: Tokenise dmesg and journalctl -o short-monotonic with full, millisecond and elided-repeat stamps; ask 'what happened just before the first error' to confirm ordering still resolves.
- **#515** [R] _small_ **ss, netstat, conntrack: aggregate socket rows by state and port** — From memory: busy hosts list hundreds of ESTAB and TIME-WAIT rows. Keep LISTEN and unusual-state rows verbatim; merge the rest into 'ESTAB :443 <- 10.0.0.0/24 x212 (nginx)'. Merges rows rather than dropping columns (#57), so counts answer load questions directly.
  - Test: Capture ss -tanp and netstat -tan during a local load test (ab, wrk); compare tokens; answer 'who is connected to :8080' and 'how many TIME-WAIT' on both.
- **#516** [L] _small_ **sysctl -a: fold per-interface and per-CPU key families** — Measured here: sysctl -a was 10,650 o200k tokens; net.ipv4.conf.* took 2,930 and neigh.* 1,285, and 30 of 33 conf keys were identical across all six interfaces. Print 'net.ipv4.conf.{all,default,eth0}.key = v' once and list only exceptions.
  - Test: Run sysctl -a on a host with docker bridges and many CPUs; fold; expand back with a script and diff against the original; compare tokens.
- **#518** ★ [R] _medium_ **Collapse git ref series in ls-remote, tag, branch, fetch** — GitHub remotes advertise refs/pull/N/head and /merge per PR; tag -l, branch -r and fetch '[new branch]' lines can run to hundreds. Print ranges and counts ('refs/pull/1..N head+merge x2N'); keep heads, default branch, newest tags, refs named in the command.
  - Test: Run git ls-remote on a large GitHub repo, git tag -l, and a first git fetch --all; collapse; compare tokens; check the agent still finds a named tag or PR.
- **#601** [R] _small_ **Hoist shared source body across parametrized failures** — One @parametrize or test.each failing 12 ways prints 12 blocks with identical function source, differing only in the [id], param line and E lines. Print the shared body once, then one compact param plus E-line group per id. Beyond #17 and #478.
  - Test: Generate 50 failing parametrized cases in pytest and jest; factor shared bodies; assert every id and distinct assert value survives; compare tokens; ask which params fail.
- **#653** [M] _small_ **Sub-line n-gram typicality prior built from earlier family outputs** — Keep a hashed word n-gram count table per command family, digits masked, from prior outputs. Phrases frequent across runs, even inside lines whose variable parts differ (trailing hints, shared prefixes), get demoted. Catches what #142 and #261 miss. Kilobytes, no inference.
  - Test: Build tables from 5 runs of npm install or pytest; score the 6th; count demoted phrases the classifier kept and check needle recall.
- **#695** [R] _small_ **Dedupe Go race detector reports by access-site set** — go test -race prints a WARNING: DATA RACE block per goroutine pair. Measured: a small test gave 4 reports (1,768 of 1,878 o200k tokens) but 2 unique sets of top-frame file:line. Keep the first per key with a count; TSan likewise.
  - Test: Run go test -race with 20 goroutines on 3 shared variables; count reports versus unique keys; verify every distinct racing line pair and read/write kind survives.
- **#698** [R] _small_ **Cut cause messages repeated inside wrapper exception messages** — new RuntimeException(cause) copies cause.toString() into its own message, older Spring appends '; nested exception is', and .NET AggregateException embeds inner messages before '--->'. Each Caused by line then repeats text already printed. Replace the embedded copy with '...' when the cause line follows.
  - Test: Collect 30 Spring bean-creation and Hibernate failures plus .NET AggregateException traces; measure the share of characters that are substring repeats; confirm the root-cause message survives intact.
- **#699** [R] _small_ **.NET console logger: join two-line records, fold request pairs** — The default .NET console logger (dotnet run, ASP.NET Core, EF Core) reportedly prints 'info: Category[eventId]' then the message on an indented second line. Join to one line, then fold 'Request starting'/'Request finished' pairs and EF 'Executed DbCommand' boilerplate. The join is lossless.
  - Test: Capture dotnet run output for a webapi sample after 20 curl requests; tokenise before and after; verify status codes, paths and SQL text survive.
- **#782** [L] _tiny_ **Word-diff form for long-line modified pairs in git diff** — Minified JSON, lockfile entries, Markdown paragraphs and SQL changes print the whole old and new line. When a -/+ pair shares most words, emit one line with [-old-]{+new+} markers. Synthetic paragraph with two edits: 358 to 244 o200k tokens.
  - Test: Take git diff output from doc, lockfile and config commits; count tokens under git diff --word-diff=plain versus default; ask what-changed questions of a reader on both forms.
- **#793** [R] _small_ **Fold healthy same-stem pod rows from DaemonSets and ReplicaSets** — kubectl get pods -A lists aws-node-x7k2p, kube-proxy-9fq4d and csi-node pods once per node, and replicas differing only in the random suffix. Collapse healthy same-stem groups to 'stem-* x40 Running 0 restarts'; keep unhealthy or restarted rows verbatim.
  - Test: Build a synthetic 60-node pod table with 2 CrashLoopBackOff rows; fold; check the agent still finds bad pods and per-stem counts; compare tokens with ditto marks (#778).
- **#794** [R] _small_ **Fold healthy Normal event chains in kubectl events and describe** — Healthy pods emit a fixed chain: Scheduled, Pulling, Pulled, Created, Started, plus SuccessfulCreate and ScalingReplicaSet. Collapse each healthy chain to one line with image and pull time; print Warning events (BackOff, FailedScheduling, Unhealthy) verbatim. #320 covers progress states, not this.
  - Test: Capture get events and describe pod during a rollout and a CrashLoopBackOff on kind; fold; compare root-cause question accuracy and tokens versus raw.
- **#797** [R] _small_ **Fold Lambda START/END/REPORT triples and per-line RequestId** — CloudWatch Lambda logs wrap each invocation in START, END and REPORT lines sharing a 36-char RequestId, and runtime lines repeat it. Fold to one header per invocation with duration and memory; keep errors and timeouts.
  - Test: Capture aws logs tail output for 20 invocations including one timeout; fold; check the agent finds the failing request and its duration; tokenize.
- **#799** [L] _small_ **Unwrap docker json-file and CRI log envelopes, join partial lines** — Raw container log files carry a ns timestamp plus 'stdout F' per line, or a JSON wrapper with escaped newline. Strip the envelope, keep a short time, rejoin CRI 'P' partial chunks. Synthetic 20 lines: 740 vs 460 o200k tokens.
  - Test: Generate 20 CRI and json-file lines, count o200k tokens before and after; check partial-chunk rejoin round-trips on a line over 16 KB.
- **#804** ★ [R] _medium_ **Fold periodic sampler output (vmstat, iostat, ping, sar) to ranges** — vmstat, iostat -x, mpstat, sar, pidstat, ping -c N and top -b emit N near-identical numeric rows with repeated headers. Print the header once, per-column min/avg/max and outlier rows. Synthetic ceilings (o200k): 30 vmstat rows 1548 vs 42, 30 ping lines 809 vs 26.
  - Test: Run vmstat 1 30 and ping -c 30 locally; check spikes survive the fold and compare tokens against the raw output.
- **#809** ★ [R] _medium_ **Fingerprint SQL statement logs: literals to ?, IN-lists collapsed** — Postgres log_min_duration_statement lines, MySQL slow logs and ORM show_sql echoes repeat the same statement with different literals and long IN lists. Replace literals with ?, collapse IN (...), group by fingerprint with count and duration range. Ceiling: 5 lines 315 vs 27 o200k.
  - Test: Run a Django or Hibernate request loop with SQL logging on; group by fingerprint and check the slowest statement keeps its literal in the saved original.
- **#860** [R] _small_ **Fold green and skipped rows in check lists** — gh pr checks, get_check_runs and get_status list every check with name, state, elapsed and URL; large PRs show dozens of mostly green rows. Print 'N passed, M skipped' plus compact names, and keep failing, pending and cancelled rows in full.
  - Test: Capture checks for 10 PRs with 20+ checks via gh and MCP; fold; ask which checks block merge, folded versus raw, and compare tokens.
- **#888** ★ [L] _medium_ **Fold layer-index families in parameter and summary listings** — state_dict keys, named_parameters, safetensors headers, Keras summary tables and gguf-dump repeat 'layers.N.' names with identical shapes across N layers. Fold to 'layers.{0..31}.self_attn.q_proj.weight [4096,4096]' when shapes match; outliers stay listed. Synthetic 128 keys: 2560 vs 31 o200k tokens (ceiling).
  - Test: Dump parameter shapes of a small Hugging Face model and a safetensors header; fold, expand back to the exact original lines, ask the shape of layer 7's k_proj.
- **#889** [R] _small_ **Keep only best-improving trials in Optuna and Ray Tune logs** — Optuna logs 'Trial N finished with value: v and parameters: {...}. Best is trial K' per trial; Ray Tune reprints a status table. Keep best-improving trials, failures and the final best; fold the rest to counts. Synthetic 20 trials: 1340 vs 66 o200k tokens (ceiling).
  - Test: Run a 100-trial Optuna study on a toy objective plus a small Ray Tune run; fold, ask best parameters, failed trials and trial count; tokenise both forms.
- **#907** [R] _tiny_ **Drop Bash output lines that restate the command text** — tool_input already holds the command. npm run banners, make recipe echoes, set -x traces of the model's own heredoc, 'git commit' subject echoes and -v flags repeat pieces of it. Remove output lines that are substrings of the command, with a count.
  - Test: From transcripts, count output lines of 20+ characters that are substrings of the same call's command, grouped by first word; sample 30 for information loss.
- **#910** [R] _small_ **Elide outer stack frames shared with an earlier traceback** — Different exceptions in one session share framework and runner frames (pytest, Django, uvicorn, Node internals). Replace a frame run identical to an earlier trace's outer frames with 'N outer frames as before'; keep application frames and the exception line. #533 handles identical errors only.
  - Test: Collect tracebacks from 10 pytest/Django failures in one repo; compute the longest common outer frame run between consecutive distinct exceptions and the tokens it holds.
- **#976** [L] _small_ **Fold identical per-core stanzas in /proc/cpuinfo and lscpu** — cpuinfo repeats model name and a long flags line per logical CPU; only processor, core id, apicid and MHz differ. Measured: 4 cores cost 2320 tokens, 1380 in flags lines. Print one shared stanza plus per-core diff rows; cost grows linearly on 64+ core hosts.
  - Test: cat /proc/cpuinfo on this box and a many-core CI runner; fold identical key-value lines across stanzas; compare o200k tokens and confirm per-core values survive.
- **#978** ★ [R] _medium_ **Aggregate auth logs by source and user; fold PAM pairs** — sshd, auth.log, secure and lastb brute-force storms print Failed password, Invalid user and Connection closed [preauth]. Count by user class and source with first/last time, keep Accepted lines verbatim, fold pam_unix open/close, sudo and cron pairs. Synthetic 600 lines: 23400 vs about 55 tokens.
  - Test: Replay a public OpenSSH auth log sample (Loghub reportedly ships one) through the aggregator; check successful logins and unusual sources stay verbatim; compare reader answers on 'who got in'.
- **#982** ★ [R] _medium_ **Web fuzzer output: fold the dominant response signature** — ffuf, gobuster, feroxbuster and wfuzz print a line per probe; wildcard or soft-404 responses share status, size, words and lines. Group by signature, count dominant ones, keep rare signatures verbatim. Synthetic 2000 lines: 63995 vs 108 o200k tokens. Source-side alternative: ffuf -ac.
  - Test: Run ffuf against a local server with a wildcard route and two real paths; fold by (status,size,words,lines); verify both real hits stay verbatim and counts sum to the total.
- **#1020** ★ [R] _medium_ **Fold identical token substitutions across git diff hunks** — Renames and API swaps print the same -/+ pair in every hunk. Detect hunks whose pairs differ by one identical token substitution, emit 's/old/new/' once with file:line locations, and keep exceptions as ordinary hunks. Original diff saved.
  - Test: Rename one symbol across 20 files of a real repo; compare diff tokens with the rule-plus-locations form; check three arbitrary changed lines reconstruct exactly.
- **#1021** [R] _small_ **Fold moved blocks in git diff into pointers** — Refactors that move functions print the same lines as deletions in one place and additions in another. Detect blocks of 4+ lines identical after trimming across hunks (as git --color-moved does for display), print once, and point the other side at it.
  - Test: Make 10 extract-function and file-split refactors in a repo; compare diff tokens with and without move folding; verify added and removed line counts reconcile.
- **#1057** [M] _tiny_ **Static-embedding semantic dedupe for paraphrased log and doc lines** — Model2Vec (MinishLab) distils sentence encoders into lookup tables that reportedly embed on CPU with no transformer pass. Cluster lines by cosine to fold paraphrases that MinHash (#265) and Drain3 (#21) miss, such as retries worded differently. Unverified here.
  - Test: Verify that Model2Vec embeds on CPU without a transformer pass; embed lines of 50 saved logs; count clusters above 0.9 cosine that template mining misses; time per 1k lines.
- **#1069** ★ [R] _medium_ **Regroup monorepo runner output: turbo, nx, pnpm -r, lerna** — turbo prefixes every line with 'pkg:task: ', prints package-scope headers, and replays full logs of cache hits. Move prefixes into per-package headers and fold successful cache-hit replays to one line; failing tasks stay verbatim. Like #430, for task runners.
  - Test: Build a 6-package turbo workspace twice, once with a type error; compare second-run tokens; check the failing package's full output and exit code survive.
- **#1072** [R] _small_ **Fold pip and npm resolver backtracking to version ranges** — pip backtracking prints Collecting, Downloading and 'looking at multiple versions' per candidate release; npm ERESOLVE prints nested Found/peer trees. Fold runs on one package to 'foo: tried 1.9 to 1.0 (40 versions)'; keep the final conflict chain verbatim.
  - Test: pip install deliberately conflicting pins; npm install two packages with clashing peers; tokenise raw versus folded; confirm conflicting requirement names and versions survive.
- **#1073** [R] _small_ **Factor pytest --collect-only IDs into a file and class tree** — -q prints one full node ID per test, repeating file, class and parametrize ids; default mode prints a verbose <Function> tree. Rewrite as file header, class indent, bare names, and param ids folded to a count. Different format from #27 find/ls trees.
  - Test: Collect a 2,000-test suite with parametrized cases; rewrite; tokenise before and after; ask which tests cover module X and whether a given param case exists.
- **#1078** [L] _tiny_ **Ditto repeated function context in diff hunk headers** — git prints the enclosing function after every @@ header, so ten hunks in one big method repeat the same signature ten times. Replace a suffix identical to the previous header's with a ditto mark. Derivable line counts could go too, as a separate R variant.
  - Test: Take git diff output from 20 commits touching large functions; count tokens in repeated header suffixes; ask which function each hunk sits in, before and after.
- **#1079** [L] _tiny_ **Drop per-line plus prefix inside new-file diffs** — When the header says new file mode or --- /dev/null, every body line starts with + and adds nothing. Strip the prefix, emit one '[all lines added]' marker, keep the header. Synthetic 8-line file: 39 vs 31 o200k tokens. Deleted files stay with #329.
  - Test: Run git show on 20 commits that add files and gh pr diff on PRs with new files; tokenise with and without the prefix; ask where a given function starts.
- **#1080** [R] _small_ **Strip git patch-email constants and binary patch blocks** — format-patch and curl of .patch URLs open with 'From <sha> Mon Sep 17 00:00:00 2001' (constant date) and end with '-- ' plus the git version; 'GIT binary patch' holds base85 lines. Drop constants, stub binary literals. Synthetic binary block: 570 vs 9 o200k tokens.
  - Test: Generate patches with format-patch and git diff --binary on repos holding images; apply the rule; confirm the saved original still passes git apply --check; tokenise before and after.
- **#1151** ★ [R] _medium_ **Dedupe header-origin compiler warnings repeated per translation unit** — A warning inside a shared header prints once per .cpp that includes it; only the 'In file included from' line differs (two TUs under g++ -Wall gave identical 5-line blocks). Key on header path:line:col plus message; print once with TU count.
  - Test: Compile 30 TUs sharing one noisy header via make -j; count tokens before and after; verify one warning per distinct site remains.
- **#1155** ★ [R] _medium_ **Spring Boot logs: drop padding and PID, fold repeated banners** — Default Boot lines carry a fixed PID, '---', a 12-wide thread slot and a 40-wide logger column; every context boot repeats the ASCII banner and startup block. Synthetic 30 lines: 1709 to 839 tokens. Normalise columns, hoist PID, fold repeated startup blocks.
  - Test: Run a Spring Boot suite booting several contexts; apply the normaliser; compare tokens and check ERROR and WARN lines survive verbatim.
- **#1163** ★ [R] _medium_ **Access logs: user-agent legend and dash-field removal** — nginx/Apache combined, IIS W3C and ALB lines repeat a 40-50 token user-agent plus '- -' and '"-"' placeholders. List the few distinct agents once in a legend and drop empty fields. Synthetic 20 lines: 1554 vs 838 o200k tokens.
  - Test: Tokenise a real access log by column; build the legend; check the agent still answers top client agent and which paths returned 5xx.
- **#1166** [R] _tiny_ **Strip ephemeral client ports from log addresses** — Client addr:port in nginx error, Postgres, HAProxy, Java '/10.0.0.5:52814' and sshd ' port N' lines carries a dynamic-range port (32768+) that rarely matters. Drop it, keep listening ports. Synthetic 20 lines: 299 vs 239 tokens; skip when ports pair connections.
  - Test: Apply to sshd and nginx samples; verify listening ports survive; ask which client IP failed most; count lines where the port was needed.
- **#1241** ★ [R] _medium_ **Terraform plan count/for_each siblings: delta against first instance** — A count or for_each resource prints one near-identical block per index. Print the first block in full, then each sibling as only its differing attributes. Synthetic 3 instances: 354 vs 96 o200k tokens; gap grows with N. Text-plan cousin of #265 and #898.
  - Test: Plan a module with count=20 instances and a for_each subnet map; verify a reader can reconstruct each instance's attributes from the delta form.

## Structured data

- **#31** [L] `MCP` **JSON minify** — Verify with json.loads equality; probably the cheapest big win on pretty-printed MCP output.
- **#32** [L] **Columnar encoding for uniform object arrays** — TOON or CSV with a header; round-trip tested on GitHub, Jira, Slack lists.
- **#33** [R] **Drop null/empty/default JSON fields** — null, "", [], false.
- **#34** [R] `MCP` **Per-MCP-server key blocklists** — GitHub *_url templates, node_id, gravatar_id, _links; Jira self/avatarUrls; Slack blocks. Built from captured outputs.
- **#35** [L] **Dedupe repeated nested objects** — Same user object x50 defined once and referenced.
- **#36** [L] **Strip comments and blank lines in YAML/TOML/INI** — Config-style outputs.
- **#37** [L] **Fixed-width table padding to single separators** — ps, docker ps, kubectl get, df.
- **#38** [L] **Markdown table padding and separator rows** — Reduce to minimal form.
- **#39** [R] **HTML cleanup** — Drop script/style/svg/nav/footer and class/style/data-* attributes; compare HTML-to-Markdown vs trafilatura/readability.
- **#40** [R] **XML, JUnit and coverage reports** — Strip namespaces, collapse attributes, keep failures and uncovered lines.
- **#41** [R] **Elide blobs** — base64, data: URIs, minified bundles, source maps become '[base64 48KB, sha256:ab12]'.
- **#42** [R] **Elide signed-URL tails and tracking params** — X-Amz-*, token=, sig=, utm_*, fbclid.
- **#43** [R] **Slim curl -v/-i output** — Keep status line, content-type, location; drop '* Trying', TLS and header noise.
- **#44** [R] **Round float noise and ns timestamps** — Metrics-style outputs only.
- **#45** [R] **Cap per-line length** — ~300 chars plus '...[+N chars]'; one minified-JS grep hit can be 50 KB.
- **#120** ★ [R] _medium_ **Per-tool key pruning for CLI JSON reporters** — eslint -f json embeds each flagged file's full source (seen in eslint 9) plus nodeType, messageId, usedDeprecatedRules; jest --json reportedly repeats fullName; webpack --json reportedly lists reasons and issuerPath per module. Blocklist these keys per tool.
  - Test: Run eslint, jest, webpack and npm audit with JSON output on a mid-size repo; rank keys by total chars; blocklist the top ones; check agents still act correctly.
- **#122** ★ [R] _medium_ **Extract plaintext traceback from framework debug HTML pages** — curl against a dev server returns Django's debug 500 page: 80,349 chars here for a one-line KeyError, while its traceback_area textarea holds 1,703. Werkzeug and Rails error pages reportedly embed plaintext copies too. Extract that, drop the rest.
  - Test: curl broken routes on Django, Flask and Rails dev servers; compare page vs extracted traceback tokens; also test injecting an Accept: text/plain header via PreToolUse.
- **#128** [L] _small_ `MCP` **Normalise JSON string escapes and inline nested JSON strings** — Decode redundant \u003c, \u0026, \/ and \r\n escapes inside string values. When a string value is itself JSON (MCP text wrappers, webhook payloads), inline it as structure to drop the \" escape layer. Verify by parse equality.
  - Test: Scan captured MCP JSON for \uXXXX, \r\n and string values that parse as JSON; compare token counts before and after; assert parsed values equal.
- **#129** [L] _small_ **Minify pretty-printed JSON blocks embedded in mixed output** — Find balanced {...} or [...] blocks starting at line start inside logs, aws CLI, curl -v or debug prints. Validate with json.loads and minify only that block, leaving surrounding text alone. Whole-output JSON minify misses these.
  - Test: Over a replay corpus, count indent whitespace bytes inside valid JSON blocks of outputs that are not pure JSON; tokenise before and after.
