- **#1264** [L] _small_ **Narrowing searches as path:line keys when hits already shown** — When a refined grep, rg or test filter returns lines all present in an earlier result, emit only the path:line keys ('subset of earlier hits') and drop the text, which is already in context. Concrete form of #63.
  - Test: Replay transcripts for consecutive same-family commands where line set B is a subset of A; compare B's tokens with the key-only form.
- **#1265** [L] _small_ **Seed read-shadow from Write and Bash heredoc contents** — Content the model writes via Write, cat > f <<EOF, tee or printf is already in context as tool_use input. Record it as the shadow so a verification Read of the unchanged file becomes 'unchanged since your write'. Extends #144 beyond Edit pairs.
  - Test: Count Reads of a path within five turns of a Write or heredoc to it where the returned text equals the written content.
- **#1317** [R] _tiny_ `MCP` **Stub repeated WebFetch of same URL and prompt** — context-mode caches fetched pages 24 h and answers a repeat with a ~0.3 KB hint, not 48 KB+. Keep a session ledger keyed on URL plus prompt for WebFetch and fetch-style MCP tools; a repeat returns a pointer to the earlier result.
  - Test: Count duplicate (URL, prompt) and duplicate-URL fetches per session in transcripts; sum their result tokens as the ceiling.
- **#1322** ★ [L] _medium_ **PreToolUse denial of unchanged Read using line-range ledger** — qdf-hook reportedly denies a Read whose mtime and size are unchanged, so nothing enters context, and lets windowed reads bypass its cache. Variant: deny only when requested lines fall inside ranges already returned. Concrete form of #60.
  - Test: Replay Read calls from transcripts: count re-reads with unchanged mtime and range subset; verify that Edit still passes its read-first check after a denied Read.
- **#1324** ★ [R] _medium_ `busts-cache` **Rank aged tool results by relevance to current prompt** — A history-rewriting proxy needs a rule for which old results to stub. Score each by BM25 overlap with the latest prompt plus recent assistant text, not by age alone, as the cloud.reduce Reducer reportedly does with prior turns.
  - Test: On transcripts, label each aged result reused or not by later identifier overlap; compare AUC of age versus BM25-to-latest-prompt as reuse predictors.

## Hook overhead & coverage

- **#65** [L] **Shorten the note** — Cut ~80 tokens to ~15, e.g. 'ctxprune 12,430->6,100 chars; full: <id>'; check Claude still uses it correctly.
- **#66** [L] **Full note once per session** — Terse tag afterwards.
- **#67** [L] **Shorter saved-path or id** — The 16-hex path is token-heavy; try a fixed .ctxprune/ relative path or 'ctxprune show <id>'.
- **#68** [L] **Net-savings gate** — Skip when estimated saved tokens are under ~200.
- **#69** [X] `eval` **Threshold sweep** — 1.5k, 2k, 3k and 6k chars, reporting net tokens and latency; can likely drop a lot when the server is running.
- **#71** [L] **Skip-list by file type, not command name** — cat build.log, tail, and git log without -p are safe; cat src/*.py is not.
- **#73** [X] `eval` **Server cache hit rate** — In real sessions, how often is identical output repeated?
- **#74** [X] `eval` **Fail-open audit** — Log why the hook declines (timeout, port clash, after>=0.95*before) and count the missed savings.
- **#75** [R] **Don't-touch gate** — Detect source code, non-English text and number-dense output; pass through with no note.
- **#194** [M] _small_ **Pipeline stages like '| head' wrongly trigger verbatim skip** — VERBATIM_CMD matches cat/head/nl/sed -n after any pipe, so 'pytest | head -200', 'kubectl get | head' and 'docker logs | head' are never compressed (checked with the regex). Restrict the skip to a stage with no upstream pipe.
  - Test: Replay captured Bash events; count outputs over 6000 chars skipped only by a piped head/cat/nl/sed match, then compress them and compare tokens.
- **#196** [M] _small_ `MCP` **Apply threshold to summed slots, not each slot** — run() requires each stdout/stderr string and each MCP text block to reach 6000 chars alone. A 5,900+5,900 stdout/stderr pair, or many 3k-char MCP blocks, pass untouched. Gate on the total and compress the blocks jointly.
  - Test: From captured events count outputs whose total is at least 6000 chars but no single slot is; compress them and measure token saving.
- **#201** [X] `eval, MCP` **Log MCP response shapes the hook silently ignores** — _text_slots handles only type=text blocks. Embedded resource blocks, structuredContent and string fields elsewhere pass through, and no-slot declines are never logged. Capture real tool_response shapes per MCP server and size each block type.
  - Test: Dump tool_response from a PostToolUse hook for each connected MCP server; tabulate block types and chars; flag large uncompressed ones.
- **#202** [R] _small_ **Per-tool policy keyed on arguments, not just name** — VERBATIM_MCP matches only tool names. Reportedly mcp__github__pull_request_read with method get_diff returns code diffs that get pruned, while get_job_logs is an ideal log target. Key policy on (tool, args): skip, gentler or harder rate.
  - Test: List MCP calls in transcripts by tool and method with output size; hand-assign skip or rate per pair; compare QA on those pairs.
- **#205** [L] _tiny_ **Append note inside output instead of additionalContext** — The hook sends both updatedToolOutput and a separate additionalContext, which Claude Code reportedly wraps in a hook-context system message on every call. A one-line trailer inside the output avoids wrapper tokens and a second block.
  - Test: Verify that additionalContext adds wrapper tokens: count tokens of one transcript with the note in additionalContext versus as a trailer; check Claude still finds the path.
- **#208** [X] _small_ **Slice retrieval command for saved originals** — ctxprune show <id> --grep PAT -C3 or --lines a:b returns only the needed slice, not the whole file. Prior art: headroom_retrieve in headroom's CCR and ctx_search in context-mode. Cheap recovery makes harsher compression safe.
  - Test: Replay sessions where the saved original was cat'd or grepped; compute tokens a grep/line slice would have returned versus the full cat.
- **#278** [L] _tiny_ `MCP` **Lazy-import fast path for sub-threshold hook calls** — ctxprune/__init__ imports compress eagerly. Here a no-op Bash event costs 56 ms versus 10 ms bare Python (python -X importtime shows ctxprune.hook ~37 ms). Defer imports until a slot passes the size check; the tax is paid on every Bash and MCP call.
  - Test: Time 50 runs of 'python -m ctxprune hook claude-code' on a small event before and after; assert identical stdout and a drop of tens of ms.
- **#279** [R] _small_ **Model-price-keyed threshold: lower min_chars on expensive models** — min_chars is a constant 6000 though break-even depends on the per-token price of the running model. Read the model from the last assistant record in transcript_path; pricier models get a lower threshold, cheaper ones a higher one.
  - Test: Check transcript assistant records carry a model field; sweep min_chars 2k-12k per model on recorded sessions with the cost model; report net dollars and latency.
- **#291** ★ [M] _medium_ **Proxy rewrites newest tool_results the hook cannot reach, memoized** — Built-in Read, Grep, Glob, is_error results and inline shell injections reportedly bypass the PostToolUse rewrite. A proxy compresses the newest tool_result at first appearance, after every cache breakpoint, and memoizes by tool_use_id so later requests repeat identical bytes.
  - Test: Log which tool_result types exceed 6,000 chars without carrying the hook note; apply ctxprune in a replay proxy to those; assert byte-identical prefixes on the next turn.
- **#300** [X] _tiny_ **Replace 0.95 bail-out with a fidelity-per-token ratio floor** — The hook accepts any result under 0.95 of input characters. A 7% cut still pays the note and deletes words, so quality risk per saved token peaks there. Gate on a tighter ratio (about 0.75) or escalate rate instead.
  - Test: Replay outputs; plot achieved ratio against reader QA loss and net tokens after the note; pick the knee and gate below it.
- **#321** [L] **Hook note names the CLI's own projection flag** — When a compressed output came from aws describe-/list-, az list, gcloud list or kubectl -o json, the note adds one tailored hint: --query, --format='value(...)' or -o custom-columns. Agents can shrink later calls at the source, without a model.
  - Test: Replay recorded cloud CLI calls; count reruns of the same call with larger output; A/B note text with the hint over five tasks, comparing repeat-call size.
- **#399** [R] _small_ **Shrink Edit, Write and MultiEdit success echoes** — Edit reportedly returns a confirmation plus a cat -n snippet of the edited region, and Write a confirmation; the model just authored that text. PostToolUse updatedToolOutput could return 'ok, lines N-M changed'. terse (qdf-hook) reportedly suppresses write echoes too.
  - Test: Verify that updatedToolOutput is honored for Edit, Write and MultiEdit; tally their result chars across local transcripts; count later Reads of the same region after shrinking.
- **#454** [M] _tiny_ **Ship a pre-optimised ORT graph to cut session creation** — The repo's ~2 s cold start is likely InferenceSession building (graph fusion plus a 143 MB read), since onnxruntime import is ~100 ms here. Save the optimised graph (optimized_model_filepath, or .ort) at install and load it with optimisation off.
  - Test: Time phases (import, tokenizer, session creation) cold and warm; compare stock, ORT_DISABLE_ALL on the saved graph, and .ort; assert keep-decisions agree on 200 windows within int8 noise.
- **#459** [L] _tiny_ **Self-spawn a detached serve on first large output** — The first over-threshold call pays the model load, so let it also fork a detached server (lock-file single-flight, port probe) that exits after N idle minutes. Sessions with no large outputs pay nothing; parallel first calls stop loading 143 MB each.
  - Test: Fire 4 parallel large events with no server running; count model loads and peak RSS, then check later calls hit the daemon and it exits after the idle timeout.
- **#482** [R] _small_ **Apply JSON rules to get_file_contents directory listings** — VERBATIM_MCP matches get_file|file_contents, so a GitHub directory listing is exempt like source text. Its entries reportedly carry url, git_url, html_url, download_url and _links per file. Detect the listing shape (array of {type: file|dir}) and keep name, type, size, path.
  - Test: Call get_file_contents on 5 repo directories; check the hook skips them; apply the field strip and compare tokens; confirm file reads of real files still pass verbatim.
- **#490** [X] _tiny_ **Defensive env parsing: clamp rate, accept 6k and 50%** — CTXPRUNE_MIN_CHARS=6k makes int() raise and the outer except disables every compression, silently. CTXPRUNE_RATE is unvalidated (50 keeps everything, 0 keeps only protected atoms), unlike the CLI's (0,1] check. Parse each var alone, clamp, fall back to defaults, warn once.
  - Test: Feed bad values (6k, 6,000, 50, 50%, 0, -1, abc) through the hook on an 8k-char event; assert sane config or default plus one visible warning.
- **#491** [R] _small_ **Per-call config file and named profiles instead of env-only** — Env vars are frozen when Claude Code starts, so rate and threshold cannot change mid-session or per repo. Read ~/.config/ctxprune.toml plus repo .ctxprune.toml on each call; ship lean, balanced and safe profiles, with measured savings and QA loss printed at install.
  - Test: Edit the file between two calls in one headless session and confirm the second call uses the new rate; time the extra file read.
- **#492** [L] _small_ **Lossless-only tier from 1k chars: no note, no saved file** — run() gates everything at 6000 chars and rejects results above 0.95, which suits lossy deletion. Byte-exact transforms (ANSI, CR, blank runs, duplicate lines) risk nothing, so apply them from about 1,000 chars, accept any gain, and omit the roughly 80-token note and disk write.
  - Test: From transcripts, bucket Bash/MCP results of 1k-6k chars; run the lossless rules; sum tokens saved with zero note cost; compare against current gating on the same bucket.
- **#540** [R] _tiny_ **Shrink Edit not-found errors that echo old_string** — Edit failures reportedly echo the full old_string the model wrote one turn earlier. Return 'not found: N lines, first line X' plus the nearest matching line in the file. #399 covers success echoes only.
  - Test: Verify that PostToolUseFailure or PostToolUse can rewrite Edit error text. Count Edit error results in transcripts and compare their size to old_string.
- **#590** [X] **Unwritable cache directory silently disables all compression** — run() saves the original before returning. A read-only HOME, sandbox or full disk raises, the outer except swallows it, and every output passes uncompressed with no signal. Fall back to TMPDIR or a project directory, and log once.
  - Test: Run the hook with XDG_CACHE_HOME pointing at a read-only path; confirm empty output, then verify a fallback directory yields a note and compression.
- **#622** [R] _small_ **Gate compression on estimated tokens, not 6,000 characters** — Chars per token ranges from about 0.5 (braille) to 80+ (dash runs). A char-class weighted estimate calibrated on tiktoken would fire on 6k chars of braille or escaped CJK JSON and skip 6k of rule lines.
  - Test: Fit weights on 500 saved outputs against count_tokens; compare gate misclassification of chars versus estimate near the boundary; measure added hook latency.
- **#626** [R] _small_ **Shared size budget across parallel sibling tool results** — Eight parallel results of 5,900 chars each all slip under the 6,000 per-call threshold. Count siblings from the assistant message at transcript_path and divide one batch budget across them, lowering min_chars only for wide batches. Complements #196, which sums slots within one call.
  - Test: Scan transcripts for assistant messages with 3+ tool calls where every result is under 6,000 chars but the sum exceeds 20,000; report their share of all result chars.
- **#662** [X] _small_ `eval` **Skip-reason ledger priced in tokens left on the table** — Every hook exit logs exactly one reason code (below threshold, verbatim regex, ratio over 0.95, server error, opt-out, unrecognised shape) with estimated tokens. Pricing the mass per reason shows which gate costs the most money to keep.
  - Test: Add a reason enum to the debug log; replay 200 recorded events; verify one reason per uncompressed event; rank reasons by token mass.
- **#675** [X] _tiny_ **Serve client obeys HTTP_PROXY and silently loads model** — urlopen in hook._compress honors http_proxy. Verified: with http_proxy set and no no_proxy, a running local server was ignored and the in-process Compressor path was taken. Proxied hosts pay about 2 s and a model copy per call. Fix: ProxyHandler({}).
  - Test: Set http_proxy to a dead port, start a stub server, call hook._compress with CTXPRUNE_SERVER set; assert Compressor is never built. Add to test_hook.py.
- **#677** [X] _tiny_ `MCP` **MCP verbatim name regex errs in both directions** — VERBATIM_MCP is an unanchored substring match: get_file_history, get_files_changed and get_file_metadata are never compressed (lost savings), while view_file, open_file, cat and serena find_symbol (source bodies) are compressed. Verified with the regex.
  - Test: Run the regex over tool names from installed MCP servers and transcripts; hand-label which return file bodies; report false-skip and false-compress counts.
- **#756** [L] _tiny_ **Print saved-original path POSIX-style and shell-quoted in the note** — The note prints str(path) bare. With a space in HOME, or backslashes on Windows (Claude Code reportedly runs Bash through Git Bash there), an agent's grep splits or mangles the path, finds nothing, and reruns the command. Emit as_posix() with quoting.
  - Test: Unit test with HOME='/tmp/a b' and a Windows-style path: run a grep on the noted path via bash -c; assert it finds the file.
- **#772** [R] `MCP` **In-band Bash comment directive: agent sets rate and keep patterns** — hook.py already honours a CTXPRUNE_OFF=1 substring in the command. Extend it to a trailing '# ctxprune rate=0.2 keep=FAIL|panic' comment. Uses the existing Bash tool, no new MCP tool (#191); needs one CLAUDE.md line.
  - Test: Parse directives in hook.run; on 20 recorded noisy commands compare needle recall and tokens with plain 0.5; run 10 agent tasks and count how often the agent adds one.
- **#776** [X] **Coverage receipt in the note to cut needless refetches** — The note says only 'IDs, numbers and paths are kept'. Add a deterministic receipt such as 'all 9 lines matching error|fail|warn kept; 3 identical blocks folded' (about 15 tokens). It may reduce greps of the saved file, or add tokens for nothing.
  - Test: Two note variants on 20 replayed tasks: count saved-file greps and total tokens including note; keep the receipt only if the refetch rate drops by more than its cost.
- **#834** [L] _tiny_ **Skip model load when protected share forces bail-out** — With force_protected, output cannot fall below the protected-atom char share, and the hook rejects ratios of 0.95 or more. Compute the share via atomize and is_protected before Compressor() loads; return early if max(rate, share) reaches 0.97. Same result, saves a ~2 s load.
  - Test: Replay 500 recorded hook events; assert every predicted skip was an actual bail (zero false skips); report calls skipped and seconds saved, on env, ls -lR, seq and numeric tables.
- **#838** [L] _tiny_ **Load tokenizer and ORT session in parallel threads** — compress.py says tokenizer.json loads in ~1 s, about half the ~2 s cold start AGENTS.md quotes, and the session build is independent. Create InferenceSession on a worker thread while the main thread parses the tokenizer, if both release the GIL. Output unchanged.
  - Test: Time cold Compressor() sequential versus threaded over 10 runs with a warm page cache; check the GIL is released by comparing wall time to the sum of the two phases.
- **#839** [L] _tiny_ **Serve window scheduler: shortest request first, shared batches** — serve holds one global lock per request, so a 1 MB log makes 7 KB requests from sibling sessions or subagents wait until their hooks time out. One scorer thread could draw windows from all queued requests, smallest first, filling shared ORT batches.
  - Test: Send one 1 MB and ten 7 KB requests concurrently to serve; compare p50 and p95 latency of the small ones and total throughput against the current lock.
- **#841** [X] _tiny_ **Bound peak memory with line-aligned input segments** — Measured here with synthetic offsets: Python glue alone took 4.3 s and 741 MB peak RSS on a 4.4 MB log before any model call; serve accepts 32 MB. Score ~256 KB line-aligned segments, keeping only scores, so one huge output cannot OOM the daemon.
  - Test: Compare maxrss and output on 1, 4 and 16 MB real logs against the current path; assert identical keep-sets where windows align and bounded difference elsewhere.
- **#842** [L] _tiny_ **Resolve model paths from an install-time manifest** — _hub_file imports huggingface_hub on every cold start just to find two cached files. At install, write absolute paths plus sizes to a small JSON; the hook checks it and imports the hub only when files are missing. Import cost is unmeasured here.
  - Test: Run python -X importtime on the cold path with and without the manifest; compare total Compressor() time over 10 cold runs and confirm a deleted file falls back to hf_hub_download.
- **#869** [L] _tiny_ **Latency circuit breaker: skip the model path after slow calls** — Hooks run synchronously, so on a throttled laptop or busy CI box every large output stalls the agent. Time each model-path call; past N seconds, write a backoff stamp and pass outputs through (or lossless rules only) for ten minutes, then retry.
  - Test: Inject a sleep into Compressor.compress, fire five large events, assert calls after the first slow one skip the model; assert the stamp expires and a fast call restores compression.
- **#870** [L] _tiny_ **Prompt-keyword raw mode: 'verbatim' in newest prompt bypasses hook** — The hook can read transcript_path and find the user's newest prompt. If it says 'verbatim', 'exact output' or 'full log', pass outputs through for that turn. Users get a per-turn escape hatch without editing env or settings, so they keep the hook installed.
  - Test: Scripted claude -p runs with and without the keyword over one 8k-char command; assert passthrough only with it; time the transcript tail read over 1,000 events.
- **#904** [R] _small_ **Recovery view prints only deleted spans with word anchors** — Reading a saved original re-pays the roughly half of words already in context. Save the keep mask; a recall mode prints each deleted span between two kept anchor words, so context plus complement reconstructs the original. Differs from #562 tier re-render.
  - Test: On 20 compressed outputs, tokenise the full original versus the anchored deleted-span view; check re-merging with the emitted text reproduces the original byte for byte.
- **#948** [X] `eval` **Per-host matrix: can a hook rewrite, only observe, or neither?** — Cursor, Windsurf, Gemini CLI and Copilot CLI reportedly ship hooks of differing schema, some observe-only. Probe each with a stub hook returning a canary; where only observation works, run ctxprune in shadow mode to forecast savings before building a proxy.
  - Test: For each host, register a hook that tries to replace an 8k-char shell result with CANARY; check the model-visible transcript; record schema, matcher support and timeout limits in a table.
- **#959** [L] _small_ **Add built-in Grep and Glob results to the hook** — MATCHER is Bash|mcp__.* and the docstring says Grep/Glob are never matched, yet their output is not an Edit anchor (Edit requires a Read). Group Grep hits by file and hoist Glob directory prefixes losslessly, no model. Cf. #291.
  - Test: Verify that updatedToolOutput replaces Grep and Glob results with a canary; then replay 100 recorded results through the grouper and count tokens saved with a Claude tokenizer.
- **#1032** [X] **Append token-size tags to results so agents self-throttle** — Add '[~4.2k tokens]' to each tool result over 1k tokens via additionalContext. Seeing the price of a command may make agents add head, grep or narrower paths next time. Costs about 8 tokens a call; behavioural, so uncertain.
  - Test: Run 12 tasks in mini-swe-agent with and without tags; compare mean tool-output tokens per call, total billed tokens and pass rate, tags counted.
- **#1058** [R] _small_ **Pick noise packs by output signature, not command text** — Headroom reportedly routes by detected content type. npm test, make check, tox or uv run hide the real runner from command regexes. Match the first 20 lines against a signature table (pytest header, jest PASS/FAIL, cargo Compiling) to select the pack.
  - Test: On 200 recorded wrapper-command outputs, compare pack-selection accuracy of command regex, first-line signatures and Google Magika; report wrong-pack rate and milliseconds per call.
- **#1120** [R] _tiny_ **Never prune plan and dry-run outputs that authorise an action** — terraform plan, kubectl diff, helm diff, git clean -n and rsync -n print what the next command will destroy or replace. Lowercase 'destroy', 'replace' and 'forces' are unprotected, so deletable. Skip these by command signature, or run lossless rules only.
  - Test: Compress 10 terraform plan and kubectl diff outputs at 0.5; count dropped destroy, replace and delete words; weigh against the tokens lost by skipping.
- **#1124** [R] _tiny_ **Identical command rerun passes through uncompressed once** — A repeat of the same command soon after a compressed result signals the agent distrusts it. Keep a small stamp keyed by session_id and command hash; return the full output once. Per-call and stateful, unlike family-level breakers (#270).
  - Test: In transcripts, count identical Bash reruns within five turns of a compressed result and the tokens their second compression wasted; with the stamp, confirm the rerun is verbatim.
- **#1195** [L] **Memory-mapped ORT external-data weights shared across hook processes** — Parallel tool calls spawn parallel hook processes, each reading 143 MB into private memory. Saving initializers as ONNX external data lets ORT reportedly memory-map them, sharing page cache and cutting load time and RSS with the graph unchanged. Differs from #456.
  - Test: Verify that ORT memory-maps external data: export it, start 4 concurrent Compressors, compare summed PSS and session-creation time against the single-file model; assert identical logits.
- **#1198** [L] _tiny_ **Cancel abandoned serve work when the hook client disconnects** — After a hook timeout or kill, serve keeps scoring the abandoned request under its global lock, so later small requests queue behind it. Poll the client socket between ORT batches and drop the remaining windows. Differs from #839's scheduling.
  - Test: Send a 2 MB request, close the socket after 1 s, then a small one; compare small-request latency and CPU use with and without cancellation polling.
- **#1199** [L] **Derive ORT threads and memory guard from cgroup limits** — compress.py leaves intra-op threads at ORT defaults. In containers and cloud sandboxes the visible core count can exceed the cpu.max quota or affinity mask, risking oversubscription, and a low memory.max could kill the load silently. Read sched_getaffinity, cpu.max, memory.max first.
  - Test: In docker --cpus=2 --memory=1g on a larger host, time 100 windows with default versus derived thread counts; record whether session creation gets OOM-killed.
- **#1219** [R] _small_ **Compress BashOutput background-shell polls via id-to-command map** — run() returns for any tool but Bash, so polls of background shells (dev servers, watchers, long tests) pass raw. Record shell id to command at the originating Bash call, then apply the verbatim skip and compression to polls. Tool name reportedly varies by version.
  - Test: Verify that polls reach PostToolUse: start a build with run_in_background, poll it, dump the event (tool_name, response shape, missing command); compress a 20k-char poll and diff tokens.
- **#1310** [M] `MCP` **Compress embedded resource and structuredContent-only MCP results** — _text_slots matches only type=text blocks, so payloads carried as resource.text, or as structuredContent beside an empty text block, bypass compression entirely. Add those shapes as slots, saving the originals once. #201 only logs them.
  - Test: Replay captured results from fetch, filesystem and GitHub MCP servers; total characters in non-text shapes above 6,000, then run the extended hook and compare sizes.
- **#1311** [R] _tiny_ `MCP` **Revert individual slots that barely compress despite passing total** — run() applies the 0.95 gate to summed characters, so a stderr or MCP block at ratio 0.98 still gets its lossy version next to a well-compressed stdout. Restore any slot whose own ratio exceeds about 0.9 to its original.
  - Test: Replay recorded multi-slot events; count slots above ratio 0.9 and words deleted from them, then run reader QA on those slots with and without the revert.
- **#1314** [L] _tiny_ `MCP` **Merge many MCP text blocks into one in updatedToolOutput** — Search-style MCP servers return one text block per hit. Joining blocks with a newline may drop per-block framing tokens and lets one compression pass see cross-hit context. Hook-level counterpart of proxy idea #1035; probe the framing cost first.
  - Test: Use count_tokens on a tool_result with one block versus the same text split into 40 blocks; if the delta is zero, drop the idea.
- **#1354** [R] **Budget feedback hook: tool-call counter and one-shot token nudge** — Budget-Aware Tool-Use (Google, 2025) reportedly finds that showing calls used and remaining makes agents stop earlier or pick cheaper tools. A PostToolUse additionalContext line ('calls 7/30') does this in headless runs; a per-prompt meter can add one nudge past a token threshold. Differs from #1032.
  - Test: In 15 headless claude -p tasks, compare no line, a calls counter, and a one-shot nudge at 300k tokens: tool calls, billed tokens and pass rate, counting the added tokens.
- **#1377** [X] _tiny_ **Yield-per-millisecond scheduler for the deterministic pre-pass rules** — With hundreds of lossless rules, run time adds up on every large output. Profile bytes saved per millisecond per rule, run in that order under a fixed budget, and gate each rule behind a cheap sniff so non-matching ones cost near zero.
  - Test: Time each rule on a 200-output corpus with perf_counter; compute savings per ms; simulate a 30 ms budget in yield order versus run-all and report lost savings.
- **#1378** [L] _tiny_ **Learned bail-out predictor gates the model path from ledger** — Outputs whose ratio lands at 0.95 or above are rejected after seconds of model work. Log the ratio with cheap features (command family, zlib ratio, unique-line share, char classes); a small tree predicts bail-outs and skips the model, going beyond protected share (#834).
  - Test: Record ratio plus features for 500 hook runs; fit a depth-3 tree; report false-skip rate (predicted bail-out but model would cut over 10 percent) against seconds saved.
- **#1398** [R] _small_ **Gate on output shape for diffs and numbered source** — In a hand-made sample of 26 agent-style commands, VERBATIM_CMD skipped 6, yet 'git -C x diff', 'diff -u', 'tail', 'rg -C' and 'grep -A' still reach compression. Gate on output shape instead: diff hunk headers, path:line: runs. Complements regex fixes #292 and #676.
  - Test: Replay recorded Bash outputs; count those with 3+ '@@ -a,b +c,d @@' headers or mostly 'path:NN:' lines that the command regex let through to compression.

## Fix it at the source (PreToolUse)

- **#76** [L] `conditional` **Inject quiet defaults** — NO_COLOR=1, CI=1, TERM=dumb, GIT_PAGER=cat, --no-progress, pip -q, npm --no-fund --no-audit, --color=never; check that updatedInput works for Bash.
- **#78** [R] `conditional` **Rewrite grep -r to rg --heading -M 200** — Group by file and cap line width.
- **#79** [R] **Head, tail and path for giant outputs** — Compare with model compression on task success.
- **#143** [L] _small_ **PreToolUse byte-offset cursor for repeated reads of growing logs** — When the model re-runs cat, tail -n N or less on a build.log it already saw, verify the earlier prefix hash and rewrite the command via updatedInput to start at the stored offset, with a header naming the skipped bytes.
  - Test: Verify that Claude Code honours updatedInput for Bash; simulate an agent polling a growing log 10 times; count bytes saved versus tail -n 200 each time.
- **#203** [R] _medium_ `conditional, MCP` **PreToolUse injects minimal_output and small perPage into MCP calls** — The github MCP server's own instructions advise minimal_output=true and batches of 5-10. A PreToolUse updatedInput can add them to list_* and read calls, shrinking JSON at the source; the Bash-only quiet-defaults item does not cover this target.
  - Test: Verify that updatedInput works for MCP tools; call list_issues, list_commits, list_pull_requests with and without minimal_output; compare response tokens and fields agents later needed.
- **#217** [L] _small_ **Build-tool quiet pack: MAVEN_ARGS, Gradle console, DOTNET_NOLOGO, CARGO_TERM** — Per-ecosystem settings that cut chatter at the source: Maven -ntp -B (reportedly settable via MAVEN_ARGS in Maven 3.9+), org.gradle.console=plain, DOTNET_NOLOGO plus telemetry opt-out, CARGO_TERM_PROGRESS_WHEN=never. Extends the generic NO_COLOR/CI list.
  - Test: Verify that MAVEN_ARGS is honored; run build and test on sample Maven, Gradle, .NET, Cargo and Go projects with and without the pack; compare bytes, exit codes, failure text.
- **#227** [L] _small_ **Decode git octal-escaped non-ASCII paths (core.quotepath)** — Verified here: git status quotes non-ASCII names as octal escapes by default (an accented e becomes 8 chars). Escapes cost several tokens per byte (34 vs 8 on the o200k proxy). Inject git -c core.quotepath=false via PreToolUse, or decode octal runs forming valid UTF-8.
  - Test: Create a repo with CJK and accented filenames; run status, ls-files, diff --stat and log --stat with and without quotepath=false; compare tokens and confirm the paths round-trip.
- **#309** [R] _small_ `conditional, MCP` **Auto-inject cheap params found in MCP input schemas** — Anthropic's tool-writing guide (Sep 2025) reportedly advises concise/detailed response_format, pagination and field selection. Scan each tool's inputSchema for such params (response_format, detail_level, fields, limit, minimal_output) and set the cheapest when unset; generalizes github-only item 203.
  - Test: Dump tools/list from installed MCP servers and count tools with such params; run 10 calls with and without injection, noting tokens and follow-up re-calls for detail.
- **#400** [R] _medium_ `conditional` **PreToolUse caps on built-in Read and Grep calls** — Built-in Read, Grep and Glob results reportedly bypass PostToolUse rewriting (item 291), so updatedInput is the lever: add head_limit to Grep content mode, add limit to Read of unsized large files.
  - Test: Verify that updatedInput is honored for built-in Read and Grep; replay transcript calls: share lacking limits, and whether later Edit old_strings fall inside the first 400 lines.
- **#477** [R] _medium_ `conditional, MCP` **Steer Actions log fetches to failed-only and tailed** — Rewrite gh run view --log to --log-failed; add failed_only or tail_lines to GitHub MCP get_job_logs calls (parameters reportedly exist). Green-job logs are never fetched, so the hook skips multi-megabyte inputs and nothing dilutes the failing step.
  - Test: Verify that get_job_logs accepts failed_only; on 10 failed runs compare bytes of --log and --log-failed; check the root-cause line survives and count agent requests for the full log.
- **#486** [L] _small_ **Inject git quiet config through GIT_CONFIG_COUNT env vars** — Git 2.31+ reads config from env without touching ~/.gitconfig. Setting advice.statusHints=false, status.short and status.branch took a two-file git status from 349 to 20 characters here. Such outputs sit far below the 6,000-char threshold, so only source-side config helps.
  - Test: Set the vars in Claude Code settings env; run status, checkout, rejected push, merge conflict, detached HEAD; compare bytes; check whether agents misread short status codes.
- **#497** [R] _medium_ `conditional` **Send Accept: text/markdown on curl of docs pages** — Cloudflare's Markdown for Agents converts HTML at the origin when asked via Accept: text/markdown, and sends x-markdown-tokens; its blog example went 16,180 to 3,150 tokens (Cloudflare's figure). PreToolUse adds the header to curl/wget of docs URLs; fall back to .md or llms.txt.
  - Test: Verify that sites honor the header: curl 20 docs pages with and without it; compare o200k tokens and x-markdown-tokens; check code blocks and tables survive.
- **#499** [R] _medium_ `conditional` **Inject fail-fast limits into test runner commands** — pytest --maxfail=N, jest --bail and go test -failfast stop at the first failures, so a broken suite prints 3 failures, not 300. Unlike quiet flags (#76) this shortens the run itself; the note says how to rerun without the limit.
  - Test: Break 30 tests in a sample repo; run default versus fail-fast; compare output tokens, wall time, and extra turns the agent needs to fix everything.
- **#531** [R] _medium_ `conditional` **Block-until-changed rewrite for repeated status polls** — When the model re-issues the same status command (gh run view, kubectl get, sleep then tail), PreToolUse rewrites it to loop until the output hash differs from the stored one, with a timeout. One result per state change replaces N identical polls and turns.
  - Test: Verify that updatedInput rewrites Bash. Find runs of 3+ identical Bash commands in transcripts; replay one against a fake slow job with the wrapper; count turns and tokens saved.
- **#532** [R] _medium_ `conditional` **Rerun only previously failing tests; defer full run** — Ledger keeps failed test IDs. A repeated test command is rewritten via updatedInput to pytest --lf, jest --onlyFailures or go test -run names, so passing-test output is never produced. Full suite once at green. Differs from #499 (fail-fast) and #62 (output delta).
  - Test: Fixture repo, 200 tests, 3 failing: run five edit-test loops with and without the rewrite; compare output tokens, turns and regressions missed.
- **#537** [R] _small_ `conditional` **Stuck-loop breaker: deny repeated failed commands, nudge on repeats** — PreToolUse denies a Bash command identical to one that failed earlier unless an edit or install ran between, quoting the earlier error id. PostToolUse nudges on 3+ repeats of (command, output hash), repeated error signatures, A-B-A-B alternation (after OpenHands' StuckDetector). #447 covers only Edit pairs.
  - Test: Scan transcripts for identical failed Bash inputs without intervening Edit, 3+ repeated output hashes and A-B-A-B pairs; sum tokens after the third occurrence; shadow-log would-be denials for false positives.
- **#538** [R] _small_ `conditional` **Rewrite repeated log commands to native --since cursors** — On a repeated docker logs, kubectl logs, journalctl or git log, PreToolUse adds the tool's own cursor from the ledger (--since last timestamp, lastsha..HEAD). #143 rewrites byte offsets of files; these commands read streams, not files.
  - Test: Run docker logs on a looping container twice; compare tokens of the full second call versus the --since call; check boundary lines are neither lost nor duplicated.
- **#610** [L] _small_ **Ecosystem nag-silencing env vars for JS and Python tools** — Extend the quiet env (#76, #193) with PIP_DISABLE_PIP_VERSION_CHECK, NPM_CONFIG_UPDATE_NOTIFIER=false, NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED, BROWSERSLIST_IGNORE_OLD_DATA, PRISMA_HIDE_UPDATE_MESSAGE and DISABLE_OPENCOLLECTIVE. Each targets a banner reprinted every run. Verify each name exists.
  - Test: Run 10 build, install and test commands with and without the pack; diff bytes and exit codes; confirm each variable actually silences its banner.
- **#694** [R] _medium_ `conditional` **Inject compact diagnostic flags: cargo short, clang no-caret** — For build or check commands, PreToolUse appends cargo --message-format=short, clang -fno-caret-diagnostics, gcc -fno-diagnostics-show-caret or xcodebuild -quiet. Measured on a 2-error cargo check: 400 vs 184 o200k tokens. Help text is lost, so the note says how to rerun without.
  - Test: Break 20 files in Rust, C++ and Xcode samples; compare default versus compact tokens; replay fixes to check agents still resolve errors without help text.
- **#697** [R] _medium_ `conditional` **Maven: redirect surefire test stdout to files, trim stack traces** — For mvn test or verify, PreToolUse adds -Dmaven.test.redirectTestOutputToFile=true and -DtrimStackTrace=true (property names reportedly). Spring Boot banners, context logs and framework frames then land in target/surefire-reports; failure summaries reportedly stay on the console.
  - Test: Verify that failures stay visible: run mvn test on a Spring Boot sample with 10 classes and 2 failures, with and without the flags; compare console tokens and report files.
- **#711** [R] _small_ `conditional` **Steer HTML and docx converters to compact-output flags** — Converter defaults add tokens: pandoc's markdown writer wraps at 72 columns and can emit {#id .class} attributes and []{#anchor} spans; lynx -dump appends a numbered link list. For reading intent, rewrite to pandoc -t gfm --wrap=none, lynx -nolist, html2text --ignore-links.
  - Test: Run 10 docx and 10 HTML inputs through default and rewritten commands; tokenise both; diff text to confirm only layout and link lists changed; verify Bash updatedInput honors the rewrite.
- **#762** [R] _small_ `conditional` **Force vectors off on vector DB get and scroll calls** — Pinecone fetch, Qdrant scroll/retrieve with with_vectors, Weaviate include_vector and Chroma get with embeddings in include return full float arrays. PreToolUse sets the exclusion flag unless the prompt mentions embeddings. Parameter names are from memory; complements #309 and output-side elision (#133).
  - Test: Verify that the hook can rewrite the argument: start a local Qdrant or Chroma MCP server, call scroll/get with vectors on and off, and compare result tokens.
- **#788** [L] _small_ **Infra CLI nag, hint and pager silencing environment** — Extend the quiet env (#610 covers only JS and Python) to infra tools: AWS_PAGER empty, gcloud update-check and survey prompts, AZURE_CORE_ONLY_SHOW_ERRORS, TF_IN_AUTOMATION, DOCKER_CLI_HINTS=false, GH_NO_UPDATE_NOTIFIER.
  - Test: Verify that each variable exists and silences its banner; run 10 aws, gcloud, az, terraform, docker and gh commands with and without the pack; diff bytes and exit codes.
- **#789** [R] _small_ **Terraform TF_CLI_ARGS defaults: concise, compact-warnings, no-color** — Set TF_CLI_ARGS_plan and TF_CLI_ARGS_apply in the launch env so -concise (reportedly drops refresh and progress lines), -compact-warnings and -no-color apply to every call without rewriting commands. Errors and the plan summary stay in full.
  - Test: Verify that -concise exists in the installed terraform; plan and apply a 30-resource sample with and without; compare tokens; confirm errors, destroy lines and the summary survive.
- **#790** [R] _medium_ `conditional` **Steer unfiltered cloud reference-catalog calls to server-side filters** — aws ec2 describe-instance-types and describe-images, az vm image list --all, gcloud compute images list and kubectl api-resources return provider-wide catalogs unrelated to the user's infra. Deny or rewrite unfiltered forms with a hint naming --filters, --query or --publisher.
  - Test: Mine transcripts for catalog commands; measure each unfiltered output in tokens; replay with filters; check the agent's lookup (instance type, AMI id) still answers.
- **#798** [R] _small_ `conditional` **Source-side cloud CLI output format: yaml or value() over JSON** — aws, az and gcloud list and describe default to pretty JSON. For commands with no pipe, --query or --output, PreToolUse appends --output yaml or --format=value(...). Avoids braces and quotes at the source; #529 found minified JSON can beat YAML, so pick per shape.
  - Test: Capture 20 describe and list outputs per cloud; tokenize json, yaml, text, table; check nested-field questions answer equally; flag commands whose later jq pipes would break.
- **#858** ★ [R] _medium_ **Runner-side failure digest emitted as a workflow annotation** — Steps tee output to a file; a later if: failure() step runs it through ctxprune and emits the digest as a workflow-command annotation. Agents read it through the check-run annotations API instead of downloading the full log.
  - Test: In a scratch repo, fail a pytest step, emit the digest annotation, fetch it with gh api; compare tokens and root-cause coverage with gh run view --log-failed.
- **#863** [R] _small_ `conditional` **Tune GitHub log tool params: get_check_run textLimit, get_job_logs return_content** — get_check_run pages output.text in 4096-byte windows by default (max 8192), reportedly repeating metadata per page; get_job_logs returns URLs unless return_content is true, costing a turn plus an untailed curl. Inject textLimit 8192, or return_content with tail_lines. Complements #477 and #203.
  - Test: Verify that updatedInput works for MCP; fetch a 30 KB check run at textLimit 4096 and 8192, and a failed job with and without return_content; compare turns and tokens.
- **#878** [R] _small_ **Generate .ignore files so ripgrep-backed Grep and Glob skip noise** — ripgrep honours .ignore and .rgignore beyond .gitignore. List tracked-but-noisy paths (vendor, snapshots, *.min.js, lockfiles, generated code) there: searches stop returning them while direct Read still works, unlike deny rules (#401). Claude Code's Grep reportedly uses bundled ripgrep.
  - Test: Verify that Grep and Glob honour .ignore: in a repo with vendored dirs, compare result tokens before and after adding it, and confirm Read of an ignored path works.
- **#945** [R] _small_ **Git textconv drivers shrink lockfile and generated diffs** — A diff.<driver>.textconv script bound in .gitattributes drops integrity, resolved and minified-line noise before git diff, show and log -p print it, with no agent hook. Checked on a synthetic repo: show and log -p honor it; textconv output will not git apply.
  - Test: Run a real package-lock.json bump and a minified-bundle change through git diff with and without textconv; compare bytes and tokens; ask a reader which packages changed.
- **#953** [R] _small_ **Precomputed digest siblings for generated files, original ignored** — A build or post-checkout step writes small .digest files (lockfile versions, OpenAPI endpoint table) next to generated files, while ignore and deny rules hide the originals, so every agent reads the digest first. Unlike #401, deny leads somewhere useful.
  - Test: Generate digests for a repo's lockfile and an OpenAPI file; ask five dependency or endpoint questions through an agent with ignores set; compare tokens and accuracy to raw reads.
- **#1001** [R] _small_ `conditional` **Size-forecast steering: cap commands whose earlier runs were huge** — PostToolUse logs output chars per canonical command signature (#846); PreToolUse adds a limit, -n, --stat or head to the next call of any signature whose earlier outputs exceeded the threshold. Learned per repo, not a static rule.
  - Test: From transcripts, group Bash calls by signature; measure how often a signature's second call is within 2x of its first size; simulate caps and count saved chars.
- **#1053** [R] _small_ **Source-side git diff shrink: diff.context and noprefix config** — git diff, show and log -p are on the verbatim skip, so no hook trims them. Inject diff.context=1 and diff.noprefix=true via GIT_CONFIG_COUNT (as #486 does for status). Measured (o200k): 20 commits of modified files, 16,456 tokens default, 13,966 at -U1, 13,039 at -U0.
  - Test: Replay recorded git diff/show calls with and without the config; compare tokens; run 10 edit tasks and count Edit failures caused by reduced hunk context.
- **#1063** [R] _small_ `conditional` **Rewrite find, tree and ls -R to gitignore-aware listings** — PreToolUse swaps find . -name, tree and ls -R for git ls-files -co --exclude-standard, tree --gitignore or fd, as rtk's ls reportedly hides noise directories. Source-side sibling of #394, which folds after the fact.
  - Test: Count find, ls -R and tree calls and tokens in transcripts; replay with the rewrite; diff results to confirm only ignored paths vanish; log cases needing an ignored path.
- **#1070** [R] _small_ **Prefix commands with PYTHONNODEBUGRANGES and a frame-dropping preload** — The env var PYTHONNODEBUGRANGES removes traceback carets (250 to 240 tokens on a 3-frame sample). A preload via sitecustomize or NODE_OPTIONS=--require drops site-packages and node:internal frames by real path (5 of 10 lines in a Node sample).
  - Test: Verify that PreToolUse can prefix Bash env vars. Run failing python and jest commands with and without the preload; compare tokens; confirm failures and exit codes are unchanged.
- **#1088** [R] _small_ `conditional` **Cross-session failed-then-fixed command table, rewritten at PreToolUse** — Mine transcripts for a command that failed and a variant that then succeeded (pytest, then uv run pytest). Store pairs per repo; PreToolUse rewrites via updatedInput or denies with the fix. No standing context, unlike a SessionStart note (#307).
  - Test: Extract fail-then-succeed pairs within 3 turns over 30 transcripts; count cross-session recurrence and the tokens of the failed call, error and retry turn.
- **#1153** ★ [R] _medium_ **Audit inherited verbosity env defaults such as RUST_BACKTRACE=1** — This sandbox exports RUST_BACKTRACE=1: a two-failure cargo test printed 1142 o200k stdout tokens versus 210 with RUST_BACKTRACE=0 (aliasing /rustc/<hash>/ alone gave 806). Scan inherited env for such defaults (GOTRACEBACK, RUST_LOG, MAVEN_OPTS) and set quiet values for test runs.
  - Test: Run one failing cargo test with RUST_BACKTRACE 0, 1 and full; compare tokens, and replay whether an agent still fixes the bug.
- **#1157** [R] _small_ `conditional` **Steer log readers to cheapest native format and bounds** — adb logcat -v brief with -t N, a *:W filter or --pid; journalctl -o short with -p warning; dmesg -l err,warn: flags change per-line cost before output exists. Synthetic: logcat threadtime 850 vs brief 565 tokens; journalctl short-iso 600 vs short 480.
  - Test: Capture the same 200 lines in every -v and -o variant on a real device or host; tokenise; confirm timestamps survive at the needed resolution.
- **#1158** ★ [R] _medium_ **Fold simctl and xcodebuild destination lists; bound bare dumpsys** — 'xcrun simctl list' and xcodebuild destination errors reportedly print dozens of devices and unavailable runtimes; fold to counts plus matches. adb dumpsys with no service name dumps everything: inject the service name or a head cap.
  - Test: Run each on a dev machine and record sizes; check agents still find the right simulator UDID or dumpsys section after folding.
- **#1227** [L] _tiny_ **Silence shell-startup and env-manager chatter in command output** — direnv documents DIRENV_LOG_FORMAT= to silence its loading and export lines; nvm, pyenv, conda activate and rc-file banners can also print into the persistent shell. First prove they appear in results, then set variables in settings env or strip their fixed line shapes.
  - Test: Grep transcripts for lines starting 'direnv:', 'Now using node', '(base)'; run a no-op Bash call; if nonempty, set vars and compare token counts.
- **#1228** [L] _tiny_ **Pin LC_ALL and LANGUAGE so tool diagnostics print in English** — On non-English systems git, gcc, pip and apt print translated messages. A hand-translated sample git fatal line cost 16 o200k tokens in English versus 23 German, 27 Russian, 30 Japanese. Pin the locale in launch env, keeping UTF-8 so filenames survive.
  - Test: Run git, gcc, apt under LC_ALL=de_DE.UTF-8 and C.UTF-8; count tokens on real messages; verify filenames with accents still print correctly.
- **#1229** [R] _tiny_ **Docker config.json psFormat and imagesFormat as source-side column trim** — Docker's client config.json accepts psFormat and imagesFormat templates, so plain docker ps and docker images can drop COMMAND, CREATED and ID columns with no command rewriting. Names stay usable for logs and exec. Source-side, unlike post-hoc column dropping (#57).
  - Test: Set psFormat on a host with 30 containers; compare o200k tokens of docker ps -a before and after; confirm the agent still runs logs and exec by name.
- **#1239** [R] _small_ **Ansible env defaults: hide ok and skipped hosts, deprecations** — Launch env ANSIBLE_DISPLAY_OK_HOSTS=false, ANSIBLE_DISPLAY_SKIPPED_HOSTS=false, ANSIBLE_DEPRECATION_WARNINGS=false and ANSIBLE_LOCALHOST_WARNING=false drop per-host ok lines at the source; changed, failed, unreachable and PLAY RECAP stay. Source-side counterpart of #318.
  - Test: Verify that the env is honored: run one playbook over 20 containers with and without it; compare bytes and confirm changed, failed, unreachable and recap lines are identical.
- **#1300** [R] _small_ **RIPGREP_CONFIG_PATH with max-columns for every rg-backed agent** — One shared ripgrep config (--max-columns=200, --max-columns-preview, optionally --max-filesize) caps minified-bundle hits at the source for any agent that shells out to rg, hooks or not. The agent can rerun with --max-columns=0 for the full line.
  - Test: Verify that Claude Code's Grep honors the env; replay real transcript grep calls with and without the config and review which cut hits mattered. Synthetic 100 KB hit: 300 bytes.
- **#1303** ★ [R] _medium_ **git sparse-checkout worktree hides noisy trees from every agent** — Give agents a git worktree with sparse-checkout excluding vendor, generated output, fixtures and doc archives. ls, find, du, rg and IDE file trees never see them, unlike agent-specific ignore files or Claude-only deny rules. Commits still work normally.
  - Test: git worktree add --no-checkout, then sparse-checkout set --cone; run identical ls -R, find and rg exploration in full and sparse trees and compare token counts.
- **#1316** [R] _small_ `conditional, MCP` **Shrink search result caps as same tool repeats** — context-mode reportedly returns 2 results per query for calls 1-3, 1 for calls 4-8, then blocks and redirects to a batch call. PreToolUse could lower Grep or search-MCP head_limit by call index within a turn window, pushing the agent to fuse queries.
  - Test: Count runs of 4+ Grep or search-MCP calls within 10 turns in transcripts; sum tokens of the 4th-and-later results as the ceiling.
- **#1333** [R] _small_ `conditional` **PreToolUse: add --csv or -B to bare SQL client calls** — When psql -c, mysql -e, sqlite3 or duckdb -c carries no format flag, rewrite via updatedInput to psql --csv, mysql -B, sqlite3 -csv or duckdb -csv, avoiding padded grids at the source. Skip meta-commands and EXPLAIN. Differs from #412, which parses grids afterwards.
  - Test: Replay 20 saved SELECTs through psql default and --csv on a local database; compare o200k tokens; run NULL-versus-empty-string and quoted-comma questions on both outputs.

## Model-side

- **#80** [M] `eval` **Per-content-type rate sweep** — Find the accuracy-vs-rate knee for logs, JSON, prose, code and traces.
- **#81** [M] **Adaptive rate from a redundancy proxy** — zlib ratio, unique-line ratio, entropy.
- **#82** [M] **Threshold intersect budget** — Keep atoms with score >= tau, capped by the budget.
- **#83** [M] **Rank by score per token cost** — Knapsack with unit=callable and the real tokenizer.
- **#84** [M] `eval` **Use measure= in the hook** — Compare the real token ratio with the char ratio.
- **#85** [M] **Overlapping scoring windows** — Stride plus averaging scores at the seams.
- **#86** [M] **Per-window score calibration** — Check whether one window gets stripped bare by global top-k.
- **#87** [M] **Line-level selection mode** — Drop whole low-scoring lines; compare QA at equal tokens on logs and test output.
- **#88** [M] `eval` **Hybrid rules then model** — Rules (hygiene, repetition, structured, tool packs, stateful) first, then the model at a gentler rate (~0.6) vs the model alone at 0.5, at equal total tokens. The headline experiment.
- **#89** [M] **Query-aware boost** — Boost atoms overlapping the recent user prompt, the command, or the error being debugged (BM25 or embeddings).
- **#90** [M] **Head and tail bias for logs** — Keep first and last N lines verbatim; compress the middle.
- **#91** [M] **Error-line shield** — Keep lines matching error/fail/traceback/panic/assert plus one line of context; compress the rest harder.
- **#92** [M] `eval` **Protect-set audit** — is_protected keeps every token with a digit; measure how much of the kept budget it eats per source, then relax class by class (timestamps, line numbers, byte counts, durations, percentages) and re-run QA.
- **#93** [X] `eval` **Budget starvation** — How often does protected content exceed the budget and trigger the >=0.95 bail-out?
- **#94** [X] `eval` **What does the model delete at 50%?** — Count by atom class; reliably deleted classes can become free rules before inference.
- **#95** [M] `eval` **int8 vs fp32 vs distilled student** — Load time, speed and QA delta.
- **#96** [M] **Label audit and domain fine-tune** — Hand-check 200 teacher labels; collect real local hook outputs, label and fine-tune; the stall may be a train/real-world distribution gap.
- **#97** [M] `eval` **Teacher-prompt ablations** — Generic vs task-aware preservation.
- **#98** [M] **Ensemble with LLMLingua-2** — Average rank scores and check QA at equal ratio.
- **#99** [M] `eval` **Two passes at 0.7 vs one at 0.5** — Quick negative-result check.
- **#134** ★ [M] _medium_ **Run the token classifier on JSON string values only** — Parse JSON and apply the model only to long string leaves (issue bodies, descriptions, log text) at a per-leaf rate, leaving keys, numbers and brackets untouched so output stays valid JSON. Today the model sees raw JSON text.
  - Test: On 20 captured MCP JSON outputs over 6k chars, compare json.loads validity and QA accuracy of whole-text versus leaf-only compression at equal token count.
- **#162** [M] _tiny_ **Rank atoms by logit margin, not saturated probability** — The ONNX path computes a float32 softmax; in numpy a keep-vs-drop logit margin of 17 or more gives exactly 1.0, so confident atoms tie and the stable sort favors earlier positions. Rank on margin or mean logit; also try min versus mean over subtokens.
  - Test: Log fraction of atoms with p==1.0 and tie size at the cut for rates 0.33 and 0.5; then QA at equal tokens, margin versus probability ranking.
- **#163** [M] _small_ **Contiguity-aware selection: Viterbi smoothing or CRF head** — Top-k over independent atom scores leaves isolated kept words between dropped runs. Add a switch penalty or minimum kept-run length (Viterbi over scores at inference), later a CRF layer trained on teacher labels, so output keeps phrases, not fragments.
  - Test: Count isolated kept atoms (both neighbours dropped) in current output; compare QA at equal tokens with smoothed selection on logs and prose.
- **#164** [M] _small_ **Novelty-decayed scores for repeated lines (MMR-style)** — Windows are scored independently, so a line repeated 40 times, or seen in earlier calls, keeps full score each time. Multiply an atom's score by gamma per prior occurrence of its line template in the document or session history before top-k.
  - Test: On replayed logs with repeats beyond 512 tokens, count duplicate-template lines surviving at 0.5; sweep gamma 0.5 to 0.9, check QA at equal tokens.
- **#165** [M] `eval` **Inference window sweep: 512 versus 1k-4k tokens** — Compressor scores independent 512-token windows, so context never crosses a window. mmBERT-small reportedly supports 8k context via ModernBERT; try max_len 1024 to 4096 on the existing model, checking whether scores hold past the 500-token training length.
  - Test: Verify that the model accepts 4096-token inputs; score 20 long outputs at 512/1024/2048/4096; report rank correlation with 512, val ROC-AUC, latency, and QA at equal tokens.
- **#166** ★ [M] _medium_ **Command-conditioned scoring via encoder pair input** — Feed the tool command (and last user prompt) as segment A with the output chunk as segment B, labels only on B. Teacher labels see the same command, so atoms relevant to what was asked survive. Learned alternative to a post-hoc BM25 boost.
  - Test: On SWE-smith traces where the command is known, relabel ~500 chunks with a command-aware teacher, fine-tune with pair input, compare QA on command-specific questions versus the unconditioned model.
- **#167** [M] _small_ **Multi-budget ordinal targets instead of two-prompt keep/drop rows** — Today two teacher prompts give two hard-label rows per chunk. Label each chunk at 4-5 word budgets (e.g. 0.15, 0.3, 0.5, 0.8) and train on keep-level as ordinal or soft BCE targets, so score rank means 'survives down to budget b'.
  - Test: Relabel 300 chunks via teacher prompt v3 at four budgets; train an ordinal head versus the current two-prompt recipe; compare QA at 0.33 and 0.5 kept.
- **#168** [M] **Pairwise ranking loss with asymmetric false-drop cost** — Training uses default cross-entropy while inference is top-k ranking, and a wrongly dropped fact costs more than a wrongly kept filler word. Add a within-chunk pairwise or listwise AUC surrogate and weight keep-class errors higher.
  - Test: Fine-tune one epoch from the current checkpoint with the added loss on existing labels; compare within-chunk AUC and QA at 0.33 kept against the CE-only baseline.
- **#169** ★ [M] _medium_ **Behaviour-derived labels: atoms the agent later quotes or retrieves** — Cheap weak supervision: in Claude Code transcripts, atoms of a tool output that reappear verbatim in later tool inputs or assistant text are positives; greps of saved originals after compression mark regrets. Fine-tune or reweight teacher labels with these.
  - Test: Mine local ~/.claude transcripts: fraction of output atoms later quoted by class (protected, prose, punctuation); compare to model keep rate to find under-kept classes before any training.
- **#170** [M] _small_ **Model soup: average weights of v1, v2 and seed runs** — Weight-average fine-tunes sharing an init (seeds, last epochs, runs on different label sets); no inference cost. Model soups (Wortsman et al., 2022) reportedly help on small fine-tuning sets, and the label set here is small.
  - Test: Train 3 seeds, average weights, compare val ROC-AUC and QA at 0.5 versus the best single seed; then try a greedy soup including the v1-to-v2 chain.
- **#172** [M] **Concatenate handcrafted atom features into the classifier head** — The encoder sees only subwords. Add per-token features before the head: occurrences of the same atom or line template in the chunk, indentation, bracket depth, line index, is_protected flag. Cheap access to repetition and structure.
  - Test: Add a small feature vector to hidden states before the head; retrain on same data; compare per-source val ROC-AUC and QA at 0.5 versus a shuffled-features ablation.
- **#173** [M] _tiny_ **Sweep dangling punctuation left after atom deletion** — After top-k, kept punctuation atoms whose neighbours were both dropped (lone commas, orphan colons) cost tokens and carry nothing. A post-pass removes them, and brackets or quotes enclosing no kept atom, only where the interior had dropped atoms.
  - Test: Run current model over 100 saved hook outputs; count kept punctuation atoms with both neighbours dropped and their token cost; confirm QA unchanged at equal tokens after the sweep.
- **#199** [X] `eval` **Budget charges 1 char per whitespace gap; audit achieved ratio** — Counts use len(text)+1 per atom, but detok keeps verbatim indentation on kept atoms. A random-score simulation at rate 0.5 gave output/input char ratios from about 0.38 (indent-4 JSON) to 0.57 (compact JSON), so 'rate' varies by format.
  - Test: Run the real model at rate 0.5 on pretty JSON, compact JSON, traces and logs; plot achieved char and token ratios; try whitespace-aware counts.
- **#200** [M] _small_ **Demote protected atoms after they repeat k times** — is_protected force-keeps INFO, DEBUG, ERROR, GET, POST, PASSED, FAILED (all-caps, verified) and every digit atom on every line. Protect only the first few occurrences of a literal, freeing budget in logs; differs from class-by-class relaxation.
  - Test: On 1000-line logs count budget consumed by repeated protected literals; apply the frequency cap and compare QA accuracy at equal output chars.
- **#204** [M] **Scale rate and threshold by context fill** — Hook input carries transcript_path and session_id, yet rate and min_chars are constants. Prune harder as the transcript nears auto-compact and gentler early, since pruned tokens delay compaction and are re-read every turn.
  - Test: Verify that context fill is readable from transcript_path; replay long transcripts with rate falling from 0.6 to 0.35 by fill; compare compaction count, tokens and success versus constant 0.5.
- **#209** ★ [R] _medium_ **BM25 chunk selection against command and prompt intent** — For outputs over 5 KB, split into chunks (headings, blank lines, stanzas), rank by BM25 against the command plus last user prompt, keep top chunks with context windows; rest saved. context-mode reportedly does this with SQLite FTS5, no model.
  - Test: 30 large outputs with known answer spans; compare answer-chunk recall at equal tokens against the token model at 0.5 and against head/tail.
- **#210** [M] **Local small-LM abstractive digest for giant outputs** — For outputs over ~50 KB, a 0.5-1.5B local instruction model writes a short digest with line references; original saved. Abstractive variant of RECOMP (Xu et al. 2023); ctxprune today only deletes words. CPU latency is the risk.
  - Test: 20 giant build/test logs; compare QA accuracy, CPU seconds and tokens against token deletion at equal output size.
- **#260** [M] **Small causal-LM surprisal as selection and repetition signal** — Selective Context (Li et al. 2023) and LLMLingua v1 rank tokens by small-LM self-information; ctxprune uses only the supervised classifier. A causal LM sees earlier copies, so repeated lines get near-zero surprisal without hand-written dedup. Needs no labels; combine with classifier score.
  - Test: Score 30 outputs, 20 repetitive logs among them, with a ~135M causal LM on CPU; Spearman versus classifier; QA at equal tokens for surprisal alone and times classifier; note latency.
- **#263** [M] _small_ **Hashed-feature linear atom scorer distilled from the classifier** — Train a fastText-style linear model on classifier scores using atom shape, hashed neighbour words, line position and indentation. Aims to run in numpy in milliseconds without ONNX or the 143 MB download, so it could serve outputs under 6k chars or cold-start.
  - Test: Fit on 5k scored chunks; on held-out report Spearman and top-50% overlap with the classifier, latency, and QA at 0.5 versus classifier and random.
- **#266** [M] **Ablation-attribution labels from a small reader LM** — Label each line by how much deleting it lowers a small instruct LM's likelihood of the agent's recorded next action or quoted text (leave-one-out). Task-grounded labels without a teacher prompt, rather than verbatim-quote matching.
  - Test: Take 100 SWE-smith tool outputs with known next turns; compute line importance with a ~1.5B LM; measure overlap with teacher v2 keep set and quote labels.
- **#267** [M] **Reader-verified teacher labels: drop chunks that fail QA** — Generate questions per training chunk from the original, answer them from the teacher's compressed text with a small reader, and discard or downweight chunks that fail. Task-grounded label filter, complementing LLMLingua-2's variation-rate and alignment-gap filters (per its paper) and the hand audit.
  - Test: 300 labeled chunks, 5 questions each; report pass rate by source; fine-tune on pass-only versus all; compare QA at 0.33 and 0.5 kept.
- **#268** [M] **LogHub annotated templates as free token labels** — LogPAI's Loghub datasets ship hand-annotated templates for HDFS, BGL, OpenStack and others; template constants versus parameters give per-token labels without an LLM teacher. The corpus build has no system or CI log source. Check dataset license first.
  - Test: Label Loghub-2k with constants dropped and parameters kept; measure the current model's ROC-AUC against it; then add to training and compare log-chunk QA.
- **#270** [M] _small_ `MCP` **Per-command-family rate bandit and refetch circuit breaker** — Thompson sampling over rates {skip, 0.7, 0.5, 0.33}, keyed by the command's first two words or MCP tool. Reward is tokens saved minus a penalty for a rerun or grep of the saved original. Refetches near 1 minus keep roughly cancel savings; auto-skip that family.
  - Test: Verify that refetches are detectable from Bash commands; replay recorded transcripts with regret events as penalties; compare net tokens against constant 0.5 and the best fixed rate per family.
- **#298** [X] _tiny_ `eval` **Protect-set recall: hyphenated names, bare filenames, quoted strings** — is_protected leaves Makefile, Dockerfile, left-pad, serde-json, feature-login, Error and 'quoted' unprotected, so they can be deleted. Branch, package, container and subcommand names are what later commands reuse. Measure how often copied tokens were unprotected.
  - Test: From transcripts, collect tokens later copied into Bash or Edit inputs that appeared in compressed outputs; report unprotected share by class; add class rules and re-measure misses.
- **#299** [R] _tiny_ **Shield backticked commands and hint lines from deletion** — 'Run `npm audit fix --force`' keeps only '--force' because npm, audit and fix are unprotected; rustc 'help:', git 'hint:' and pip 'Try:' lines fare the same. Agents copy these suggestions. Keep backtick spans and hint-prefixed lines whole.
  - Test: Collect hint lines from cargo, git, npm, pip outputs; compress at 0.5; count commands that no longer match the original after shlex split; add the shield and recount.
- **#302** [M] **Hindsight waste labels: useless, redundant, expired spans** — AgentDiet (arXiv 2509.23586) has a cheap LLM reflect on a step's output after later steps and mark useless, redundant or expired spans, reportedly cutting input tokens 40-60%. Run it offline as a labeler for classifier training, since live hindsight rewrites break cache.
  - Test: On 30 transcript tool results plus the next 3 steps, label spans with Haiku; compare against the classifier's drops and the verbatim-reuse labels from item 169.
- **#356** [M] **Fail/pass log-pair differential labels with no LLM teacher** — Run pytest, cargo or npm on a fixture repo with and without a seeded bug, align the two logs. Lines identical in both runs are boilerplate (drop); lines that differ carry the signal (keep). Labels come from program behavior, not a prompt.
  - Test: Make 20 before/after pairs from SWE-smith repos; label by line diff; measure atom agreement with teacher v2 labels and failing-test-name recall at 0.33 kept.
- **#357** [M] **Localized CLI output corpus: German, Japanese, Russian tool logs** — Re-run git, gcc, pip, apt and pytest under LC_ALL=de, ja, ru, zh, fr so real tools emit translated diagnostics. Transfer English-run labels by line position and anchors. Fills the multilingual-tool-output gap; fineweb2 is web prose only.
  - Test: Run 5 commands under 6 locales, compress at 0.5, count paths, codes and identifiers lost versus the English run; check per-script deletion rate.
- **#358** [M] _small_ **Odd-one-out synthetic logs as hard negatives** — Take repetitive logs or listings, mutate one line (new status word, changed value, rare exception), label that line keep and its near-duplicates drop. Teaches the classifier that one deviation among hundreds of look-alikes matters.
  - Test: Build 200 logs of 300-3000 lines with one planted anomaly; measure how often the anomaly line survives at 0.33 for the current model, then after fine-tuning.
- **#359** [M] **Entropy and committee sampling to choose chunks for teacher labeling** — Pool unlabeled public and local saved outputs; score each chunk by mean keep-probability entropy or disagreement between int8, fp32 and earlier checkpoints; send only the top slice to the teacher. Spends GPU labeling budget where the student is least sure.
  - Test: Label 1k top-entropy versus 1k random chunks with the same teacher, fine-tune each from the current checkpoint, compare AUC and QA at 0.5 on held-out real hook outputs.
- **#360** [M] **Re-serialize labeled JSON as YAML, XML, CSV, tables** — Take teacher-labeled Toucan JSON records, re-render the same objects as YAML, XML, CSV, markdown tables and key=value lines, carrying keep/drop labels by key path. Fills format gaps in a JSON-heavy corpus and trains format invariance.
  - Test: Probe first, no training: run the current model on JSON versus YAML renderings of 50 records; report Jaccard of kept key paths at 0.5.
- **#361** [M] **Self-consistency soft labels from repeated teacher sampling** — Sample the teacher five times at temperature 0.7; per-atom keep frequency becomes a soft label, and high-variance chunks are flagged as noisy. Differs from the two-prompt v1/v2 grading by measuring the teacher's own noise.
  - Test: Sample 5 outputs for 200 chunks; report atom-level Fleiss kappa by source, then train soft versus hard labels on equal chunks and compare AUC and QA at 0.33.
- **#362** [M] **Multi-family teacher consensus to break teacher-reader overlap** — The model card notes one reader model also labeled the training data. Relabel chunks with two other model families and train on agreement-weighted labels, so gains are not tuned to one family's taste.
  - Test: Relabel 500 chunks with two other families; report atom agreement; compare QA at 0.5 for single-teacher versus consensus-trained students using a reader from a third family.
- **#363** [M] **Domain-adaptive masked-LM pretraining on unlabeled tool outputs** — Labels are the bottleneck, raw tool outputs are not. Continue mmBERT-small's masked-LM pretraining on many unlabeled logs, JSON and traces (Gururangan et al. 2020, domain-adaptive pretraining), then fine-tune on the existing teacher labels.
  - Test: One MLM epoch over ~50k unlabeled tool outputs, then fine-tune on 10% and 100% of labels; compare val AUC against plain mmBERT-small, especially in the 10% arm.
- **#364** [M] **Whole-document teacher labels capturing cross-window redundancy** — Corpus chunks are at most 500 tokens, at most 2 random chunks per document, labeled in isolation. Hook inputs run thousands of tokens. Label full documents so repeated content is dropped on later occurrences, train on 2-4k-token rows.
  - Test: Label 100 outputs over 6k chars whole versus per 500-token chunk with the same teacher; count atoms whose label flips, split by repeated-line versus unique-line atoms.
- **#365** [M] **Mutation-testing labels for code: load-bearing lines** — For small Python modules with unit tests, delete each statement and rerun tests or py_compile. Lines whose removal breaks something are keep; comments, docstrings and dead code are drop. A deterministic code oracle with no LLM teacher.
  - Test: On 20 permissive modules with tests, compute load-bearing flags; compare to teacher and model keep scores per line; check that signatures score high and comments low.
- **#366** [M] _tiny_ **Mask protected atoms from training loss and validation AUC** — is_protected atoms are force-kept under protect, yet count in loss and AUC. On a toy 60-line log 62% of atoms were protected (10% in JSON, 0% in prose). Masking focuses capacity on real decisions and de-inflates per-source AUC.
  - Test: Recompute val AUC per source for the current checkpoint with protected atoms excluded; then fine-tune with protected labels set to -100 and compare QA at 0.33 with protect on.
- **#367** [X] **Relabel corpus after deterministic pre-pass to avoid skew** — Planned lossless rules (ANSI strip, minify, dedent, blank-run collapse) change what the model sees, but it trained on raw text. Apply the pre-pass to corpus chunks before teacher labeling, or measure score drift first.
  - Test: Apply the pre-pass to 300 chunks; compare per-atom score correlation and keep-rate drift before versus after; relabel 100 and compare AUC on raw versus prepassed.
- **#378** [X] `eval` **Int8 batch-composition and thread-count determinism audit** — Windows are padded and scored 16 per batch by an int8 ONNX model. If activation scales are dynamic or kernels vary by thread count, a window may score differently depending on its neighbours, breaking replay and A/B. Unverified here; model not cached.
  - Test: Score 200 windows alone, in batches of 1, 16 and shuffled, with 1 and 8 threads; report keep-decision flips and max score drift; compare fp32.
- **#455** [M] _tiny_ **Trim the embedding vocabulary to tokens seen in tool output** — mmBERT-small reportedly spends most of its 140M parameters on a ~256k-token embedding table (unverified). Drop rows unseen on a tool-output plus multilingual sample and remap tokenizer ids, as in 'Load What You Need' (Abdaoui et al. 2020) for mBERT. Smaller download, load, RSS.
  - Test: Verify that the embedding table dominates parameters; count token ids hit over 1k real outputs; trim; report file size, load time, ROC-AUC and keep-decision agreement, including non-English logs.
- **#456** [M] _tiny_ **Do embedding lookup from np.memmap, feed ONNX inputs_embeds** — Export the transformer body without the Gather over the embedding table; look up only the rows each window needs from a memory-mapped array and pass inputs_embeds. Resident memory and session load shrink, no vocabulary or accuracy change. Keeps non-English intact, unlike vocab trimming.
  - Test: Export with inputs_embeds, compare logits to the stock graph on 200 windows, and measure RSS and cold load time with a cold versus warm page cache.
- **#457** [X] _tiny_ **Pick int8 or fp32 per CPU with a first-run benchmark** — Int8 is assumed faster everywhere, but dynamic-int8 speed reportedly depends on VNNI or dot-product instructions, and users span Apple silicon, aarch64 and older x86. If int8 loses on some CPUs, a one-window timing at install can choose the file.
  - Test: Per-window latency of int8 versus fp32 on AVX2-only x86, AVX512-VNNI, Apple M-series and aarch64 Linux; if int8 ever loses, gate the 563 MB fp32 download behind it.
- **#458** [X] _tiny_ `eval` **Sweep ONNX threads, spinning, arena and batch size defaults** — compress.py sets only the log level, so threads, spin-waiting, memory arena, batch 16 and dynamic widths are all defaults. Parallel hooks or an idle serve may steal CPU from the build just run. Try intra_op 2-4, allow_spinning=0, arena off, batch 1-4.
  - Test: On 4 cores, time 100 windows per setting, plus serve idle CPU over 10 min and latency with 3 concurrent hooks; pick the Pareto setting.
- **#460** [M] _tiny_ **Line-aligned content-defined windows plus on-disk score cache** — Windows cut every ~510 tokens, so one inserted early line shifts and rescores every later window. Cut at newlines chosen by a rolling hash (content-defined chunking) and cache per-window keep-probabilities on disk by token-id hash, usable without serve.
  - Test: Score two runs of one log differing by a line near the start; compare per-line score drift and cache hit rate, fixed versus content-defined cuts; check QA parity.
- **#461** [L] _tiny_ **Skip model calls on windows holding only protected atoms** — The hook passes force_protected=True, so numbers, IDs and paths are kept whatever their score. Windows made only of such atoms (hex dumps, numeric tables) cannot change the output, yet are still scored. Skip them, still counting their chars against the budget; latency drops.
  - Test: Count windows with zero unprotected atoms over a captured corpus, then assert byte-identical output with and without the skip and record the time saved.
- **#464** [M] _tiny_ **Streaming filter mode with a running score quantile** — The CLI reads all stdin before scoring, so a piped docker logs -f or long build shows nothing and holds everything in memory. Score each window as it fills and cut at a running quantile of earlier scores, approximating global top-k.
  - Test: On 20 saved logs, compare streaming kept-atom overlap with global top-k at rate 0.5 and the achieved-rate error; pipe a 500 MB file and track peak memory.
- **#494** [R] _tiny_ **Keep separator punctuation between protected atoms: 1,299 and 80,443** — is_protected(',') is false, so when the model drops a comma, detok leaves a space (simulated: 1,299.00 gives '1 299.00', 80,443,8080 gives '80 443 8080', 3,14 gives '3 14'). The README says numbers are kept exactly. Force-keep zero-whitespace punctuation flanked by protected atoms.
