  - Test: Count digit-comma-digit and key=value spans in saved originals; run the model; count spans split in output; add the rule and compare tokens at equal QA.
- **#496** [M] **Size-dependent keep rate instead of a constant 0.5** — Hook applies rate 0.5 to a 6k-char and a 30k-char output alike, but redundancy plausibly grows with length. Try target = max(floor, rate * N^0.8) or an absolute cap near 2k tokens per call; the rate parameter is already per call, so no retraining.
  - Test: Bucket saved originals or corpus chunks by size; at equal total kept tokens compare reader QA for constant rate versus sublinear budget; report accuracy per kept token.
- **#535** [M] **Session-seen atom prior: drop-bias for atoms already in context** — Add a classifier feature, or post-score demotion, for identifiers, paths and phrases seen in the last K thousand context tokens, since the reader can resolve them. #164 and #200 only look within one output.
  - Test: Offline: measure the share of atoms already present in the previous 20k tokens of recorded sessions; then QA at equal rate with and without demotion.
- **#553** [M] **Conformal-calibrated keep threshold with a recall guarantee** — Fixed rate 0.5 ignores per-call risk. Calibrate a score cutoff on held-out teacher-labelled chunks (conformal risk control, Angelopoulos et al., reportedly 2022) so expected recall of must-keep atoms is at least 1-alpha; emit the cutoff, capped by budget.
  - Test: Calibrate alpha=0.1 on half of val rows per source; on the other half compare achieved recall and kept fraction against fixed rate 0.5 and 0.33.
- **#554** [M] _small_ **Predictive-entropy OOD gate: gentler rate on unfamiliar windows** — AGENTS.md says the model is weaker on code and non-English text, yet the rate is constant. Per-window mean softmax entropy of the existing logits is a free uncertainty signal; high-entropy windows get a higher keep rate or pass through.
  - Test: Log per-window entropy on code, German/Japanese logs, JSON, English logs; check separation, then QA at equal tokens with entropy-scaled rate versus constant 0.5.
- **#555** [M] _small_ **Atom-normalised loss weights so each atom counts once** — Training loss runs per subtoken, so digit-split numbers, hashes and paths weigh many times a prose word. In a local cl100k proxy, protected atoms were 52-79% of tokens in ls -lR, dpkg -l and git log --stat. Weight each token 1/n subtokens.
  - Test: Fine-tune one epoch with 1/n token weights versus plain CE on the same rows; compare atom-level AUC on unprotected atoms and QA at 0.33 kept.
- **#556** [X] `eval` **Select checkpoints by atom-level top-k recall, not token AUC** — The trainer picks the best epoch by subtoken ROC-AUC (metric_for_best_model=auc), but deployment averages subtokens per atom and cuts a char budget. Compute char-weighted recall of teacher-keep atoms at 0.33 and 0.5, protected excluded.
  - Test: Re-evaluate saved v1/v2 checkpoints and epochs with both metrics on val; check whether the winner changes and which metric tracks stored QA results better.
- **#557** [M] _small_ **Shape-collapsed scorer input: placeholders for force-kept atoms** — The hook force-keeps numbers, hashes and paths, yet the encoder spends tokens reading them (digits split one by one). Substitute a short shape placeholder in the scorer input only, so each 512-token window covers more real content and runs faster.
  - Test: On 50 saved outputs, compare unprotected-atom scores raw versus collapsed (Spearman, top-50% overlap), window count and latency; then QA at equal tokens with existing weights.
- **#558** [M] **Cluster near-duplicate lines by encoder hidden states** — Export the last hidden layer as a second ONNX output from the same forward pass; mean-pool per line, cosine-cluster, keep one exemplar per cluster and fold the rest into a count. Catches paraphrased repeats that template mining and MinHash may miss.
  - Test: On 20 logs, compare clusters at cosine 0.95 with Drain3 templates and exact duplicates; count extra foldable lines and spot-check QA on folded counts.
- **#559** [M] **Confident-learning label-error detection from out-of-fold predictions** — Train k-fold models on the teacher labels; atoms where out-of-fold P(keep) strongly contradicts the label are flagged as likely teacher noise (confident learning, Northcutt et al. 2021; cleanlab). Automates the 200-label hand audit (#96) and feeds relabel or drop.
  - Test: 3-fold on a 5k-chunk subset; hand-check 100 flagged atoms against 100 random ones for error rate; train on cleaned versus raw labels and compare QA at 0.5.
- **#560** [M] **Auxiliary window-level keep-fraction head for learned per-window budgets** — Add a regression head on the pooled encoder output predicting the share of atoms the teacher kept in the window, free from existing labels. At inference it sets each window's budget, so repetitive windows lose more and dense ones less than a constant rate.
  - Test: Train the head on existing rows; report R-squared and correlation with zlib ratio; then QA at equal total tokens for predicted-budget versus constant-rate allocation.
- **#561** [M] _tiny_ **Per-layer linear probes to find an early-exit truncated encoder** — Fit a keep/drop linear probe on each layer's frozen hidden states using existing labels. If a middle layer holds most of the final AUC, export an ONNX graph truncated there with that head, cutting per-window compute and file size.
  - Test: Extract hidden states for 2k val windows; plot probe AUC and top-50% overlap with the full model per layer; time a truncated int8 graph at the best layer.
- **#562** [M] **Nested keep tiers with stored ranks for progressive expansion** — Global top-k makes the 0.33 output a subset of 0.5 and 0.8. Save one-byte atom ranks next to the original; an expand command re-renders a higher tier (optionally for a line range) without the model, cheaper than reading the full original.
  - Test: Assert tiers nest on 20 outputs; on recovery tasks needing a cut fact, compare tokens spent on expand-to-0.8 of a line range versus reading the whole original.
- **#563** [M] **Value-swap augmentation: keep decisions should ignore which numbers appear** — Probe first: replace digits, hashes and paths in outputs with fresh random values and measure score drift on unprotected atoms. If large, retrain with label-preserving value swaps and formatting noise (ANSI codes, CRLF, indent changes) so the model stops keying on specific strings.
  - Test: Perturb 100 saved outputs, rescore, report Spearman and top-50% Jaccard on unprotected atoms; if below 0.95, fine-tune with swaps and compare QA at 0.5.
- **#564** [M] _tiny_ **Balance window splits to avoid tiny context-starved tail windows** — Greedy 510-token windows leave a remainder window of a few tokens, possibly under the 48-token training minimum (chunking.py), scored with almost no context. Split n tokens into ceil(n/510) near-equal windows at atom boundaries, or at least split the last two evenly.
  - Test: Count tail windows under 64 tokens on saved outputs; compare tail-atom scores, kept share and top-k overlap between greedy and balanced splits; check latency is unchanged.
- **#591** [X] _tiny_ `eval` **Detect truncated teacher outputs under the len//2 token cap** — Teacher max_tokens is len(text)//2+64 and finish_reason is never read. Digit-splitting tokenizers (reportedly Qwen) can need about one token per character on numeric or hex chunks, so cut-off outputs would label the tail as dropped.
  - Test: Count teacher responses with finish_reason length; compare tail keep rates on digit-heavy chunks with and without those rows excluded.
- **#598** [M] **Reward-driven pruner training: reader-verified mask rejection sampling** — PCRL and TACO-RL (2024, reportedly) train a keep/drop policy with a reader-task reward plus length penalty, not teacher labels. Cheaper variant: sample several masks per window from classifier probabilities, keep the shortest one the reader still answers from, retrain on those.
  - Test: On 200 windows with QA pairs, sample 8 masks each at p-scaled keep rates; measure how often the shortest passing mask beats the current 0.5 mask in kept tokens.
- **#645** [M] **Bidirectional cloze-redundancy score from the base MLM head** — Mask atoms in batches and read the MLM head of mmBERT-small, the base checkpoint ctxprune was tuned from. Atoms both neighbours predict confidently are redundant. Label-free, uses right context unlike left-to-right surprisal (#260), and is a selector, not the #171 metric.
  - Test: On 30 mixed outputs, mask 15% of atoms per pass; compute Spearman against classifier scores, QA at equal tokens for cloze alone and blended, and CPU seconds.
- **#648** [M] _small_ **Per-family linear heads on the frozen encoder, routed by command** — Keep one shared 140M encoder; train tiny keep/drop linear heads per family (traces, tests, JSON, prose, build logs) on frozen hidden states, selected by command regex. Far cheaper than per-family fine-tunes (#96); one ONNX graph can emit all heads.
  - Test: Train 5 heads on frozen features with existing labels; compare per-source validation ROC-AUC and QA at 0.5 against the shared head.
- **#649** [M] **Line-selection aggregator sweep with top-atom residue for dropped lines** — Line-level mode (#87) loses a line whose one rare word matters. Score lines by max, mean or top-2 atom score, and let each dropped line leave its single best atom as a trace. Sweeps aggregator and residue, which #87 leaves unspecified.
  - Test: On 20 logs and test outputs, compare mean, max and top-2 line scores, with and without residue, on needle recall and QA at equal tokens.
- **#650** [R] _small_ **LexRank/TextRank sentence centrality as a model-free prose baseline** — Build a TF-IDF cosine graph over sentences, rank by centrality (TextRank, Mihalcea and Tarau 2004; LexRank, Erkan and Radev 2004) plus lead bias, keep top sentences verbatim. Runs in milliseconds, so it can serve prose before the 143 MB model is downloaded.
  - Test: On 30 README, issue and docs outputs, compare QA accuracy and tokens against the classifier at 0.5 and against lead-only truncation.
- **#651** [M] _small_ **Learned line-type tagger routes mixed outputs to per-segment compressors** — A tiny character n-gram tagger labels each line (JSON, table, stack trace, log, prose, code, diff); contiguous segments then get the matching rule or rate. Replaces global sniffing for mixed outputs, such as a log with embedded JSON and a traceback.
  - Test: Label 500 lines from 40 captured outputs; train a fastText-style tagger; report line accuracy and compare per-segment against single-rate compression on QA at equal tokens.
- **#681** [X] _tiny_ **detok flattens indentation after any dropped preceding atom** — Verified: when the atom before a line-leading kept atom was dropped, _merge_ws returns a bare newline and the original indentation is lost. YAML hierarchy, traceback frames and nested JSON flatten. Keeping newline-led whitespace costs few tokens.
  - Test: Count kept line-leading atoms whose indentation changed over 100 saved outputs; ask parent-key and calling-frame questions on flattened versus indentation-preserved variants at equal tokens.
- **#682** [M] _tiny_ **Time-budgeted windows: keep unscored tail verbatim at deadline** — In-process compression is all-or-nothing against the 60 s host kill; a very large output wastes the whole run and passes uncompressed. Score windows in order until about 40 s, keep the remaining windows verbatim, and still emit a note.
  - Test: Set the deadline to 2 s via env, feed a 300 KB log; assert output returns in time, early windows compressed, tail verbatim, note names the cutoff.
- **#739** [X] _tiny_ `eval` **Cross-source split leakage: same repo in train and test** — The group key carries the source name, so a repo hashes independently in swe_smith, swe_rebench and github_code. Simulating split_of on 2,000 made-up repos, 12% split differently across three sources and 6.5% land in both test and train. Real overlap is unmeasured.
  - Test: Normalise owner__repo and owner/repo across sources; count val/test chunks whose repo also has train chunks; re-run the QA eval without them and compare.
- **#740** [M] **Weak-supervision label model over many cheap labelers** — Combine teacher v1, v2, template-constant, corpus document-frequency, compressor marginal cost and later-quote signals with a Snorkel-style generative label model (Ratner et al.), estimating each source's accuracy without gold labels. Yields probabilistic labels for a large unlabeled pool.
  - Test: Write 5 labeling functions over 500 chunks; fit the label model; check agreement with the teacher and 100 hand labels; train aggregated versus teacher-only and compare QA at 0.5.
- **#741** [M] **Noisy-student self-training on unlabeled local tool outputs** — Score a large unlabeled pool of captured outputs with the current student, then retrain with soft pseudo-labels plus noise (dropout, value swaps, ANSI injection), as in Noisy Student (Xie et al. 2020, ImageNet). Extends domain coverage without teacher calls. Differs from MLM pretraining (#363).
  - Test: Pseudo-label 5k unlabeled hook-style outputs; train with and without them from the same init; compare AUC on held-out teacher labels and on 50 hand-labeled real outputs.
- **#742** [M] _small_ **Severity-word confusers: benign 'error' lines, fatal lines without it** — Build hard negatives and positives for lexical shortcuts: '0 errors', 'failed=0', test_handles_error and DEBUG error-handler lines should drop; 'Killed', 'exit status 137', 'panic:', 'command not found', 'Permission denied' must keep. Label by construction, no teacher.
  - Test: Score 200 synthetic lines of each kind inside real log windows; report keep rate at 0.33 for benign-with-keyword versus fatal-without-keyword; fine-tune on confusers only if the gap is large.
- **#743** [M] _small_ **Position tokens: tell the scorer which window of the output** — chunk_doc keeps 2 random chunks per document and discards their index, so head or tail position is invisible to training. Record chunk index and relative depth; prepend a first/middle/last control token at training and inference.
  - Test: Rebuild the corpus recording chunk index for 1k long docs; plot teacher keep rate by relative position; if head or tail differ, train with tags and compare per-position AUC.
- **#744** [M] _small_ **Splice chunks from different formats into one training window** — Real hook inputs mix formats in one blob: a command echo, JSON, stack trace, table. Training chunks are single-source. Concatenate chunks from two or three sources with their labels intact, so the model learns regime switches and boundaries. Probe score drift first.
  - Test: Build 300 spliced windows from labeled val chunks; rescore; Spearman of each half versus its isolated score. If below 0.9, fine-tune with splices and compare AUC on held-out spliced windows.
- **#745** [M] _small_ **Repair teacher recall with verbatim answer-span quotes** — Ask a stronger LLM for 5 questions per chunk with verbatim answer quotes; atoms inside quotes the teacher dropped are flipped to keep. Repairs labels where #267 would discard the chunk, and gives a per-source teacher-recall number.
  - Test: On 300 labeled chunks, measure the share of answer-quote atoms the teacher dropped, by source; fine-tune on repaired versus original labels; compare QA at 0.33 with a different-family reader.
- **#746** [M] **Listwise teacher ranking of lines for continuous importance labels** — Instead of two keep/drop prompts, ask the LLM to order 15-20 lines by importance in sliding windows (RankGPT-style listwise ranking, Sun et al. 2023), then merge into a per-chunk rank. Yields labels usable at any budget, with no word-count target.
  - Test: Rank 200 chunks of 40 lines; compare Kendall tau with v1/v2 graded labels, then train on rank-derived targets versus v1/v2 and compare needle-line recall at 0.33.
- **#747** [M] _small_ `MCP` **Machine-translate MCP issue and comment text, keep identifiers fixed** — Multilingual data today is web prose; the CLI-locale idea (#357) covers only terminal tools. Real MCP results (GitHub issues, Jira, Slack) can carry Dutch, German or Chinese comments beside English keys and code. Translate string leaves of Toucan-style JSON, relabel with the teacher.
  - Test: Translate string values of 100 Toucan records into 4 languages; compare per-language drop rate and lost identifiers/numbers against the English original at 0.5, before any training.
- **#748** [M] **Verbosity-ladder labels: quiet output as subsequence of verbose** — Run one command at -q, default and -v (or LOG_LEVEL=WARNING versus DEBUG). Tool authors already chose what matters at each level, so the quiet output aligned as a subsequence of the verbose one (existing LCS aligner) gives free keep labels. No LLM teacher.
  - Test: For 10 tools, run quiet and verbose; use align's variation_rate to find true subsequences; keep pairs under 10%; compare model scores on kept versus dropped lines.
- **#749** [M] _small_ **LogChunks: human-labeled CI failure spans as keep labels** — Corpus tool sources are Python-centric (SWE-smith and SWE-rebench reportedly Python repos) with no CI build logs. LogChunks (Brandt et al., MSR 2020, reportedly) pairs Travis logs from many languages with human-marked failure-cause chunks. Use as keep labels and an out-of-domain recall test.
  - Test: Verify that LogChunks is downloadable and licensed for training; on 100 logs, measure failure-chunk atoms kept at 0.33 and 0.5 per language; fine-tune on half, test on the rest.
- **#771** [M] **Off-the-shelf cross-encoder reranks output chunks against command intent** — Split a large log or doc into 40-line chunks and score them against command plus last prompt with a small MS MARCO cross-encoder (cross-encoder/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2, sentence-transformers), keeping top chunks verbatim. Model-based counterpart to BM25 (#209); no training.
  - Test: On 100 saved outputs with a known answer line, compare top-3 chunk recall for BM25, cross-encoder and ctxprune alone at equal kept tokens.
- **#774** [R] **Model-free atom scorer: word frequency times in-output IDF** — A stronger contrarian control than stopword deletion (#297), and a cold-start tier with no 143 MB download: score atoms by negative log of a bundled 20k-word frequency table plus within-output document frequency, keeping the top atoms with the same budget loop in compress.py.
  - Test: Feed the scores into compress()'s budget loop; compare reader QA against the model at 0.5 per content type; report where it ties and its latency.
- **#777** [R] _tiny_ **Paired-delimiter closure after atom deletion: fences, backticks, brackets** — atoms.py splits '```', '**' and quotes into one-character atoms, so top-k can keep an opening backtick or fence and drop its partner; the reader then sees everything after it as code or bold. Post-pass: drop or restore the partner of each pair. Cf. #173.
  - Test: On 100 markdown and log outputs, count outputs whose backtick, fence or bracket parity flips versus the original; add the post-pass and compare reader QA on those.
- **#785** [R] _small_ **Use Bash tool_input.description as a free intent query** — The model already writes a one-line description for every Bash call (#443 counts its cost). Feed it, with the command, to BM25 chunk selection (#209) or query-aware boosting (#89). Unlike #191 and #655, it needs no new tool or transcript read.
  - Test: On 30 captured outputs with known answer spans, compare answer-chunk recall at equal tokens for description-only, command-only and both as BM25 query; report how often descriptions are empty or generic.
- **#835** [X] `eval` **Int8 keep-decision drift across CPU instruction sets** — scripts/export_onnx.py notes signed int8 or a quantized head drops keep agreement to 0.1-0.84, so the model is quantization-fragile. ORT reportedly picks different int8 matmul kernels per CPU (AVX2, VNNI, ARM64), so rankings, and savings, may differ by laptop.
  - Test: Score 200 windows on CI runners (linux x64, macos arm64, windows) against an fp32 reference; compare top-50% atom overlap and max probability gap; flag outlier machines.
- **#836** [M] **Per-layer int8 sensitivity map and static calibrated quantization** — Only the head is kept fp32, though the export comment shows the model breaks easily. Quantize all but one layer in turn to find fragile layers, and try static QDQ calibrated on 256 real tool-output windows instead of dynamic activation scales.
  - Test: Export baseline, per-layer-exempt and static variants; report MB, ms per window, ROC-AUC and top-50% atom agreement with fp32 for each; keep the Pareto winner.
- **#837** [X] **Try CoreML, DirectML and OpenVINO execution providers** — compress.py hard-codes CPUExecutionProvider, so Apple Silicon and Windows users never get an accelerator. Faster windows would let the 6,000-char threshold drop (#277). Those providers reportedly lack dynamic-int8 ops, so this needs the fp32 or fp16 graph.
  - Test: On a Mac and a Windows box time 100 windows: int8 on CPU versus fp16 or fp32 on each available provider; check keep-decision agreement and cold session-creation time.
- **#923** ★ [M] _medium_ **Learned protect head replacing the is_protected regex** — Second head trained to flag atoms a reader later needs verbatim (labels from teacher answer-span quotes and agent copy-forward). Context-aware: keeps the failing test id, drops the 500th timestamp. Frees budget now eaten by force-kept numbers.
  - Test: Label protect=1 where a later command or answer copies the atom; train head on frozen encoder; compare copied-atom recall and QA at equal chars versus regex protection.
- **#924** [R] _small_ **Orphan-value coupling: keep numbers with their label words** — force_protected keeps numbers while their labels can drop; random stub scores turned 'Compiled 142 modules in 3.2s with 2 warnings' into '142 3.2s 2'. Post-pass keeps the nearest preceding word of each kept number, or drops numbers whose label went.
  - Test: On 50 saved outputs, count kept numeric atoms with no kept word within two atoms on the line; run QA on 'how many/how long' questions for each variant.
- **#925** [M] _tiny_ **Mid-line crop augmentation to match inference window seams** — chunking.py cuts training chunks on line boundaries, but compress.py cuts inference windows at any atom every ~510 tokens. Randomly crop training chunks to mid-line starts and ends, labels kept, so seam tokens are in-distribution.
  - Test: Measure val AUC by token position inside fixed windows; fine-tune with crops; compare first/last-32-token AUC and QA at 0.5 on outputs over 6,000 chars.
- **#926** [M] _small_ **Group-DRO training loss over source and format groups** — Replace average cross-entropy with group-DRO (Sagawa et al., 2020): online exponentiated weights on per-group loss over code, non-English, JSON, logs, prose. Aims at the weaknesses AGENTS.md admits, not the average.
  - Test: Fine-tune one epoch from the current checkpoint with group-DRO; report per-group and worst-group within-chunk AUC versus baseline, then QA on code and non-English slices at 0.5.
- **#927** [M] **Gradient-boosted rescorer over frozen scores and handcrafted features** — Train LightGBM LambdaRank on the classifier's atom score, neighbour scores, atom class, line position, repeat count and command family, with teacher labels. One learned rescoring replaces hand-tuned adjustments; retrains on CPU in minutes, no encoder retrain.
  - Test: From a frozen score table of 500 outputs, train on 70% of chunks; compare top-50% atom recall and QA at equal chars versus raw scores on the held-out 30%.
- **#928** [M] _small_ **Small-encoder bake-off: ELECTRA-small, DeBERTa-v3-xsmall, MiniLM** — Fine-tune each on the existing labels. ELECTRA's replaced-token-detection pretraining is token-level like this task, and it is reportedly about 14M parameters, so load time and latency could drop. English-only vocabularies are the risk.
  - Test: Train each on v1 labels for equal epochs; report val AUC, top-50% atom overlap with mmBERT-small, int8 ONNX size, ms per window, and QA at 0.5.
- **#929** [M] _tiny_ **Weight-only int8 or 4-bit ONNX with fp32 activations** — The export comment says signed int8 or a quantized head collapses keep agreement to 0.1-0.84. Try weight-only quantization (ORT MatMulNBits for 4-bit, reportedly available), leaving activations in fp32, for fewer rank flips at similar size. Differs from #836.
  - Test: Export dynamic-int8, weight-only int8 and 4-bit variants; compare Spearman and top-50% atom agreement with fp32 on 500 windows, file MB, ms per window.
- **#930** [M] _tiny_ **Read-only header carry-over prefix for later scoring windows** — Windows after the first lose the table header row, JSON parent keys and test-suite banner. Prepend the first lines plus the nearest lower-indent scope line to each later window, labels masked in training and ignored at inference.
  - Test: On 30 multi-window tables and JSON outputs, compare window-2+ scores with and without prefix against a 4k-token whole-document pass; then QA at 0.5 and latency.
- **#931** [M] _small_ **Per-line sentinel tokens with a learned line keep head** — Insert one learnable token at each line start; pool its hidden state into a line-level keep/drop head trained on lines the teacher emptied, beside the atom head. Whole-line drops become one learned decision, not a sum of atom scores.
  - Test: Count teacher-emptied lines in v1 labels; train the sentinel head; compare line-drop precision and recall against thresholding mean atom score (#87) and QA at 0.5 on logs and test output.
- **#932** [M] **Structured-twin labels: machine-readable output marks essential atoms** — Run each build or test command twice, human text and machine-readable form (cargo --message-format=json, go test -json, eslint -f json, pytest junitxml). Atoms matching the tool's own file, line, code, message and test-name fields get keep=1; the rest drop, with no LLM teacher.
  - Test: On 20 small repos, make twin pairs for 3 tools; measure agreement with the LLM teacher; fine-tune on twin labels; compare AUC on cargo, pytest, eslint outputs.
- **#957** [R] _small_ **Atomize runs of one punctuation char as a single atom** — atomize() emits '='*80 as 80 atoms, so rule lines, +----+ borders and |---| rows are scored per character and can be half-deleted; detok then injects spaces (synthetic mask gave '=== === ='). Keep or drop a run whole, optionally capped at 8 chars.
  - Test: Count atoms and encoder tokens spent on punctuation runs in saved hook inputs; diff pytest and table outputs with and without run atoms.
- **#961** [M] _tiny_ **Sweep atom score aggregator: mean, first piece, minimum** — atom_scores averages keep-probability over every subword touching an atom, yet identifiers and URLs split into many pieces while labels were assigned per atom. First-piece or min-piece scores may rank them differently; only selection changes, no retraining.
  - Test: Dump per-token probabilities for val chunks once (#840); compare aggregators on teacher-label F1 and reader QA at equal char rate using frozen scores.
- **#962** [M] **Canonicalise whitespace in scorer input, not in output** — atom_spans rebuilds source indentation for the encoder, so pretty-printed JSON, code and aligned tables spend 510-token windows on whitespace tokens. Score a dedented, single-space form and map back by atom; output still uses source whitespace. Needs matching training inputs.
  - Test: Measure whitespace-only share of encoder tokens and window count on 200 hook-sized inputs; compare keep-decision agreement with the original input on held-out chunks.
- **#993** [X] **Atomizer splits words at combining marks; add grapheme-safe atoms** — Verified read-only: atoms.py makes one atom per mark in Hindi, Thai, Arabic harakat, NFD accents and ZWJ emoji, since \w excludes Mn/Mc. Top-k can delete a mark or base alone, corrupting words and inflating atom counts.
  - Test: Atomize samples from six scripts; assert each mark shares an atom with its base; run random drops and check detok output stays valid.
- **#1012** [M] **kNN datastore of atom states blended into classifier scores** — kNN-LM style (Khandelwal et al. 2020): store last-layer atom hidden states with keep/drop labels from teacher, flags and refetch regrets, then blend a nearest-neighbour vote into the score. A user's own command families adapt instantly without retraining.
  - Test: Export hidden states for 5k labeled atoms; hold out one source, add 50 labeled atoms from it; compare ROC-AUC and QA at 0.5 with and without the kNN blend.
- **#1016** [R] _small_ **Error-seeded personalised PageRank over a line and identifier graph** — Link lines to the rare identifiers they contain (test names, symbols, paths). Seed with error or summary lines such as FAILED tests/x.py::t, run a few power iterations, and give nearby lines a keep floor. Unlike TextRank (#650), centrality is seeded, not global.
  - Test: On 30 pytest, tsc and cargo failures, compare gold-answer line recall at equal chars against the classifier alone and TextRank; confirm runtime stays in the tens of milliseconds.
- **#1046** [R] _tiny_ **Keep mixed-character operators (!=, <=, ->, :=) as single atoms** — Verified: atomize splits '!=' into '!' and '=', and punctuation is never protected, so dropping '!' turns 'a != b' into 'a = b'; '2>&1' and '<=' degrade likewise. #957 only joins runs of one character. Join known operator digraphs into one atom.
  - Test: Mutation test: on assertion, diff and set -x lines, drop each single operator-char atom in turn and count flipped semantics; compare rates before and after joining digraphs.
- **#1047** [R] _tiny_ **Protect backslash and UNC paths from directory-word deletion** — Verified: 'C:\Users\dario\proj\build\out.log' atomizes into C, :, \, Users, dario, proj, build and out.log; only out.log is protected, so the rest can be dropped despite the note's 'paths kept exactly'. Treat backslash as a path separator inside a word.
  - Test: Run MSBuild, dotnet, PowerShell and cmd logs with backslash paths through is_protected; count paths whose directory words are droppable, then re-measure after the atomizer change.
- **#1048** [R] _tiny_ **Shield line-leading diff markers and pass/fail glyphs** — Verified: '+', '-', '✓' and '✗' are single punctuation atoms that is_protected never keeps, and 'OK' is too short to qualify. In compressed PR diffs or test-runner lists, a dropped marker flips added/removed or pass/fail while the line text survives. Force-keep line-initial markers.
  - Test: On compressed gh pr diff and vitest/jest outputs, count surviving lines whose leading +/-/✓/✗ marker was dropped; run a QA 'was line X added or removed' probe.
- **#1052** [R] _tiny_ **Protect high-entropy lowercase IDs that carry no digit** — Verified: is_protected is False for 'kzxqjwmvbtnrplqd' and 'xqzjwvkmbtrplsdf'. Digit-free random tokens (nanoid alphabets, session or quote tokens, lowercase keys) are deletable although the note promises exact IDs. Add a length plus vowel-ratio or character-entropy shield.
  - Test: Generate 1,000 random lowercase tokens of length 8-24 inside log lines; compress at 0.5 with force_protected and count survivors; repeat with the entropy shield.
- **#1106** [M] **Teacher outputs atom-index delete ranges, so rewriting is impossible** — Number atoms in the prompt ([17]word) and have the teacher reply only with ranges to delete, like 3-9,14. No alignment and no variation filter, so non-English chunks the model card says were dropped as rewrites survive; output tokens shrink, cutting teacher cost.
  - Test: On 200 non-English and 200 code chunks, compare variation-filter pass rate and atom agreement of free-text v1 versus index-range output; train both on equal chunks, compare QA at 0.5.
- **#1107** [M] _small_ **SWE-bench test lists as free keep labels for pytest logs** — SWE-style instances carry FAIL_TO_PASS and PASS_TO_PASS test names (SWE-bench; SWE-smith and SWE-rebench reportedly too). In trajectory pytest outputs, atoms of lines naming FAIL_TO_PASS tests and their tracebacks are keep; PASS_TO_PASS pass lines are drop. No teacher needed.
  - Test: Join 100 pytest-bearing SWE-smith tool outputs to instance metadata, label by test name, report failing-test-name and traceback atom recall of the current model at 0.33 and 0.5 versus teacher labels.
- **#1108** [M] **Gold-patch hunks as task-conditioned keep labels for code reads** — SWE trajectories pair file views with the instance's gold patch. Lines in or near patched functions, plus signatures, are keep for that task; distant bodies drop. Gives teacher-free code labels and training signal for pair-input scoring (#166) and goal-hint skimming (#1056).
  - Test: For 100 file views in SWE-smith traces, derive labels from the patch; measure how many patched-line atoms the current model keeps at 0.33 versus a head-truncation baseline of equal size.
- **#1109** [M] **Failing-test coverage slices as deterministic code keep labels** — Run a repo's passing and failing tests under coverage.py; spectrum-based fault-localization scores (Tarantula, Jones and Harrold 2002) rank lines. Suspicious lines plus signatures are keep, never-executed bodies drop. Cheaper than per-statement mutation (#365) and task-shaped.
  - Test: On 15 SWE-smith repos with a failing test, compute Tarantula scores; check fixed lines rank in the top 20%, then measure the current model's keep rate on them at 0.33.
- **#1110** [M] _small_ **Dictionary word segmentation for CJK and Thai atoms** — Verified: atomize() gives one atom per Han or kana character, so deleting one character inside a word garbles it. Atomize with a word segmenter (jieba, BudouX or ICU break iterator) in training and inference so deletions drop whole words. Complements #993.
  - Test: Segment 200 fineweb2 zh/ja chunks; count current model deletions that split a segmenter word; relabel with word atoms, fine-tune, compare zh/ja QA at 0.5.
- **#1111** [M] **Teacher position-bias audit via reversed and shuffled line order** — For order-independent chunks (logs, listings, JSON arrays), relabel with lines reversed and shuffled. Atoms whose keep flips are position noise or lost-in-the-middle bias (Liu et al. 2023). Train on agreement-only or soft labels. Differs from temperature resampling (#361).
  - Test: Relabel 200 log and listing chunks forward and reversed with the same teacher; map lines back, report flip rate by relative position and by duplicate versus unique line.
- **#1112** [M] **Instruction-file corpus with imperative-preserving labels** — No training source resembles AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md or SKILL.md text, which #257, #450 and #1062 want to compress. Mine permissive public ones; the teacher prompt must protect must/never/always/only and conditions. Probe first: how often the current model deletes modal words there.
  - Test: Score 50 public permissively licensed AGENTS.md files at 0.5; count deleted must/never/always/only/not and conditional clauses per file versus a sentence-level BM25 baseline at equal tokens.
- **#1113** [M] **Mine fenced console output blocks from READMEs and docs** — Public trajectory data is reportedly Python-heavy. READMEs and docs show real outputs of cargo, go, helm, terraform, kubectl and npm in console or text fences right after the command block. Extract command and output pairs as a non-Python tool-output source; license-filter by repo.
  - Test: On a 1,000-file markdown sample from a permissive code corpus, extract pairs; report distinct command families and median output length versus SWE-smith.
- **#1114** [M] **Inflate-to-label: LLM inserts filler into dense text** — Reverse the teacher. Give an LLM terse, human-written text (quiet logs, commit messages, API docs) and ask it only to insert verbose filler. The original is a guaranteed subsequence, so labels are exact with no alignment failures. Watch for shortcut learning on LLM-style filler.
  - Test: Inflate 300 terse chunks (subsequence check), fine-tune mixed with real labels, compare held-out real AUC and QA at 0.5 against real-only; first score inserted filler with the current model.
- **#1115** [M] **Score-conditioned needle writer targets the pruner's blind spots** — Show an LLM the chunk with per-atom scores and ask for questions whose answers sit in the lowest-scored decile, quoting verbatim. Quoted low-scored answer spans become hard keep labels. Unlike random question sets (#745), sampling targets where the current checkpoint is weakest.
  - Test: Generate 5 low-score-targeted and 5 random questions for 100 chunks; compare the share of answer spans the model drops at 0.33, then fine-tune on each set and compare QA.
- **#1116** [M] _tiny_ **Mine 'trim' commits for human deletion-only edit labels** — Git history holds human deletion-only edits: commits whose messages say shorten, trim, tighten or drop noise, with hunks that only remove words from docs, comments, logged strings or fixtures. Before/after pairs are natural subsequence labels, with no LLM teacher.
  - Test: git log -p --grep over 20 permissive repos; keep pure word-deletion hunks, hand-check 100 for low-value versus obsolete content, score removed atoms with the current model.
- **#1117** [X] `eval` **Leave-one-source-out ablation to tune the corpus mix** — DEFAULT_MIX fractions in sources.py are hand-set. Train small runs with each source removed or doubled (DoReMi, Xie et al. 2023, learns domain weights with a proxy model). Cut teacher spend on sources whose removal costs nothing; reallocate to weak slices.
  - Test: Using existing labels, train 8 leave-one-source-out runs with fixed steps; report per-source held-out AUC and QA-at-0.5 deltas. Repeat one removal with a second seed to estimate noise.
- **#1118** [R] _small_ **Spend keep rate on unprotected atoms, with a prose floor** — compress() charges force-kept atoms against the budget: once protected share reaches the rate, only protected atoms survive and the 0.95 gate still ships it. Simulated: 74% protected, rate 0.5, 0 of 156 prose words kept. Budget the unprotected remainder or reserve a prose floor.
  - Test: Replay hook events; count outputs where protected share exceeds rate and no unprotected atom survives; compare forced-only, remainder-rate and floor arms on error-line QA and total tokens.
- **#1121** [R] _tiny_ **Shield true, false, null and empty-value literals from deletion** — Verified: is_protected is false for true, false, null, None, yes, no, on, off, up, down and '<none>'; '[]' and '{}' are droppable, and the hook runs keep_structure=False. So '"enabled": false' can lose its value but keep its key. Force-keep literals after ':' or '='.
  - Test: Count these literals dropped right after a kept key across 200 saved MCP JSON, YAML and kubectl outputs at 0.5; add the shield; compare tokens and 'is X enabled' QA.
- **#1137** [R] _tiny_ **Glue path sigils, signs, percent and units to protected atoms** — Verified: atomize('~/proj') gives '~' and '/proj'; '../x' gives '.', '.', '/x'; '+5' splits into '+' and '5', '50%' into '50' and '%'. Only the core is protected, so a dropped sigil, sign or percent changes meaning. Atomize affix plus core as one atom.
  - Test: Mutation test: on 200 saved outputs with relative paths and signed or percent values, drop each lone affix atom and count changed meanings; recount after gluing; QA 'how much slower'.
- **#1138** [L] _tiny_ **Re-glue punctuation, CJK and tabs after deletions in detok** — Verified: detok treats a zero-whitespace dropped atom as a space, so kept punctuation gains one: 'log.txt .', 'Retrying ( 2 .'; Chinese '你好世界很好' became '你 世界 好'. Tabs collapse to spaces. Keep source adjacency unless two word atoms would fuse.
  - Test: Compare Claude token counts of detok output before and after on 100 outputs and 20 CJK files (cl100k showed no change on one sample); assert no word atoms fuse.
- **#1140** [R] _tiny_ **Protect precision: prose compounds like and/or and e.g. cost budget** — Verified: 'e.g' and 'i.e' are protected as dotted atoms; by the same rule 'and/or' counts as a path, and CamelCase brand names like GitHub count as identifiers. They eat budget meant for IDs. Require path or identifier shape. Precision side of #92.
  - Test: Count protected atoms in saved prose outputs that fail a path or ID shape check; relax and compare kept useful words and QA at equal output characters.
- **#1143** [M] **Next-action-invariant mask search as training labels** — CoACT (arXiv 2607.02911, reportedly) compresses observations while preserving the agent's next action. Use that as acceptance: sample masks per recorded result, keep the shortest one where a cheap policy repeats the same next tool call, train on them. Differs from QA-verified #598.
  - Test: On 50 recorded results, sample 8 masks each; Haiku repeats the next call 3 times; report share where the shortest action-safe mask keeps fewer tokens than the uniform 0.5 mask.
- **#1144** [M] **Intent-to-output attention maps from a 0.5B local LM** — Reportedly AttentionRAG-style work scores context by attention from the query tokens. Run a tiny causal LM over command, output and an intent prompt; sum mid-layer attention per line as relevance. Differs from surprisal #260, ablation #266 and encoder-pair #166.
  - Test: On the QA set with gold spans, compare gold-line recall at equal kept tokens against current classifier scores and BM25; time it per 10k chars on CPU.
- **#1192** [L] _tiny_ **Score the short tail window unpadded, not padded to 510** — atom_scores pads every window in a 16-window batch to the widest, so a short remainder window (can be tens of tokens) is computed at full 510 width. Run it as its own call, or bucket widths 64/128/256/512. Input text is unchanged.
  - Test: Count real versus padded tokens on 50 saved outputs; time batched versus tail-separate runs; assert probabilities match within int8 noise (#378).
- **#1193** [L] _tiny_ **Pool windows across documents into shared ORT batches** — compress_documents, the LlamaIndex postprocessor and serve 'texts' call compress() once per text, so each 300-token chunk runs as a batch of one. Add compress_many: atomize all, sort windows by length, fill 16-window batches. Unlike #839 this is one sequential caller.
  - Test: Time 20 retrieved 300-token chunks per-document versus pooled on 4 cores; assert identical kept text within int8 noise; report windows per ORT call.
- **#1194** [M] **Confidence-gated cascade: cheap scorer first, transformer for uncertain windows** — Run a hashed-feature or IDF scorer (#263, #774) over every window; send only windows with atoms in an uncertain band to ORT and reuse cheap decisions elsewhere. The transformer stays authoritative; target is giant boilerplate-heavy logs.
  - Test: On saved logs over 50 KB, measure the share of windows the cheap scorer settles; compare kept-atom overlap with all-ORT scoring at 0.5, plus latency.
- **#1200** [M] **Re-export with fused or banded attention, not eager opset 17** — export_onnx.py forces eager attention, opset 17, dynamo off, so ORT may run dense 512x512 attention even if mmBERT keeps ModernBERT's local-window layers (reportedly). Try SDPA export, ORT transformer-optimizer fusions or the opset 23 Attention op, then re-quantize.
  - Test: Export each variant and rerun the script's parity and ms_per_chunk report; require keep agreement near the current int8 build and lower latency at 512 tokens.
- **#1221** [R] **Project-vocabulary protect list from repo symbols and filenames** — Verified here: is_protected is false for validate, Config, Users, handler, render, timeout and localhost. Plain lowercase or Capitalized names the agent will grep for stay deletable. Build a cached per-repo set (git ls-files basenames, ctags symbols) and force-keep matches.
  - Test: On saved outputs from one repo, count droppable atoms found in the symbol set; compress at 0.5 and count how many are deleted; compare byte cost of keeping them.
- **#1261** [M] _small_ **Repetition-indexed keep schedule per command family** — First sighting of a command family in a session keeps the normal rate; each repeat lowers the keep rate, since the reader already knows the layout. Call-level, unlike atom-level #164 and #200 and refetch-driven #270.
  - Test: Replay recorded sessions with rates 0.5, 0.4, 0.3 for repeats 1, 2, 3+; compare reader QA on later outputs and refetch counts against flat 0.5.
- **#1279** [M] **Learn keep-masks end-to-end through a frozen reader LM** — Relax the keep decision to a Gumbel-sigmoid or HardKuma gate on the input embeddings of a frozen 0.5B reader; train the head to minimise reader next-token loss plus an L0 budget penalty. Rationale-extraction style (Lei 2016; Bastings 2019), gradient-based rather than #598 rejection sampling.
  - Test: Fine-tune on 2k cached rows with a 0.5B reader; compare reader perplexity of kept text and QA at equal chars versus the teacher-label head.
- **#1280** [M] _small_ **Pool atom scores per template slot, column or JSON key** — Mask digits and hex to get line templates, table columns or JSON key paths, then average each slot's atom scores over all members before top-k. Keep/drop becomes consistent per field, so objects share surviving keys. Differs from #164, which only decays repeats.
  - Test: On 30 saved logs, ps/df/docker-ps tables and 20 MCP lists with 100+ objects, count slots or key sets with mixed keep/drop before and after pooling; QA on column-value questions.
- **#1281** ★ [M] _medium_ **Global skeleton pass scores every line before detail windows** — For outputs beyond a few windows, concatenate the first 8 atoms of each line into one long input (ModernBERT-family backbone, reportedly 8k context), score line importance with a global view, then spend detail-window budget per line.
  - Test: On 10 logs over 50 KB, compare kept-line overlap and needle recall of skeleton-then-detail versus independent 512-token windows at 0.5.
- **#1282** [M] **Small cross-window BiGRU over frozen atom embeddings** — Export pooled last-layer atom vectors per window (as in #558), then train a 2-layer BiGRU across the whole output's atom sequence on the existing labels. Gives global context without retraining the encoder or lengthening windows.
  - Test: Cache embeddings for 1k rows over 1000 tokens; compare AUC and top-50% recall on tokens past position 512 versus the encoder alone.
- **#1283** [M] _small_ **Choose keep count from score-distribution elbow or mixture** — Fit a two-component mixture (or Otsu threshold) to each window's logit margins and keep the high component, clamped between rates 0.3 and 0.7, so dense windows keep more and padding-heavy ones less. Uses only model outputs, no redundancy proxy.
  - Test: Check bimodality on 100 saved outputs per type; compare kept fractions and QA at equal total tokens versus constant 0.5.
- **#1284** [M] _small_ **Per-atom-class isotonic calibration before global top-k** — Digits, punctuation, lowercase words, paths and capitalised tokens may sit on different probability scales, so one global sort favors a class. Fit a monotone map per class on validation labels, preserving within-class order. Lighter than the #927 rescorer.
  - Test: Plot reliability curves per class on val rows; compare class mix of kept atoms against the teacher's mix at 0.5, then QA at equal chars.
- **#1285** [M] **Cap near-duplicate templates in the training corpus** — Hash digit-masked line templates per row and downsample rows whose templates exceed a cap, so repetitive build and server logs do not dominate gradients. Works within a source, unlike source-level mix weights (#926, #1117).
  - Test: Count template concentration in the current corpus; retrain a small subset capped versus uncapped and compare val AUC on held-out sources.
- **#1286** [M] _tiny_ **Quantization-aware fine-tune so int8 matches fp32 decisions** — Post-training int8 shifts scores; fine-tune a few hundred steps with fake-quant (torch.ao QAT or ORT QDQ) on the training mix before export. Differs from #836 (calibration) and #929 (weight-only), which stay post-training.
  - Test: On 200 held-out outputs compare Jaccard of kept sets at 0.5 versus fp32 for PTQ int8 and QAT int8, plus val AUC.
- **#1287** [M] _tiny_ **Per-token early freezing inside the encoder (PoWER-BERT style)** — Tokens whose layer-4 probe margin is already extreme skip layers 5 to N and copy their decision; only uncertain tokens continue. Adapted from PoWER-BERT (Goyal et al., 2020) and Learned Token Pruning (Kim et al., 2022). Differs from whole-model exit #561.
  - Test: Fit layer probes on cached hidden states; measure decision agreement and compute share saved at margin cutoffs on 100 outputs; prototype only if agreement exceeds 99%.
- **#1312** [X] `eval` **Aligner ground-truth test with known deletions; line-aware duplicate ties** — _lcs_pairs matches the earliest equal atom, so when a repeated atom (comma, quote, 'the') is deleted later, the keep label lands on an earlier copy. A quick local run, random 50% deletions on prose, mislabeled about 2% of atoms (partly irreducible).
  - Test: Delete known masks from corpus chunks, run align(), report mismatch by atom frequency; try same-line-first tie-breaking and compare mismatch plus student AUC after a short retrain.
- **#1337** [R] _small_ **Deterministic wordy-phrase table for README, issue and docs prose** — Replace multi-word padding phrases: 'in order to' to 'to', 'due to the fact that' to 'because', 'a large number of' to 'many'; drop 'it is important to note that'. Single-word swaps like utilize gain nothing. Measured 2-5 o200k tokens saved per phrase hit.
  - Test: Count phrase hits per 1k tokens across 30 READMEs, issue bodies and doc pages fetched via MCP or WebFetch; apply the table; compare count_tokens; run reader QA before and after.
- **#1364** [R] _small_ **Concept-coverage greedy line selection, classifier-free extractive summariser** — Gillick and Favre (2009) and Lin and Bilmes (2011) summarise by greedily maximising idf-weighted coverage of digit-masked word bigrams per character cost, with diminishing returns. A knapsack selector for repetitive logs and docs; unlike MMR-decayed classifier scores (#164) or centrality (#650).
  - Test: On 40 saved logs with planted needle lines, compare needle recall and QA at equal chars against the classifier at 0.5, TextRank and head/tail; report milliseconds per 10k chars.
- **#1366** [M] **Maintainer reply-quote labels from GitHub issues with pasted logs** — In public issues, fenced log or error blocks followed by replies that quote lines give human-chosen relevance labels, independent of the LLM teacher. Quoted lines become keep labels. Different from LogChunks CI spans (#749) and agent-derived labels (#169). Check licences first.
  - Test: Sample 300 issues with fenced blocks over 1,000 chars; count replies quoting a line, then compare classifier line recall on quoted versus random lines at 0.5.
- **#1367** [M] **Notebook cell outputs labelled by values reused in later cells** — Public permissively licensed notebooks pair code with real stdout, tracebacks and DataFrame prints. Atoms an author later retypes in code or markdown become keep labels; never-reused spans become drop evidence. A data source with natural command-output pairs, outside the agent-trajectory sets.
  - Test: Sample 500 notebook outputs over 2,000 chars; report share with reused spans, and classifier recall of reused atoms at 0.5 versus length-matched random atoms. Check dataset licences.
- **#1368** [M] _small_ **Universal line-drop decision list distilled from classifier line decisions** — Fit an interpretable rule list or shallow tree (RIPPER, Cohen 1995) on line-shape features: digit share, indent, first-token class, severity word, repeat count, position. Labels are classifier line-drop rates across all families. Model-free fallback for unseen commands, unlike per-family tables (#261, #1013).
  - Test: Leave one command family out per fold; report rule precision on needle lines, chars removed, and agreement with classifier line drops, against the classifier at 0.5 and the family table.
- **#1369** [M] _small_ **Column-name-annotated scorer input for tables, CSV and logfmt** — Build a virtual twin of a table where each cell is prefixed by its column header (status=Running), score that, and map scores back to the original atoms; output text stays untouched, so bare cells carry meaning. Differs from #557, #962, #1280.
  - Test: On 30 docker ps, kubectl get, ps and CSV outputs, compare column-value QA and per-column keep consistency at 0.5, annotated versus plain scoring, zero-shot and after a short fine-tune.
- **#1370** [R] _tiny_ **Keyphrase sketch inside elision markers for deleted line runs** — Where whole lines vanish, insert '[-37 lines: ECONNRESET, retry, 3 hosts]' listing the top idf-weighted terms of the deleted block. The agent can judge relevance and grep an exact term. Beyond counts-only markers (#269) and column stats on row folds (#1321).
  - Test: Ask questions about deleted facts on 30 logs: compare refetch rate, confident-wrong rate and total tokens for no marker, count-only and keyphrase markers at equal marker budget.
- **#1371** [M] _small_ **Clause and parenthetical units for prose deletion, not atoms** — For READMEs, issue and PR text, delete whole syntactic constituents (parentheticals, appositives, trailing clauses, per Filippova and Strube 2008 dependency-tree compression) ranked by mean atom score. Avoids ungrammatical splices and flipped negations. Lightweight option: punctuation-delimited clauses, no parser.
  - Test: On 50 issue and PR bodies, compare splice-error rate (LLM judge) and QA at equal tokens: atom deletion versus clause units versus spaCy-subtree units; note latency.
- **#1373** [M] **Reader-cited spans from Claude's Citations feature as keep labels** — Feed a tool output as a document with citations enabled in the Anthropic API plus a real question; the character ranges the reader cites are production-reader attribution labels. Differs from small-LM ablation (#266), later-quote behaviour (#169) and teacher-written deletions.
  - Test: Verify that cited ranges map back to character offsets. On 100 contexts with questions, collect cited spans and check classifier survival at 0.5 versus gold-span survival; record API cost.
- **#1383** [M] `eval` **Break-even size above which a small-model digest beats pruning** — A cheap model reading raw output costs once at input price plus digest output; pruning leaves about half the tokens to be re-read every turn. Solve for the size and survival turns where an opt-in query-conditioned digest wins, keeping the saved original.
  - Test: Price both paths from the published price table over 20k to 200k chars and 5 to 50 surviving turns; mark where digest wins and the share of real outputs there.
- **#1397** [X] _tiny_ **Atoms no subword touches default to score 0.0, dropped first** — compress.py gives atoms with zero token hits a score of 0.0, so characters the tokenizer normalises away or emits no offsets for always rank last and are deleted first, even when meaningful. Unverified for the real tokenizer. A fail-safe default would keep them.
  - Test: Run atom_scores over saved real outputs; list atoms with no token touching them and their characters; check whether pass/fail glyphs or box rails land there; try default 1.0.
- **#1399** [M] **Contrastive conditional perplexity: command-conditioned small-LM atom scoring** — LongLLMLingua (Jiang et al., 2023) scores tokens by how much prepending the question lowers perplexity. Run a ~135M causal LM twice, with and without the Bash command plus description as question; keep atoms the command makes likelier. Unlike #260 and #1144.
  - Test: On 30 recorded outputs, compute the log-ratio score on CPU; compare kept-line recall for lines later quoted in Edit or Grep versus the classifier at equal tokens; log seconds.

## Reversible substitution (legend)

- **#100** [L] **Path aliasing** — Repeated long prefixes become $P1 plus a legend line; also make paths relative to the cwd carried by the hook event.
- **#101** [R] **UUID and hash shortening** — 40-char SHA to 12; test whether the agent can use shortened ones in follow-up commands.
- **#102** [L] **Phrase table for repeated long strings** — e.g. com.company.service.impl.; applied only when net-positive in real tokens.
- **#103** [X] `eval` **Legend comprehension test** — Does Claude resolve $P1 correctly in later edits? Measure the wrong-path rate.
- **#315** [L] _small_ **Cloud resource-ID grammar: hoist ARN, Azure, GCP prefixes** — Grammar-aware aliasing of ARN partition/region/account, Azure /subscriptions/GUID/resourceGroups/RG/providers prefixes and GCP googleapis.com/compute/v1/projects/P URLs, with one legend line. Synthetic: GCP URL 29 to 13 tokens, Azure ID 40 to 16.
  - Test: Replay aws, az and gcloud JSON outputs; count repeated prefixes; alias only when net-positive in tokens; have an agent write follow-up commands using the shortened tails.
- **#476** [L] _small_ **Shorten GitHub URLs to org/repo#N and sha7 shorthand** — Release notes, issue bodies, gh pr checks and git merge text repeat https://github.com/org/repo/pull/N and /actions/runs/ID/job/ID. Rewrite to #N, org/repo#N or id pairs plus one legend line naming the base repo. Synthetic 10 release-note lines: 250 to 130 o200k tokens.
  - Test: Run the rewrite on 20 release bodies, gh pr checks tables and issue threads; assert regex round-trip restores every URL; ask which PR fixed X versus raw.
- **#784** [L] _small_ **Session-wide alias glossary shared across tool outputs** — #100 and #102 put a legend in every output. Instead, mine long strings repeated across a session (package prefixes, URLs, absolute paths), announce a glossary once via additionalContext, re-announce after compaction, and alias later outputs. Risk: wrong alias resolution in Edit paths.
  - Test: In transcripts, list strings over 25 chars repeated in 5 or more tool results; sum their tokens minus glossary cost; run 10 edit tasks with aliases and count mis-resolved paths.
- **#1034** [L] **Proxy aliases opaque toolu_ ids to short stable names** — Each tool_use id and its matching tool_use_id is a long random string; a sample costs about 19 o200k tokens (not Claude's tokenizer). If the API renders them, a deterministic per-session alias like t17 applied across history saves tokens on every request.
  - Test: Verify that aliasing is accepted: count_tokens on one recorded 60-call history with real ids versus t1..t60; if counts match, drop the idea; else replay with prefix-stable aliasing.
- **#1152** [R] _small_ **Alias toolchain-owned path roots and hashed directories** — Fixed per-ecosystem prefixes: /rustc/<40hex>/library, GOROOT, JDK, Xcode SDK, DerivedData/<Proj>-<hash>, Gradle files-2.1/.../<sha1>/, cargo target/debug/deps/name-<16hex>. Synthetic: SDK path 44 to 19 tokens, Gradle cache path 79 to 29. A legend maps aliases back.
  - Test: Collect failing builds from five toolchains; apply a prefix table; measure token drop and whether the agent can still open the aliased file.
- **#1237** [L] _small_ **Cross-document label legend for helm template and kustomize** — Each rendered resource repeats the same helm.sh/chart and app.kubernetes.io/* label set in metadata, selector and pod template. YAML anchors (#792) cannot cross --- boundaries, so emit a one-time legend 'L1={...}' and reference it per document. Reversible.
  - Test: Run helm template on 3 popular charts with 10-30 resources; count label-line tokens, then ask a reader which resources carry a given label from the legended form.
- **#1341** [X] _tiny_ `eval` **Alias-label token ladder: single words versus sigil plus digit** — Alias labels cost differently: o200k proxy ' Alpha' 1 token, ' $1' 2, ' [1]' 3, ' <A>' 3, '{{p1}}' 4. A 3-token label can erase the gain from shortening a path. Rank label styles by tokens and reader accuracy before building aliasing (#100, #784).
  - Test: Run count_tokens on 30 label styles in context ' X/file.py'; pick the cheapest 3; test whether Claude resolves each back through the legend on 20 path-edit prompts.
- **#1358** [L] _small_ `MCP` **Round-trip aliases for long opaque tokens in MCP results** — Signed quote tokens can reach 2,048 chars; a 400-char random base64 token cost 263 o200k tokens against 2 for an alias, paid in the result, echoed call argument and history. A hook aliases it; PreToolUse updatedInput expands it. Unlike #101, covers the call round trip.
  - Test: Capture get_quote results, tokenise the tokens, prototype alias plus expansion against a stub server (never real place_order), run 10 quote-to-booking flows checking the server receives the identical token.

## Adjacent surfaces

- **#104** [X] `eval, MCP` **Static-context audit** — Tokens in the system prompt, CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md, skill list and MCP tool schemas; a per-turn tax.
- **#105** [R] `MCP` **MCP tool-schema minifier** — Strip examples and redundant parameter descriptions through a wrapper proxy; check tool-call accuracy is unchanged.
- **#108** [R] **Pasted blobs** — Check whether UserPromptSubmit can rewrite a prompt; if not, a ctxprune paste clipboard filter.
- **#159** [X] _small_ `eval` **Edit anchors: measure shared old and new context** — Edit tool_use inputs stay in context and cost output tokens. Measure shared prefix/suffix lines between old_string and new_string, and over-long anchors, in transcripts; if material, add a smallest-unique-span steering line.
  - Test: Parse Edit calls in transcript JSONL; compute the fraction of old_string tokens identical to new_string edges; report distribution by file type.
- **#248** [R] _small_ `MCP` **Strip generator boilerplate from MCP schemas and ToolSearch results** — pydantic/FastMCP and zod emit per-property title, default null, anyOf [T, null], $schema and additionalProperties:false. A toy two-tool schema went 273 to 143 tokens (o200k proxy). Strip in a tools/list proxy, and in ToolSearch results if PostToolUse may rewrite them.
  - Test: Verify that a PostToolUse rewrite of ToolSearch output is honored; strip tools/list dumps from installed servers, tokenise before/after; run 30 tool-call prompts comparing argument validity and tool choice.
- **#257** [M] _small_ `MCP` **Compress tool descriptions and server instructions once, offline** — Run the token classifier at install time on MCP descriptions and the initialize instructions field (in the system prompt every turn; several servers here inject long blocks), protect parameter names and enums, cache the result in a proxy.
  - Test: Compress github tools/list descriptions at keep 0.6 with protection; have the model choose tools and fill arguments for 50 prompts; compare accuracy with the originals and count tokens saved.
- **#286** [R] _small_ **Dedupe repeated client-injected system-reminder blocks in history** — Claude Code attaches system-reminder text (todo nudges, file-changed notices, IDE hints) to turns, and it persists in history. Proxy hashes blocks, keeps the first and rewrites repeats at first sight, so the cached prefix stays byte-stable.
  - Test: Log a 50-turn session through a passthrough proxy; hash every system-reminder block; report repeat counts and token share before writing any rewrite.
- **#307** [X] _small_ `eval` **Measure repeated failed-then-fixed commands across sessions** — Agent-memory prior art (Reflexion, Voyager skill library, Anthropic's memory tool) stores lessons across runs. Here: if the same repo repeatedly sees pytest fail then 'uv run pytest' work, a SessionStart note of working commands would skip that discovery.
  - Test: Scan transcripts per repo: find nonzero-exit Bash calls followed by a different successful invocation; count recurrences across sessions and tokens spent on the failed attempts and their output.
- **#635** [R] _small_ `MCP` **Strip redundant parameter descriptions: sibling duplicates and restated limits** — GitHub schemas repeat 'Repository owner', 'Repository name' and perPage text in every tool; perPage reads '(min 1, max 100)' beside minimum:1 and maximum:100. In a tools/list proxy keep the first copy, leave later exact duplicates bare, and strip clauses restating schema keywords.
  - Test: Dump tools/list for 3 large servers; measure token share of duplicate and restating text; assert stripped numbers equal schema keywords; run 30 tool-call prompts comparing argument validity.
- **#636** [R] _small_ `MCP` **Lazy-load MCP server instructions on first tool call** — initialize.instructions blocks (four in this session) sit in the system prompt every turn, used or not. A proxy blanks the field and prepends the text to a server's first tools/call result per session; dormant servers cost nothing.
  - Test: Count instruction tokens per connected server; with a proxy deferring them, run each documented flow as a dry run and check required order and safety rules still hold.
- **#637** [R] _tiny_ `MCP` **Strip x-mcp-header and other schema extension keys** — ToolSearch output shows 'x-mcp-header' on owner and repo of GitHub tools: apparently transport metadata the model cannot use. Strip all x-* keys. Also canary-test whether outputSchema, annotations, title, icons and _meta reach the prompt. Extends #248.
  - Test: Count x-* keys across installed tools/list and tokenise them; add canaries in a toy server's outputSchema, annotations and icons, then grep a logged request for them.
- **#638** [R] `MCP` **Collapse long enum lists in tool schemas to a pattern** — Some servers embed enums of dozens of values (timezones, currencies, locales). Replace any enum over N values with a type plus a one-line hint and a pointer to the saved full list; the server still validates.
  - Test: Scan installed tools/list for enums over 20 values, tokenise their share of schema size, then check the model still picks valid values on 20 prompts.
- **#642** [R] `MCP` **Hide write tools by MCP annotations in read-only sessions** — Spec tool annotations (readOnlyHint, destructiveHint) let a generic proxy or settings rule hide every non-read-only tool of any server for review, research or Explore subagents. Differs from hand-written allowlists (#253): works on servers never seen before.
  - Test: Dump annotations from installed servers; count tools marked readOnlyHint true and schema tokens of the rest; run a read-only task with the filtered list and confirm it completes.
- **#643** [R] `MCP` **Detect duplicate MCP servers across scopes and plugins** — The same server can load twice: user config, project .mcp.json, a bundled plugin and a claude.ai connector, each under another prefix. Hash tools/list descriptions per server, flag near-identical pairs, and drop one copy of every schema and instruction block.
  - Test: Script over claude mcp list, plugin manifests and the deferred tool-name list: group tools by normalised name and description hash; report servers with Jaccard above 0.8 and their schema tokens.
- **#859** [R] _small_ `MCP` **Zero-schema gh extensions for CI digest and PR card** — gh alias set --shell or a gh extension adds ci-digest and pr-card commands: failing step, annotations, unresolved threads, failing checks in one call. Costs one CLAUDE.md line instead of an MCP schema each turn, and output passes the Bash hook. Delivery variant of #485.
  - Test: Count tokens of the alias line versus the #485 tool's schema; run 10 PR-review tasks with raw gh versus the aliases; compare turns and tokens.
- **#906** [R] _small_ **Strip Agent report passages copied from the prompt** — Subagent reports often restate the task, file lists or code the parent wrote into the Agent prompt. PostToolUse on Agent compares report lines of 40+ characters against tool_input.prompt and replaces verbatim matches with a one-line marker.
  - Test: Over transcripts, for each Agent result compute the share of lines of 40+ characters that appear verbatim in the prompt; report tokens per call.
- **#1002** [R] _small_ `MCP` **Bind session-constant MCP params and hide them from schemas** — owner/repo and similar cloudId, project_id or org-slug params repeat on every call of GitHub-style servers. Bind from git remote at launch, drop from schemas, fill via PreToolUse updatedInput. Saves schema, output and history tokens; in github list_issues each costs 25 o200k tokens.
  - Test: Count owner/repo/id tokens in recorded tool_use inputs and schemas across 3 servers; prototype fill-in with updatedInput; run 10 tasks checking calls still hit the right repo.
- **#1003** [R] _small_ `MCP` **Drop never-used optional parameters from MCP tool schemas** — Tally which optional params appear in tool_use inputs across transcripts; a tools/list proxy removes never-used ones. In github list_issues, field_filters alone is 175 of 649 schema tokens (o200k proxy, one schema). Usage-driven, unlike #253's tool-level allowlists.
  - Test: Count param keys per tool in transcripts, tokenise the never-used share on 3 servers, then rerun 20 tasks and count failures needing a removed param.
- **#1006** [R] _small_ `MCP` **Compact ToolSearch schemas: minify separators, then TypeScript signatures** — ToolSearch returns JSON schemas with a space after every colon and comma; compact separators are lossless (toy schema 91 vs 68 o200k tokens). Going further, render TypeScript signatures with prose kept as comments: github list_issues 649 vs 440 (one schema).
  - Test: Verify that PostToolUse may rewrite ToolSearch output; dump 20 real schemas, tokenise JSON, minified and signature forms, run 30 prompts comparing argument validity and tool choice.
- **#1007** [R] _small_ `MCP` **Defer per-tool description tails to the first call result** — Keep one sentence of each MCP tool description in the schema; a proxy appends the rest (usage notes, cautions) to the first result of that tool per session. Differs from server-instructions deferral and offline compression: per-tool lifecycle.
  - Test: Tokenise description text beyond the first sentence on 3 servers; run 30 tool-choice and argument prompts, flagging errors the tail would have prevented.
- **#1008** [R] _small_ `MCP` **Hide MCP tools that duplicate built-in Read, Write, Grep, Bash** — Filesystem, shell and git-style MCP servers expose read_file, write_file, search_files, list_directory, run_command, which overlap built-ins. Each costs a schema and a roster name for no new capability. Deny them per server (overlap criterion, unlike usage-based #253).
  - Test: List installed MCP tools, classify overlap with built-ins by description, tokenise their schemas, run 5 file tasks with them hidden.
- **#1035** [L] _tiny_ **Merge adjacent text blocks inside one request message** — Claude Code user turns usually carry a tool_result plus separate system-reminder text blocks. Each block boundary may cost framing tokens. A proxy joins adjacent text blocks with a newline wherever no cache breakpoint sits between them.
  - Test: count_tokens on a 30-turn history with blocks split versus joined; report tokens saved per merged boundary. If zero, close it; else check byte-stable prefixes.
- **#1356** [R] _small_ `MCP` **Hoist repeated subschemas in tool schemas into $defs** — Jet Cab request_booking repeats its entire ride-field schema inside additionalRides.items, enums and patterns included. Hoist nested, cross-tool and template-family duplicates into one $defs entry referenced by $ref in a tools/list proxy. Unlike #635, this targets structure, not description text.
  - Test: Dump both booking servers' schemas, tokenise nested duplicate subtrees, rewrite with $defs, run 20 booking prompts including multi-ride, and compare argument validity and tool choice.
- **#1357** [R] _small_ **Validation-keyword diet with error-triggered constraint restore** — Drop pattern, min/maxLength, minimum/maximum, uniqueItems and zero-value defaults that servers re-check; booking schemas repeat the HH:MM regex several times. A synthetic email regex cost 65 o200k tokens versus 5 for format:email. A proxy re-adds the violated constraint to validation errors.
  - Test: Count validation-keyword tokens across 3 installed servers, strip them, send 30 boundary-value prompts (overlong note, bad time) and count calls where the error text lets the model self-correct.
- **#1361** [R] _small_ **Hide tools the credential or account type cannot call** — Org-only, scope-gated or feature-gated tools (teams, issue types and fields, secret scanning, Actions) still cost a schema for a personal repo with a narrow token. Probe get_me and token scopes at launch; deny what cannot succeed. GitHub's server reportedly filters by PAT scope already.
  - Test: List GitHub tools, map each to required scope or org-only, tokenise the unreachable schemas, then call each read-only tool once on a personal repo and count 403 or 404 results.
- **#1362** [R] _tiny_ **Sentence-level dedupe across server instructions, tool and parameter text** — The booking servers state consent rules in instructions, the request_booking description, and the quoteToken and acceptedTerms text. Keep one canonical copy of each sentence per server, with consent guards pinned at the call site; drop paraphrased repeats. Rule counterpart of census #1271.
  - Test: Shingle-overlap three servers' instructions plus schemas, remove repeats, then run 20 prompts where the customer has not approved the total and check the model still refuses to book.
- **#1363** [M] `MCP` **Delta-debug tool schemas against a generated tool-choice suite** — Run ddmin (Zeller's delta debugging) over a tool's description sentences, properties and keywords: delete a span, rerun the #644 prompt suite with a cheap model, keep deletions that change no tool choice or argument. Yields the smallest passing schema per tool instead of hand-written rules.
  - Test: Apply to one 15-parameter tool with 20 generated prompts; report minimal schema tokens and which deleted spans a larger held-out prompt set flags as regressions.

## Safety nets

- **#112** [X] `eval` **Property tests for every lossless rule** — Round-trip or parse-equality, idempotent, never grows output (hypothesis fuzzing).
- **#113** [X] `eval` **Golden corpus** — Per-rule snapshot diffs from real captured outputs.
- **#114** [X] `eval` **Rule-interaction tests** — Ordering and double application.
- **#115** [X] `eval` **Hook latency budget** — Enforce p99 under a fixed limit.
- **#116** [X] `eval` **Edit-safety simulation** — Copy a line from processed output into an Edit old_string and check it matches the real file.
- **#117** [X] **Saved-originals hygiene** — Disk growth, TTL cleanup, secret redaction at save time.
- **#182** [X] `eval` **Audit deleted negators and spliced adjacencies** — Kept atoms are rejoined, so deleting 'not', 'no', 'never', 'without', 'failed' or a line break can flip or fuse meaning. Count lines where a negator was dropped but the rest survived, and kept bigrams absent from the original.
  - Test: Run held-out chunks at keep 0.5; list dropped-negator lines and cross-line splices; hand-judge 50 for flipped meaning; if material, add a negator shield.
- **#183** [X] `eval` **Reader-parity QA for every lossless format transform** — Round-trip tests prove a parser recovers the data, not that an LLM reads it equally well. Columnar JSON, tree listings, grouped grep, path aliases and padding strip may shift accuracy. Run identical questions on original and transformed output; require non-inferiority before enabling.
  - Test: Per transform, 30 real outputs x 5 questions with scripted gold; paired comparison with a 2-point non-inferiority margin, using the Claude tier users actually run.
- **#184** [X] `eval` **Cross-tool false-fire matrix and version-drift corpus for noise packs** — Run every tool-specific rule on every other tool's output, prose and source code; any rewrite is a false positive. Also capture the same tool at older and newer versions (pytest, npm, cargo) to catch rules that silently stop matching or over-match.
  - Test: CI job: N packs x M corpora, assert off-target output is byte-identical; weekly matrix over two tool versions reporting per-rule fire-rate change.
- **#195** [X] _tiny_ **Exempt recovery greps of saved originals from compression** — Claude is told to grep ~/.cache/ctxprune/outputs/*.txt, but that command is not in the skip regex. A result over 6000 chars is pruned again, deleting what it just asked for and creating a second saved file plus note.
  - Test: In a hooked session, grep a saved original with many hits; confirm a second note appears. Add a path exemption and recheck.
- **#292** [X] _tiny_ **Verbatim-skip regex misses wrapped source-printing commands** — Ran VERBATIM_CMD: it skips 'cat a.py' and 'git diff' but still compresses 'sudo cat', 'docker exec c cat', 'env X=1 cat', 'timeout 5 cat', 'git -C repo diff', 'git --no-pager diff', 'gh pr diff', 'diff -u', 'tail -n', 'rg -n'.
  - Test: Build a 60-command fuzz corpus of wrappers and flag orders around source-printing commands; assert skip. Prototype a shlex stage parser and compare false-skip rate on captured Bash events.
- **#294** [X] _tiny_ **Verify hook output is an ordered subsequence of the input** — _compress writes whatever any HTTP service on port 8719 returns (stale serve, other software, local attacker) into the tool output. Require kept atoms to be an ordered subsequence of the original, protected atoms present and length shrinking; else fail open.
  - Test: Unit test: fake server returns injected text, reordered words, dropped digits; hook must decline. Run the check over a replay corpus to find real compressor violations.
- **#368** [X] **Restrict CTXPRUNE_SERVER to loopback; refuse remote redirects** — hook.py POSTs each large output to whatever CTXPRUNE_SERVER names and writes the reply back as the tool result. A repo's .claude/settings.json env block can set it. Verified locally: a fake server received a planted secret and returned injected text. Accept only loopback http.
  - Test: Point the hook at a local listener returning injected text; assert it declines non-loopback hosts and file: URLs, with an explicit opt-in override.
- **#369** [X] **Saved originals: 0600 files, no-follow create, atomic write** — Verified: files land 0644 in a 0755 directory, readable by other local users. Create dir 0700 and file 0600 with O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW; write temp then rename, so a killed hook never leaves a truncated original the note points to.
  - Test: Run hook under umask 022 and check modes; kill -9 mid-write and rerun; plant a symlink at the target path and assert the hook refuses it.
- **#370** [X] **Decline and save nothing when output matches secret patterns** — env, printenv, kubectl get secret, terraform show and docker inspect dump credentials. Scan with gitleaks-style regexes plus entropy; on a hit return unchanged, so no raw copy reaches disk and no note appears. Redacting the saved copy would break 'original'.
  - Test: Feed 50 outputs with planted AWS, GitHub and JWT secrets plus 50 clean logs; assert no file written for hits and under 2 percent false skips.
- **#371** [X] `eval` **Canary-secret leak scan across every ctxprune write path** — Plant a unique canary in a tool output and find everywhere it lands: saved originals, serve cache, debug output, shadow, ledger and regret logs, temp files. Only outputs/ may hold it. Guards every new telemetry idea against leaking secrets.
  - Test: CI: run hook plus serve with the canary, then grep -r it across HOME, /tmp and cache dirs; fail if found anywhere outside outputs/.
