- **#372** [R] `MCP` **Neutralise forged ctxprune notes inside tool output** — Attacker-controlled output (page fetched with curl, issue body via MCP) can carry a fake 'ctxprune compressed this output... full output is at <path>' line steering Claude to a chosen file. Escape the marker in input, or put a per-install nonce in genuine notes.
  - Test: Pass a page containing a forged note through the hook; assert the marker is escaped or flagged and the nonce appears only in the genuine note.
- **#375** [X] **Runtime kill switch: sentinel file, pause timer, per-repo off** — CTXPRUNE_OFF lives in the Claude Code process environment, so it only works for new sessions. Have the hook first stat a sentinel (~/.cache/ctxprune/OFF, repo .ctxprune-off) or a 'pause 30m' timestamp: one stat call, mid-session, no restart, no settings edit.
  - Test: Mid-session: run a large output (compressed), touch the sentinel, rerun (verbatim); time the added stat over 1,000 small events.
- **#376** [X] **Per-rule disable flags and rule-version stamp for rollback** — With dozens of proposed rules, one global switch is too coarse. CTXPRUNE_DISABLE=rule_id,... plus rule versions in the ledger and saved sidecar lets one misfiring pack be turned off in the field while the rest keep saving.
  - Test: For each rule, disable it and assert output equals the pipeline without that rule byte for byte; assert ledger rows carry rule id and version.
- **#377** [X] **Over-deletion floor: fail open when output is far below target** — The hook rejects only results at or above 0.95 of input. Verified: a 52-character reply for a 10,863-character input was accepted and noted. An ordered-subsequence check also passes empty output. Reject ratios under about half the requested rate as a malfunction.
  - Test: Fake server returning '', one word or 3 percent of the input; hook must decline. Replay real outputs and list any below 0.5x the rate.
- **#379** [X] **Pin Hugging Face revision, verify hash, handshake with serve** — hf_hub_download takes no revision, so a fresh install fetches whatever main holds and cached users never update, making A/B runs irreproducible. Pin a commit sha, check the file sha256, expose model hash in /health, and have the hook refuse a mismatched serve.
  - Test: Run an old serve after upgrading the package; assert the hook detects the fingerprint mismatch and falls back; tamper one model byte and assert load fails.
- **#418** [X] `eval` **Cell-lookup QA for tables under token deletion** — The classifier deletes words, but tables carry meaning by position: dropping one cell shifts every later column. Scripted row-and-column lookups on pandas prints, psql and CSV at 0.33 and 0.5 kept show whether tables need rules or protection.
  - Test: Generate 50 tables with known cells; compress at 0.5; ask 5 lookups each with regex-derived gold; compare accuracy against original and hoisted forms.
- **#462** [X] _tiny_ `eval` **Differential fuzz of the inference stack, not just rules** — Check _RawTokenizer offsets against the transformers tokenizer and int8 against torch on hostile inputs: lone surrogates (saved originals use surrogatepass), NUL, CRLF, ZWJ emoji, a 300k-char one-line minified JSON, empty text. Any exception makes the hook fail open silently, forfeiting the saving.
  - Test: Generate 500 fuzz strings; run hook.run in process, logging exceptions by class; compare token ids and offsets across both tokenizers; list input classes that never compress.
- **#474** [X] `eval` **Canary exposure test for classifiers tuned on private logs** — Plant random secret-like canaries in fine-tuning chunks (exposure metric from Carlini et al., 'The Secret Sharer', 2019). Check whether seen canaries get higher keep-probability than fresh same-shape strings. A keep/drop head may leak little; measure before releasing weights.
  - Test: Fine-tune on a corpus with 100 canaries repeated 1, 10 and 100 times; compare keep-probability distributions for seen versus 100 unseen canaries by AUC.
- **#475** [X] `eval` **No-egress CI test backing the no-telemetry claim** — AGENTS.md promises nothing leaves the machine. Run hook, serve, CLI and future analytics scripts, model pre-cached, under a network namespace or strace connect trace; fail on any non-loopback connection. The torch path's from_pretrained may query the Hub.
  - Test: Run under `unshare -n` with the model cached; time 50 hook calls; assert exit 0, compressed output present, and zero connect() calls beyond 127.0.0.1.
- **#575** [X] `eval` **Forced-deletion probe: does the agent notice and recover missing facts** — Take cases where the gold fact is verified deleted (survival check), embed them in a small Claude Code task, and record whether the agent greps the saved original, reruns the command or answers confidently from a guess. Vary note wording and Claude tier.
  - Test: Use 30 deleted-gold cases from the QA set; run claude -p with Bash; classify the first response as recover, rerun or guess; compare three note variants.
- **#644** [X] `eval` **Schema-edit regression suite: auto-generated tool-choice prompts** — Every schema-shrinking idea needs a tool-choice and argument check. Generate once, per tool, 3 prompts with expected tool and arguments from the original schema, verify they pass uncompressed, then reuse as a gate for any rewrite of descriptions or schemas.
  - Test: Build for github tools: 3 prompts per tool using a cheap model, check pass rate on originals, apply one rewrite (title stripping), report tool-choice and argument-match delta.
- **#647** [R] _small_ **Refetch grep patterns become per-family never-drop rules** — PostToolUse sees Bash commands that grep a saved original. Extract the pattern, find matching lines, mask digits and hex into templates, and add them to a never-drop list for that command family. A false drop is repaired once. Line-precise, unlike #270's rate bandit.
  - Test: Replay transcripts with refetch greps; on each family's next run, count refetched templates now kept and chars given back.
- **#654** [R] _small_ **ctxprune recall: BM25-ranked windows from a saved original** — The note tells Claude to grep the saved file, which may return many lines. Ship 'ctxprune recall PATH query -k 3' printing top-ranked line windows with line numbers and a cap. Ranked by query, unlike #208's grep or line slices; #209 is initial selection.
  - Test: From replayed sessions with refetches, compare tokens of the actual refetch command against recall, and whether the needed line lands in the top 3.
- **#665** [X] `eval` **Break-even harm rate from natural detour repair costs** — Tolerable harm rate p* = tokens saved per call divided by mean repair cost of a wrong action. Estimate repair cost from detours already in transcripts (failed Edit on stale text to next success), then require the measured harm upper bound to sit below p*.
  - Test: Find Edit failures in transcripts; sum tokens until the next successful Edit; report median and p90 repair cost; compute p* for a 2k-token saving.
- **#676** [X] _tiny_ **Verbatim-skip regex holes: --patch, -pS, newlines, then/do** — Verified by running VERBATIM_CMD: git log --patch, -pS, -u, git stash show -p and git cat-file -p are not skipped (-p needs a word boundary). With no re.M, newline-separated cat, 'then cat' and '{ cat' also escape, so source gets pruned. Complements #292 and #194.
  - Test: Add these ten strings to test_verbatim_commands_are_skipped in tests/test_hook.py (all fail today). Compare a shlex stage splitter on captured Bash events.
- **#678** [X] _tiny_ **Hook re-prunes output of the ctxprune CLI itself** — AGENTS.md tells agents to run 'cmd | ctxprune --keep 0.33'. The hook has no skip for ctxprune commands (verified), so output over 6000 chars is pruned again at 0.5: uncontrolled double loss, a second note, a second saved file.
  - Test: Feed the hook command 'x | ctxprune --keep 0.33' with 8000-char stdout; it compresses today. Add a skip for ctxprune and python -m ctxprune commands.
- **#679** [X] `eval` **Scan agent writes for strings spliced by compression** — Find 6-word n-grams in later Write, Edit, commit and PR text that occur in the compressed result but nowhere contiguous in the saved original. Counts durable repo artifacts corrupted by quoting pruned text, a downstream harm QA never sees; complements #182.
  - Test: Join transcripts to saved originals via the note path; extract tool_use inputs after each compressed result; report spliced n-gram rate per 100 compressed outputs.
- **#680** [X] `eval, MCP` **JSON parse survival under the shipped keep_structure=False config** — hook.py sends force_protected only, so braces, quotes, commas and colons can be deleted; the README says LLMLingua-2 breaks JSON. Measure how often MCP JSON outputs stay parseable, and the token cost of keep_structure=True.
  - Test: Run the hook config over 200 saved MCP JSON outputs; count json.loads successes with keep_structure False versus True; compare LLM-token ratio of each.
- **#750** [X] _tiny_ **Per-project subdirectories for saved originals to scope recursive greps** — Saved originals share one global directory keyed by content hash. An agent that lost the note and runs grep -r over outputs/ can pull another repo's or client's output, secrets or planted instructions into this session. Use outputs/<project-hash>/<session>/ so recursive greps stay scoped.
  - Test: From transcripts, count recovery commands using a glob or grep -r on outputs/. Plant a canary in project A's output; from project B, assert a directory grep cannot reach it.
- **#751** [X] _tiny_ **Reference-aware cleanup of saved originals; refresh mtime on re-save** — _save_original skips writing when the hash file exists, so mtime never refreshes. An age-based cleaner, or the documented rm -rf, then strands notes in live or resumed sessions, which may rerun commands. Keep files whose paths appear in recent transcripts.
  - Test: Scan ~/.claude/projects for note paths and report the age distribution of referenced files. Save, backdate mtime, re-emit the same output, run an mtime cleaner, and assert the file survives.
- **#754** [X] _tiny_ **Reject browser-origin and non-loopback Host requests at serve** — serve.py ignores Content-Type, Origin and Host, so any web page may be able to POST text/plain to 127.0.0.1:8719 without preflight, burning CPU behind the single model lock and queueing the hook. DNS rebinding is a second route. Require loopback Host and no Origin header.
  - Test: curl with Origin: https://evil.example and a foreign Host; assert 403. Time hook latency while a 30 MB request holds the lock; add a smaller body cap.
- **#755** [X] _tiny_ **Serve cache: digest keys, byte budget, no raw outputs retained** — The serve cache holds up to 512 entries keyed by the full raw text, so tool output, possibly with secrets, lingers in a long-lived process and memory is bounded by entry count, not bytes. Key by sha256, cap total bytes, expire idle entries.
  - Test: Post 40 distinct 5 MB texts and record RSS today; after the change assert RSS stays under the byte cap and a repeated text still hits.
- **#757** [R] _tiny_ `MCP` **Do-not-rerun clause in note for side-effecting commands** — The note says grep the original, but a cut fact may tempt a rerun. For apply, deploy, migrate, push and MCP tools without readOnlyHint, add 'do not rerun; side effects' to the note, or skip compression. Classify by verb list and MCP annotations.
  - Test: Fake deploy script appends to a counter file and prints a cut-prone 8k log. Over 20 claude -p runs per note variant, count extra counter increments.
- **#758** [X] `eval` **Sidecar with output hash and model fingerprint; ctxprune verify replays** — Only the original is saved, not what the agent saw, so post-mortems cannot tell which words were cut. A sidecar stores sha256 of the emitted text, rate and model hash; verify recompresses, flags drift between serve and in-process runs, and marks cut spans.
  - Test: Compress 100 saved outputs via serve and in-process; store hashes; run verify in a fresh process on another CPU or ORT version if available and count byte mismatches.
- **#759** [R] _tiny_ `MCP` **Normative-sentence shield for MCP specs, issues and requirement docs** — Deleting words from requirements text can flip an obligation the agent must obey, unlike log noise. For Notion, Jira, GitHub issue and doc MCP outputs, force-keep whole sentences containing must, never, do not, required, breaking, deprecated, plus checklist lines; cost is a lower ratio.
  - Test: Take 30 issue and spec bodies with planted obligations ('must not log tokens'); compress at 0.5 with and without the shield; count obligations lost or flipped, plus ratio change.
- **#780** [R] _tiny_ **LongLLMLingua-style subsequence recovery for Edit anchors** — LongLLMLingua (Jiang et al.) repairs answers by matching them back to the original prompt. Same here: if an Edit old_string is absent from the file but is an ordered subsequence of one file span, PreToolUse expands it, saving the failed-edit retry.
  - Test: Compress Bash reads of config and docs at 0.5, let an agent edit them 20 times, count not-found Edit failures with and without the repair, and any wrong-span matches.
- **#848** [X] `eval` **ddmin-minimise a bad compression into a regression fixture** — When compression loses something, delta-debug the saved original by line to the smallest slice that still reproduces the loss, then pseudonymise. A tiny fixture is safe to review and paste, and feeds the golden corpus.
  - Test: Take 5 known misfires; run ddmin with predicate 'needle atom dropped at 0.5'; report slice size versus original and whether the fixture still fails after pseudonymising.
- **#851** [X] `eval` **ctxprune flag and show: pruned twin plus human hard negatives** — The hook saves only the original, not the pruned text Claude read. Keep the twin; 'ctxprune show' renders deletions inline and 'ctxprune flag [why]' copies original, pruned text and rule ids into local fixtures. Explicit labels complement implicit regret logs (#8).
  - Test: Prune 20 saved outputs, render diffs, flag harmful deletions for a week; replay fixtures through the current build; count flags the regret log never caught.
- **#876** [X] _tiny_ **Shims filter only when stdout is the agent's capture pipe** — arXiv 2607.12161 reportedly found a stdout-pipe incompatibility in a command-rewriting compressor that gave wrong answers without errors. PATH shims (#189) and BASH_ENV filters (#579) must pass through under $(), pipes and redirects, e.g. by comparing fd 1 with the top shell's.
  - Test: Through the shim run git log --format=%H | xargs echo, V=$(pytest -q), and cmd > f; assert byte-identical to the real binary and exit codes equal.
- **#903** [X] **Dedup ledger must store emitted text, not originals** — A stub saying 'lines already shown' is false for words the hook deleted from the earlier output. Record the updatedToolOutput text per call and match later lines against it; partly deleted lines count as unseen and are re-emitted in full.
  - Test: Compress output A with the hook, then run B sharing 30 lines with A. Assert every B line stubbed as seen is byte-present in A's emitted text.
- **#1014** [X] `eval` **Counterexample loop that attacks synthesised filters with mutated needles** — Before accepting a #646 filter, a generator relocates and rewords needle lines (failing test name, error code, new warning class) in captured outputs into forms the filter drops. Survivors feed back into synthesis (counterexample-guided synthesis) until none remain.
  - Test: Take one synthesised pytest filter; mutate 200 captures by moving the failure line and rewording its message; count needles dropped before and after two repair rounds.
- **#1031** [X] _tiny_ `eval` **Adversarial worst-case timing and ReDoS fuzz for rule pre-pass** — Hundreds of regex rules will meet 5 MB minified lines, deep nesting and near-miss repeats where backtracking explodes. Generate pathological inputs per rule, fail CI above a ms-per-MB cap, and wrap the pre-pass in a wall-clock deadline that returns the original.
  - Test: Time every rule on 1 MB of: one unbroken line, repeated near-matches, nested brackets, long digit runs; use the regex module's timeout argument to find any rule exceeding 100 ms.
- **#1040** [X] `eval` **Property test: rewritten history prefix stays byte-stable and idempotent** — For any history rewriter, rewrite(msgs[:k]) must be a byte prefix of rewrite(msgs[:k+1]), and rewriting its own stubs must change nothing. Generated tool-loop histories catch rules that peek at later turns, counts or clocks and silently bust the cache.
  - Test: Run Hypothesis over generated tool-loop histories against the proxy rewrite function; assert prefix equality at every k and rewrite(rewrite(x)) == rewrite(x), including ctxprune's own notes.
- **#1049** [X] `eval` **Rerun stability: do near-identical outputs keep the same words?** — Windows shift when an early line changes (#460), so reruns of a test suite delete different words. The agent then sees a warning vanish from compressed run 2 though it persists, a phantom fix. Measure it; fix by caching keep decisions per line hash.
  - Test: Compress two pytest logs differing in timings and one early line; count lines kept in run 1, deleted in run 2, yet present verbatim in both originals.
- **#1050** [X] _tiny_ `MCP` **Saved original merges stdout, stderr and MCP blocks unlabeled** — run() saves '\n\n'.join(originals) when several slots compress. Stream and block boundaries vanish, so a recovery grep cannot say whether a line was stderr, and jq on a multi-block JSON original fails. Write one file per slot or labeled sections.
  - Test: Feed a Bash event with 7k stdout plus 7k stderr and an MCP event with two JSON blocks; check the saved file splits per slot or parses with jq.
- **#1051** [X] `eval` **Hook host and Bash host may not share a filesystem** — The note's saved path lives where the hook ran. If hooks and the Bash tool run on different hosts or mounts (WSL, SSH remote, devcontainer, docker exec, remote sessions), the grep finds nothing and the agent reruns the command. #374 probes permissions only.
  - Test: In each setup, have a stub hook write a marker file; run `test -f` on it from the agent's Bash tool and record which setups fail.
- **#1059** [X] _tiny_ **Trust gate and hash pin for repo-supplied filter files** — A repo-local .ctxprune/filters.toml (#783) can hide failing tests, warnings or planted text from the agent. rtk reportedly gates project-local filters behind explicit trust (from memory). Require approval keyed on file hash; reset trust when the file changes.
  - Test: Commit a filter dropping FAILED lines; confirm it is ignored until trusted; edit the file and confirm trust resets; confirm user-level filters still load without a prompt.
- **#1064** [X] _tiny_ `eval` **Mine rival compressors' bug trackers for failure-mode fixtures** — Open issue trackers of rtk, Headroom and context-mode reportedly hold reports of filters hiding needed lines or breaking pipelines. Each closed report becomes a regression fixture before ctxprune ships a similar rule; cheaper than discovering the same bug in users' sessions.
  - Test: Use gh to list closed bug issues mentioning wrong, missing or hidden output; classify 30 by rule type; write one fixture each for those matching our planned packs.
- **#1092** [X] _small_ **Saved originals vanish with cache cleaners and fresh containers** — Originals cannot be regenerated, yet sit under ~/.cache, which the XDG spec calls non-essential; cleaners may purge it and restored cloud sessions may lack it, stranding note paths. Use XDG_STATE_HOME or a repo-local store; a resume check validates paths.
  - Test: Run BleachBit or systemd-tmpfiles --clean over a home holding saved originals; separately delete the cache dir and resume; count failed greps and command reruns versus ~/.local/state storage.
- **#1119** [X] _tiny_ **Verify existing saved original's digest before skipping the write** — _save_original skips writing whenever the hash-named file exists, never checking content. A stale or pre-planted file (a prompt-injected agent can compute the name of a predictable output) then becomes the 'full output' the note points to. Compare digest; rewrite atomically.
  - Test: Pre-create outputs/<hash>.txt with different text, then run the hook on the matching output; assert the file is repaired or the hook declines. Repeat with a truncated file.
- **#1122** [R] _tiny_ **Stub high-confidence secrets in the model's view only** — PEM private keys, AKIA keys, ghp_ tokens, JWTs and Bearer headers become '[secret kind, N chars, sha8]' in updatedToolOutput at any size; the 0600 saved file keeps originals. Keeps credentials out of the transcript and provider. Differs from #370, which declines.
  - Test: Plant ten secret kinds in command outputs; assert the model view holds only stubs and the file holds originals; run a task needing one value and count recovery tokens.
- **#1123** [X] `eval` **Probe whether agents present pruned output as verbatim to users** — Asked 'show me the exact output' after a compressed call, an agent may paste spliced text as the real thing, an honesty failure no QA metric sees. Compare note variants (e.g. 'abridged; quote only from the saved file') and price the added words.
  - Test: Run 20 prompts asking for the exact output of compressed commands; score replies that show spliced text without a caveat or a refetch, per note variant.
- **#1141** [X] `eval` **Single-atom ablation fuzz with parse oracles ranks unprotected atoms** — Delete each unprotected atom alone from golden outputs; flag deletions that break a cheap oracle (bash -n on commands, json.loads, yaml, shlex, path resolution, number parse). Rank atom classes by breakage rate to prioritise protect rules (#1046, #494, #924).
  - Test: Run on 500 saved outputs; report breakage per atom class; check the top classes include the verified cases (~, %, +, false); ship rules for the rest.
- **#1197** [X] **Authenticate serve: unix socket or token against port squatting** — hook._compress trusts any process answering on 127.0.0.1:8719 and substitutes its text for the tool output. On shared hosts another user can bind that port first and inject content into the agent's context. Use a 0700 socket or 0600 token; #379's fingerprint is spoofable.
  - Test: As a second user, bind 8719 with a stub returning canary text; assert the hook accepts it today and rejects it after socket or token auth.
- **#1212** [X] **AGENTS.md pipe recipe masks failing exit codes** — The recipe 'some_command 2>&1 | ctxprune' returns ctxprune's status, so a failing build reads as success (checked: 'false | cat' gives 0, with pipefail gives 1). Document 'set -o pipefail', or add a 'ctxprune run -- cmd' wrapper that exits with the command's status.
  - Test: Pipe a failing pytest through the recipe in claude -p with and without pipefail; check whether the agent reports failure and whether Claude Code prints an exit-code line.
- **#1232** [X] `eval` **Canary for harness-induced duplicate tool executions** — A claude-code GitHub issue (number 41750, per its title) reports all tool calls executing twice when context management fired with empty edits. Flag adjacent identical tool_use inputs with near-equal timestamps; duplicate side effects and outputs both cost tokens.
  - Test: Scan local transcripts for consecutive identical Bash inputs under two seconds apart, excluding model-issued retries; report rate per Claude Code version.
- **#1386** [X] _tiny_ `busts-cache` **Proxy fail-open: replay original body after upstream 400** — History rewrites can orphan a tool_result or break thinking-block signatures, giving a 400 that stalls the session. Claude Code itself resends once without the context-1m beta on rejection (binary string). Proxy replays the unmodified body once and disables the failing rule for that session.
  - Test: Make the rewriter emit an orphaned tool_result on purpose. Assert the client gets a normal response, the log shows exactly one fallback, and later requests skip that rule.
- **#1387** [X] _tiny_ `eval` **Buffering rewrite proxy versus client idle timeouts and billed retries** — A proxy that buffers a message to edit tool_use arguments delays bytes. The binary has API_TIMEOUT_MS, API_FORCE_IDLE_TIMEOUT and CLAUDE_BYTE_STREAM_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS. A timeout makes the client resend the whole prefix, possibly billed again. Measure time-to-first-byte added and retry rate per rule.
  - Test: Add 5, 30 and 90 second artificial buffering delays in a passthrough proxy; count client retries and requests whose usage appears twice in the upstream log.
- **#1393** [X] _tiny_ **Atomizer is quadratic on long trailing whitespace runs** — Verified here: atomize() on 1,500 words plus 2k, 4k and 8k trailing spaces took 0.10, 0.36 and 1.5 s, about 4x per doubling. The leading-whitespace group of the atom regex rescans the tail from every start, so 64k would pass a 60 s hook timeout.
  - Test: Time atomize on 'word '*1500 plus N trailing spaces, N up to 64k; assert under 0.5 s after rstripping the tail before the regex and re-adding it as one atom.
- **#1395** [R] _tiny_ **Shield host truncation markers from word deletion** — If the host or a tool inserts a marker such as '[N lines truncated]' or '... output truncated' before the hook runs, words like 'truncated' and 'omitted' are unprotected and can be deleted. The agent then believes the output is complete. Force-keep marker lines.
  - Test: Run a Bash command printing 40k chars so the host truncates, apply the real hook, check the marker text survives; add marker regexes to the protect set.

## Other agents and IDEs

- **#188** ★ [M] _medium_ `MCP` **MCP stdio proxy that compresses any server's results** — ctxprune mcp-wrap -- <server cmd> sits between agent and MCP server and rewrites tools/call text blocks with the hook logic. Gives savings to agents with no output-rewrite hook (reportedly Codex CLI, Gemini CLI, Zed, Cline, Continue).
  - Test: Wrap a filesystem or github MCP server, drive it with a minimal JSON-RPC client, assert tools/list is unchanged and big results shrink with a saved original.
- **#189** ★ [R] _medium_ **Command wrappers for hookless agents: PATH shims, Aider test-cmd** — Shims for pytest, npm, pip, docker, cargo, git on PATH run the real binary, apply noise packs, save the original and keep the exit code. For Aider, point --test-cmd and --lint-cmd at a ctxprune runner so savings compound each resent turn.
  - Test: Generate 5 shims, run commands direct versus shimmed, compare bytes and exit codes; on a one-failing-test repo compare Aider /tokens after five more turns, wrapped versus not.
- **#191** ★ [M] _medium_ `MCP` **Shell MCP tool with an agent-supplied 'want' argument** — ctxprune_run(cmd, want, max_tokens) as a drop-in shell for any MCP agent. The agent states what it needs ('failing test names'); compression becomes query-aware, an intent channel hooks never receive. Tag as model plus rules.
  - Test: On 20 recorded failing-build outputs, compare tokens and needle recall of the want answer versus plain 0.5 compression; also measure how often an agent picks the tool.
- **#304** ★ [R] _medium_ `MCP` **Add optional jq/JMESPath projection argument to MCP tools** — A stdio proxy adds an optional _select argument to every tool schema, run server-side before results return. Prior art: Anthropic's code-execution-with-MCP post (Nov 2025) filters rows in a sandbox, reportedly 150k to 2k tokens. Complements the free-text 'want' of item 191.
  - Test: Record 10 github list_issues/list_pull_requests tasks; compare full responses against agent-projected ones, and count the schema tokens the extra argument adds per tool per turn.
- **#387** ★ [R] _medium_ `MCP` **Snippet-then-expand wrapper for search-style MCP results** — A proxy trims each hit body in list-shaped search results (code or doc search MCP servers) to about 200 characters plus id and path, and adds a synthetic expand(id) tool. Full text is paid only for hits the agent opens; originals saved.
  - Test: Replay search-MCP calls from transcripts: compare tokens of full hit bodies with the share of hits the agent later opened, quoted or edited.
- **#483** [L] `MCP` **Drop the text block that duplicates structuredContent** — The MCP spec says a tool returning structuredContent should also put the serialized JSON in a text block for old clients. If the host forwards both to the model, the payload is paid twice. Drop the text block when it parses to the same value.
  - Test: Verify that both forms reach the model: build a FastMCP tool with an output schema, dump the PostToolUse tool_response, check the transcript, and count duplicated tokens.
- **#484** [R] `MCP` **Drop MCP content blocks annotated audience user-only** — MCP content blocks can carry annotations with audience [user] or priority. A wrapper or hook can drop blocks the server addressed only to the user, or low-priority blocks past a budget, before the model reads them. Spec-defined, so it needs no learned judgement.
  - Test: Verify that Claude Code forwards user-only blocks: write a toy server returning audience [user] and [assistant] blocks; if it does, count tokens a drop rule saves on real servers.
- **#485** ★ [R] _medium_ `MCP` **One ci_failure MCP tool replacing four serial gh calls** — Wrap gh api calls: find the failing check, job and step from steps[].conclusion, fetch only that step's log, append annotations and the matrix legs affected. One call returns root-cause lines as a compact digest instead of checks, view, log and grep turns.
  - Test: Replay 10 CI-failure tasks; compare turns and billed tokens for the agent using raw gh versus the tool; check the digest names the same root cause as the full log.
- **#503** ★ [R] _medium_ `MCP` **Gateway MCP exposing list, describe, invoke instead of N schemas** — mcp-compressor reportedly proxies any MCP server, swapping the upfront tool dump for a compact tool list with schemas fetched on demand. Aimed at hosts without deferred tool loading, where #250's ToolSearch route does not apply.
  - Test: Wrap the GitHub MCP (about 50 tools); count tool-block tokens before and after; run 10 tasks for success rate and extra describe calls.
- **#578** [R] _small_ **GIT_PAGER and PAGER set to a filter in pty agents** — Agents using a pty (OpenHands tmux, Cline and Cursor VS Code terminals, Zed terminal) make git, man, psql, systemctl act interactive. Pointing GIT_PAGER, PAGER, SYSTEMD_PAGER at a filter interposes with no wrappers. Checked here: GIT_PAGER fires under script(1), not when piped.
  - Test: Under script -qec, run git log -p with GIT_PAGER set to a noise-pack filter; compare bytes. Check which target agents allocate a pty and preset GIT_PAGER=cat.
- **#579** ★ [R] _medium_ **BASH_ENV stdout filter covering every command in non-interactive bash** — BASH_ENV pointing at a file containing exec > >(filter) streams all stdout of each bash -c through one filter, no per-binary shims. Checked here: exit code kept, no lost lines in 5 runs; stderr bypasses and reorders. Login shells skip BASH_ENV.
  - Test: Set BASH_ENV in Codex shell_environment_policy, Zed terminal env or OpenHands sandbox env; run a failing pytest and a 20 KB command; compare bytes, exit status, login-shell behavior.
- **#581** [M] **ACP middleman compresses terminal and file responses for any agent** — Agent Client Protocol (Zed's JSON-RPC protocol, reportedly also in JetBrains and Neovim) lets agents delegate terminal/output and fs/read_text_file to the client. A shim in the client role compresses those replies and lowers outputByteLimit, covering every ACP agent at once.
  - Test: Verify that agents delegate terminal/output to the client. Serve a 100 KB log from a mock ACP client to an ACP agent (gemini --experimental-acp); check the transcript for compressed text.
- **#585** [X] _tiny_ `eval` **Cross-agent canary-command probe: envelope tokens, caps, spill files** — Run one script (true, echo hi, seq 1 N, one 1 MB line) in each agent. Record fixed per-call envelope tokens (Gemini shell result reportedly lists Stderr/Error/Signal fields even when empty), truncation style and cap, and whether full output spills to a file.
  - Test: Drive the script through codex exec, gemini -p and Cline; dump the model-visible tool result from logs; tabulate envelope tokens per call and the cap where output is cut.
- **#669** [R] _medium_ `conditional` **Rewrite tool_use arguments in the response stream** — Proxy buffers tool_use input_json_delta (or OpenAI tool_calls arguments), applies PreToolUse-style rules such as quiet flags, head caps and dropping Bash description, then emits the edited call. Client runs and stores the edited call, covering hookless agents.
  - Test: Mock-stream a Bash tool_use with a bare pytest command through the proxy; assert the client receives -q appended, valid JSON deltas and unchanged stop_reason; repeat with OpenAI tool_calls.
- **#684** ★ [R] _medium_ `MCP` **Bridge MCP servers into shell CLIs such as mcp2cli** — Wrappers like mcp2cli (Show HN claims 96-99% fewer tokens; author's figure) expose MCP tools as shell commands: schemas leave the prompt, and results flow through jq, head and the Bash hook. Checkly measured near-equal totals for Playwright, so benchmark per server.
  - Test: Wrap github and one browser server; run 10 identical tasks via native MCP versus CLI bridge; compare schema tokens, result tokens and turn counts, not only per-call size.
- **#864** [R] `MCP` **Return digest plus resource_link from wrapped MCP tools** — The MCP 2025-06-18 spec (from memory) adds a resource_link content block. A ctxprune mcp-wrap (#188) returns the digest plus a link, and serves the full text on resources/read, so no extra expand-tool schema is added. Host handling of the block is unknown.
  - Test: Verify that Claude Code handles resource_link: a toy server returns text plus a link; check whether the host shows the URI, auto-reads it or drops it; count tokens.
- **#946** ★ [R] _medium_ **SHELL override: one wrapper shell filters every agent command** — Agents that spawn $SHELL -c (Aider reportedly) or take a VS Code terminal profile (Cline, Roo, Cursor reportedly) can use one wrapper that execs the real shell and filters piped output. Unlike BASH_ENV (#579) it covers login shells; a prototype kept exit code 7.
  - Test: Verify that each agent honors SHELL. Point it at the wrapper; run a failing 20 KB command in each agent's bash tool; record exit codes and bytes returned.
- **#1218** [X] `eval, MCP` **MCP wrap conformance suite: capabilities, progress, cancellation passthrough** — A stdio proxy that rewrites results must forward everything else unchanged: initialize capabilities, tools/list cursors, list_changed, progress, cancellation, resources, prompts, sampling and isError flags. Diff scripted-client transcripts of the wrapped and unwrapped server.
  - Test: Drive a reference server directly and through the MCP stdio proxy (#188) with one scripted client; diff every JSON-RPC message except tools/call text blocks over the threshold.
- **#1302** ★ [R] _medium_ **Read-only digest view of the repo for hookless agents** — Mount a FUSE or rsync-mirror view where lockfiles, minified bundles, snapshots, big fixtures and notebooks appear as digests and source stays real. It sits beneath every agent's tools, so Cursor, Zed, Aider and Codex need no hook.
  - Test: Build the mirror with symlinks plus digests for a real repo; run one scripted rg, cat and ls exploration in both trees; compare o200k tokens and answers to five questions.
- **#1304** [X] `eval` **Gemini CLI summarizeToolOutput as baseline arm or ctxprune backend** — Gemini CLI has a summarizeToolOutput setting giving run_shell_command a token budget, enforced by an extra LLM call. Measure that call's cost and loss against ctxprune on the same outputs; if a base-URL override exists, point it at a local endpoint.
  - Test: Verify that the setting exists; replay 30 recorded shell outputs through it and through ctxprune at equal output tokens; count extra requests and tokens, and score one question per output.
- **#1309** [M] **Standalone JS port of the classifier for TypeScript agent plugins** — The int8 ONNX model could run in onnxruntime-node or WASM, with a JS tokenizer and atom rules ported. Plugins for opencode, Gemini CLI extensions, Cline hooks and VS Code extensions then compress in-process, with no Python install or serve daemon.
  - Test: Run model_quantized.onnx in Node on 200 saved inputs; diff per-token logits against Python onnxruntime and time cold load. Port atom logic only if parity holds.
- **#1385** [X] `eval` **Wire-format matrix: Bedrock and Vertex routes versus proxy rewrites** — Binary supports ANTHROPIC_BEDROCK_BASE_URL and Vertex routes, whose bodies and streams reportedly differ from /v1/messages (model in URL, AWS event-stream framing, betas in body). Mark which proxy ideas need per-route adapters, and whether context-management and 1h caching exist there.
  - Test: Set CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK with ANTHROPIC_BEDROCK_BASE_URL on a logging server; capture one turn; diff path, headers, body fields and stream framing against a first-party capture.
- **#1391** [R] **Set hook threshold from the backend's real context window** — Agents behind gateways on Ollama, llama.cpp or vLLM may run with far less context than hosted models; Ollama reportedly defaults small and truncates from the front. Set CTXPRUNE_MIN_CHARS and keep rate from the served window, not constants.
  - Test: Serve a small-context model; run one agent task with and without the hook; log prompt_eval_count against tokens sent, and note silent truncation and task success.

## Code-aware views

- **#206** ★ [R] _medium_ **Signature-only skeleton for large source reads** — tree-sitter skeleton keeps imports and class/def signatures with first docstring line, elides bodies as '... lines 41-88'. Reportedly similar to rtk smart read, repomix --compress and headroom CodeCompressor. Opt-in first look only, never when editing.
  - Test: Take 30 real cat/Read outputs over 300 lines; compare skeleton vs full tokens, then check an agent still finds the target function via the line ranges.
- **#305** ★ [R] _medium_ **Query-ranked function bodies for large source reads** — Split a big source file into functions with tree-sitter, rank them by BM25 against the user prompt and command, keep top bodies whole and shrink the rest to signature plus docstring line. LongCodeZip (Shi et al., 2025) reports up to 5.6x on code tasks.
  - Test: Take 30 large Reads from transcripts; check what share of lines later quoted in Edit old_strings fall inside kept bodies, versus signature-only skeleton item 206.
- **#403** [R] `MCP` **Zero-dependency FTS5 snippet search tool replacing exploratory grep and Read** — claude-context (Zilliz) reportedly pairs BM25 with a vector database in an MCP server. Lighter variant: SQLite FTS5 over code chunks, top 5 snippets with path:line, no embeddings. Aims to cut multi-step grep and Read exploration, distinct from repo map (item 207).
  - Test: Index this repo; ask 10 'where is X handled' questions to an agent with and without the tool; compare tokens until a correct answer, and score correctness by hand.
- **#506** [R] _small_ **Strip comments and blank lines from exploratory source dumps** — rtk reportedly filters comments and whitespace; repomix offers --remove-comments. Opt-in for cat of source when no edit follows, with a note 'comments stripped; Read before Edit'. Exact-match Edit would otherwise fail on stripped lines; license headers (#327) are a subset.
  - Test: Measure comment+blank token share in 50 cat/Read results of source from transcripts; replay 20 Edit calls against stripped text and count old_string mismatches.
- **#691** [R] _small_ **Collapse docstrings to first sentence in exploratory source dumps** — claude-compress (PyPI) reportedly collapses block comments to one line and multi-line docstrings to their first sentence on cat, head and tail, claiming 15-50% on source. #506 strips only comments and blanks. Docstrings carry contracts, so gate on no Edit following; keep the original.
  - Test: Measure docstring token share in 50 Python cat/Read results from transcripts; replay 20 questions answerable only from docstring bodies and count answers lost.
- **#781** [R] _small_ **Replace grep -C context lines with enclosing-scope headers** — Aider's grep-ast shows matches under their enclosing def or class lines, not N raw neighbour lines. For -A/-B/-C output, drop context lines and prepend the nearest lower-indent line. In a local -C3 grep, context lines outnumbered matches 298 to 59.
  - Test: Replay Grep calls with context flags from transcripts; compare token cost of raw versus scope-header form; check the model still locates the right function in 20 find-the-definition questions.
- **#1056** [M] **Goal-hint line skimmer for source reads, SWE-Pruner style** — SWE-Pruner (reportedly a recent paper) has the agent state a goal hint per read; a small neural skimmer keeps goal-relevant lines and drops the rest. Line-level and learned, unlike BM25 function ranking (#305); intent comes from the agent.
  - Test: On 30 recorded Reads over 300 lines, derive a goal from the next Grep or Edit; compare kept lines later quoted versus #305 at equal tokens.
- **#1074** [R] **Fold long Tailwind class strings in component file reads** — Replace className literals over ~12 classes with 'cls[14]: flex items-center ...' plus an id; PreToolUse expands the id inside Edit old_string (mechanism of #779). Lossy, for exploratory reads only; share of component tokens unmeasured.
  - Test: Tokenise class/className literals in five open-source Tailwind repos to get their share of component files; then run 10 logic-only edit tasks with and without folding.
- **#1076** [R] _small_ **Fold ORM boilerplate in Django migrations and Rails schema.rb** — makemigrations repeats the same id field tuple, BigAutoField(auto_created=True, primary_key=True, serialize=False, verbose_name='ID'), per model; schema.rb repeats created_at/updated_at datetime lines per table. Fold to 'id=auto' and 'timestamps' markers on exploratory reads.
  - Test: Generate migrations for 20 models and a 20-table schema.rb; fold; tokenise; ask an agent to write a dependent migration or model and compare correctness.
- **#1318** [R] _small_ **Trigram and identifier-split index for BM25 recall** — context-mode fuses Porter BM25 with an FTS5 trigram index by reciprocal rank fusion, plus proximity rerank. Word-level BM25 misses partial camelCase, snake_case and path tokens. Add trigram and identifier-split channels to recall over saved originals (#654) and code search (#403).
  - Test: Use agents' later grep patterns as queries and the grepped line as gold; compare top-3 hit rate for word BM25, trigram and fused ranking on saved originals.
- **#1319** [R] _small_ **Per-glob Read levels: full, signatures, tree-only, processors** — Repomix lets each glob choose full, compressed or tree-only content and run external processors such as JSON to TOON or SVG minify. Ship a project table mapping globs to a level or processor, applied to Read and cat results, beyond one-off deny rules (#401).
  - Test: Draft a 5-glob table for one repo; replay its Read and cat calls from transcripts and tally tokens each glob rule would have saved.

## Prompt-cache management

- **#256** [L] _small_ `MCP` **Canonicalise cache prefix: tool order, key order, volatile strings** — Cache hits need byte-identical prefixes, and tool definitions come first, so one changed byte invalidates everything after. Diff tools/list across sessions for volatile descriptions, unstable order and reconnects; a proxy sorts tools and keys and moves volatile text (date, git status) after the last breakpoint.
  - Test: Start each MCP server twice and diff tools/list byte-wise; diff first requests of consecutive same-repo sessions in a logging proxy; replay canonicalised and compare cache_read_input_tokens.
- **#434** [R] _small_ `busts-cache` **Suffix-aware gate for stubbing old history** — Editing history at position p rewrites every later token at +1.15x over a read while warm, but costs nothing right after a gap past the TTL. Stub only if saved × 0.1 × turns_left beats suffix × 1.15, or the cache is cold.
  - Test: On recorded transcripts, compute suffix tokens and remaining turns for every superseded tool result; count candidates passing the warm rule versus the cold-gap rule; total net token-equivalents.
- **#438** [R] _tiny_ `MCP` **Pin MCP tool list against mid-session list_changed churn** — Tools sit first in the cached prefix, so one edited or reordered tool after a tools/list_changed notification rewrites system and messages too. A proxy keeps the first-seen tool array and applies changes only after compaction or a cold gap.
  - Test: Fake MCP server emits notifications/tools/list_changed every 3 minutes; verify Claude Code refetches, and measure next-request cache_creation; repeat with the pinning proxy.
- **#767** [L] _small_ **Document-order sort of retrieved chunks to extend cached prefix** — Score-ordered top-k reshuffles between follow-up questions, breaking any shared prefix. Sorting hits by source and offset makes overlapping retrievals share leading bytes and reads coherently, also feeding adjacent-hit merging. Reading-order effect on accuracy must be checked.
  - Test: Run 30 follow-ups over one document set; compare the longest common token prefix of consecutive contexts in score versus document order, then reader accuracy.
- **#1036** [L] **Request-hash response replay for re-run headless agent jobs** — A proxy stores each upstream response keyed by a hash of the full request body. A CI re-run of the same task on the same commit replays every turn free until a tool output differs and the hash diverges. Needs volatile strings canonicalised.
  - Test: Run one claude -p review twice on a fixed commit through a recording proxy; count replayed requests before the first miss and diff the first diverging request body.
- **#1177** [R] _small_ `busts-cache` **Stub history on forced prefix mutations, not just idle gaps** — A tool-list, system or model change already rewrites the cache from the changed point. A logging proxy detects the changed tools or system hash and runs the pending stub pass in that same request, when it costs no extra rewrite. Extends #434's cold-gap rule.
  - Test: Replay recorded sessions with seeded toggles (MCP reconnect, model switch); count stub candidates fireable free on the toggle request versus waiting for an idle gap; price with #811's simulator.
- **#1384** [L] **Drop volatile billing-header line from system prompt behind gateways** — Binary builds an x-anthropic-billing-header system line with cc_version and, on first-party, a per-turn cc_prev_msg id. Unknown whether gateways or local servers strip it; if not, byte-level prefix caches (KV, Bedrock) miss every turn. Proxy removes it, or set CLAUDE_CODE_ATTRIBUTION_HEADER=0.
  - Test: Verify that the env var exists. Diff system[0] of two consecutive requests via a passthrough proxy; run a llama.cpp or vLLM backend with and without the line; compare cache hits.
- **#1401** [X] `eval, MCP` **Cache cost of per-turn tool-menu narrowing versus masking** — Manus's context-engineering post (2025) warns that adding or removing tools mid-run invalidates the KV cache since tool definitions sit at the prefix start, and masks logits instead. #1305 does not price this; weigh the cache rewrite against schema tokens saved.
  - Test: Simulate with recorded usage: 40-turn session, tool set changed every turn versus every 10 turns versus never; compute cache_creation at 1.25x versus tokens saved from the smaller schemas.

## Media, browser & documents

- **#330** [R] _small_ **Clean pdftotext residue: page furniture and hyphen breaks** — pdftotext separates pages with form feeds. Drop lines recurring at page edges after digit masking (headers, footers, 'Page 3 of 40'), keeping the first; rejoin 'compres-' newline 'sion' when the joined word occurs elsewhere. Six-break sample: 32 vs 17 tokens (o200k proxy).
  - Test: Run pdftotext on 10 papers and manuals; count recurring edge lines and hyphen joins; check joins against a word list for fused compounds like 'well-known'; tokenize before and after.
- **#347** ★ [R] _medium_ **Clean YouTube-style VTT and SRT caption files** — Auto-caption VTT repeats each phrase across rolling cues and tags every word with <00:00:01.599><c>. Merge overlapping cues, drop inline tags and positioning, keep a [mm:ss] stamp about every 30 s. One synthetic cue went 253 to 18 tokens (o200k).
  - Test: Fetch real subtitles with yt-dlp --write-auto-subs for 5 videos; tokenize raw versus cleaned; assert cleaned words are an ordered subsequence; ask 10 content questions.
- **#348** ★ [R] _medium_ **Whisper and ASR JSON to plain timestamped text** — openai-whisper style results carry token-id arrays, avg_logprob, compression_ratio, no_speech_prob and seek per segment; word timestamps add a record per word. Render '[mm:ss] speaker: text', merge same-speaker turns, flag only low-confidence segments. Synthetic segment: 132 to 16 tokens.
  - Test: Transcribe a 5-minute clip with whisper --output_format json (plus a diarized tool); tokenize JSON versus rendering; check concatenated text equals the original.
- **#350** ★ [R] _medium_ **Compact accessibility trees: wrappers, hrefs, repeated landmarks** — Collapse single-child generic wrapper chains (synthetic 47 to 14 tokens), drop refs on non-interactive nodes, fold '- /url:' lines into the link line, cap long hrefs and list children at k plus a count. Stub banner, navigation and contentinfo subtrees repeated from an earlier page.
  - Test: Capture snapshots of 10 sites via Playwright MCP; tokenize raw versus compacted; report nav/footer token share; run 20 click-by-ref and find-by-text tasks for success.
- **#351** ★ [R] _medium_ **Digest browser network, console and Lighthouse output** — Network request lists include every static asset; console lists repeat HMR and dev-warning lines; Lighthouse JSON runs to megabytes. Keep failed, slow and XHR/fetch requests, fold static assets to counts, dedupe console lines, keep Lighthouse scores and failing audits.
  - Test: Capture network, console and Lighthouse output for 5 local dev pages; tokenize full versus digest; assert every 4xx/5xx and console error survives; ask 5 debugging questions.
- **#712** [R] _small_ **Office XML reads: strip rsid noise or extract text only** — Agents run unzip -p on docx, pptx and xlsx and read XML full of w:rsid*, w14:paraId, proofErr and repeated rPr runs. For display-only reads, drop those and merge equal runs; for content reads, extract w:t/a:t text and resolve sharedStrings. Synthetic paragraph: 620 vs 360 tokens.
  - Test: Unpack 10 real Office files; measure attribute token share; strip with lxml; diff extracted text against pandoc output; replay string-replace edits to count old_string misses; gate display-only.
- **#728** ★ [R] _medium_ **Digest HAR files to one line per request** — HAR 1.2 entries carry full headers, cookies, timings and base64 bodies per request. Emit 'METHOD url status ms bytes' per entry, truncate bodies only for failed, slow or XHR rows, and fold static assets to counts. Distinct from live network lists (#351).
  - Test: Export 3 HARs from DevTools; tokenise raw versus digest; assert every 4xx/5xx and the slowest 5 entries survive; ask 5 debugging questions on both.
- **#729** ★ [R] _medium_ **OCR engine outputs (hOCR, TSV, Textract JSON) to plain lines** — hOCR wraps each word in a span with bbox and confidence; tesseract TSV has 12 columns per word; Textract and Vision JSON add polygons and ids. Render text lines, flagging only words under a confidence floor. Synthetic 20 hOCR words: 787 vs 40 tokens (o200k).
  - Test: Run tesseract hocr/tsv and pdftotext -bbox-layout on 5 scans; tokenise before and after; assert joined text equals tesseract plain output.
- **#731** [R] _small_ **Strip spoken disfluencies from ASR and meeting transcripts** — Drop um, uh, 'you know', immediate repeats ('the the', 'is is') and false-start fragments in speech transcripts from Whisper, Zoom or auto-captions. Synthetic sentence: 26 vs 11 tokens (o200k). A stricter mode could also drop hedges like 'basically'.
  - Test: Run on 5 real transcripts; tokenise; hand-audit 100 sampled removals for meaning loss; ask 10 content questions on raw versus stripped text.
- **#1083** [R] _small_ **Collapse PDF extractor glyph garbage: (cid:N) runs, spaced letters** — pdfminer prints (cid:123) for glyphs lacking a Unicode map; letter-spaced headings extract as 'C O N T R A C T'. Stub cid runs; rejoin five-plus single capitals when the result is a known word. Synthetic: 40 vs 8 and 18 vs 4 o200k tokens.
  - Test: Extract text from 20 varied PDFs with pdfminer, pdfplumber and pdftotext; count cid and spaced-letter runs; check joins against a word list; ask a heading-lookup question before and after.
- **#1084** [R] _small_ **Stub References sections in pdftotext and arXiv paper text** — Paper-to-code tasks read pdftotext or arXiv markdown ending in long reference lists and appendices. Detect a References or Bibliography heading near the end, keep an entry count and the first entries, stub the rest. Extends #264; token share untested.
  - Test: Run pdftotext on 15 arXiv papers; measure token share after the References heading; ask 10 method questions plus 3 'which paper introduced X' lookups; count refetches.
- **#1096** [R] _small_ **Fold identify -verbose histograms and exiftool maker notes** — Measured: identify -verbose on a synthetic 800x600 gradient printed 942 lines, 35,194 o200k tokens, 97% of it an 836-line Histogram; plain identify is 36. Fold the histogram to top colours plus a count; drop ICC/profile hex and exiftool maker notes.
  - Test: Run identify -verbose, exiftool and mediainfo on 10 photos and screenshots; tokenise; assert geometry, depth, colorspace, orientation and date/GPS tags survive; ask 5 metadata questions.
- **#1097** [R] _small_ **Caption cleanup for ASS, TTML, json3 and LRC files** — #347 covers only VTT and SRT. ASS carries a style header and {\an2\fad} overrides per Dialogue line; TTML wraps each cue in p attributes (synthetic 40-cue TTML: 1,948 o200k tokens versus about 520 for text alone). Extract text, keep sparse stamps.
  - Test: Fetch subs with yt-dlp --sub-format ass/ttml/json3 for 5 videos; tokenise raw versus cleaned; assert cleaned words are an ordered subsequence; ask 10 content questions.
- **#1098** [R] _small_ `MCP` **Playwright MCP envelope: drop echoed code, unchanged page header** — Playwright MCP results reportedly start with a 'Ran Playwright code' block echoing the call just made, then page URL, title and console sections. Strip the code echo (derivable from the arguments) and fold an unchanged URL/title to 'same page'.
  - Test: Verify that the layout exists: run @playwright/mcp through 10 actions; tokenise each section per response; report envelope share outside the snapshot; confirm error text is untouched.
- **#1104** ★ [R] _medium_ **Transcript chaptering: timestamped topic segments with lead sentences** — For hour-long meeting or lecture transcripts, segment by lexical shift (TextTiling, Hearst 1997) and emit '[12:40] lead sentence, N words elided', keeping the original on disk to grep. Differs from disfluency stripping (#731) and cue merging (#347).
  - Test: Segment 5 long transcripts; compare outline tokens with full text; ask 10 questions and count those answerable from the outline versus needing a refetch.

## Compaction & session lifecycle

- **#338** [R] _small_ `busts-cache` **Detect reverted edits; stub dead-end spans on resume** — Find Edit hunks later undone (git checkout, git restore, or an inverse Edit by hunk hash) and the failing runs between them. Resume surgery (147) or a proxy swaps those spans for a one-line 'tried and reverted: file, goal' stub, keeping the lesson.
  - Test: Scan transcripts for edit/inverse-edit or git checkout pairs; sum in-between tool-result tokens; replay a stubbed copy and check the model does not retry the dead end.
- **#1202** [R] _small_ `busts-cache` **Reuse hook-time atom ranks to shrink aged results without ORT** — Hook scores are paid for once. With stored ranks (#562), a proxy or compaction step at a cold-cache boundary (#630) can re-render results older than N turns at 0.25 instead of 0.5, with no model call. Mirror of expansion.
  - Test: Store ranks for 30 hook outputs; simulate shrinking those older than 10 turns from 0.5 to 0.25; count tokens saved and ask questions about old results.

## RAG and vector pipelines

- **#380** [L] _small_ **Strip chunk-overlap text duplicated across retrieved neighbouring chunks** — Splitters with chunk_overlap (LangChain RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter, LlamaIndex SentenceSplitter) make adjacent hits share text. Using source and start_index offsets, merge touching hits and emit each character span once. Exact offsets, unlike MinHash similarity matching.
  - Test: Index a docs folder with overlap 200, retrieve top-8 for 50 questions, count characters covered by more than one hit, tokenize merged versus raw context.
- **#381** [R] _small_ **Hoist or exclude per-chunk metadata blocks from LLM-visible text** — The LlamaIndex postprocessor rewrites only get_content(), so metadata that MetadataMode.LLM prepends per node (file path, dates, size, reportedly) is never pruned; same for contextual-retrieval prefixes. Set excluded_llm_metadata_keys or hoist the shared header once per source.
  - Test: Build a default SimpleDirectoryReader index, print LLM-mode text of 8 retrieved nodes, measure metadata token share, compare answer accuracy with keys excluded.
- **#382** ★ [M] _medium_ **Split one token budget across retrieved docs by rerank score** — Both integrations apply one uniform rate per document and ignore the query they are passed. Allocate a global kept-token budget by reranker or retriever score: top hits near 1.0, tail hits cut hard or dropped at a score elbow.
  - Test: On top-10 retrievals for a multi-hop QA set, compare uniform 0.5 with score-weighted allocation at equal total tokens; paired bootstrap on reader accuracy.
- **#383** ★ [M] _medium_ **Score chunks once at ingest; store quantised scores in payload** — Query-agnostic atom scores can be computed at ingestion and stored as uint8 in the vector-DB payload. Query time picks a threshold for any budget with no model call, and each chunk yields identical bytes, keeping prompt-cache prefixes stable. Embed originals, serve pruned.
  - Test: Ingest 2,000 chunks with stored scores; check recall@k is unchanged, selected text matches live compress at the same rate, and query-time latency is milliseconds.
- **#384** ★ [R] _medium_ **Strip corpus-wide document-frequency boilerplate lines before embedding** — Hash lines across all pages of a scraped or PDF corpus; lines present in over roughly 20 percent of documents (nav, cookie banners, running headers, license footers) are removed at ingestion, so they never fill retrieved chunks. Corpus-level, unlike per-output dedupe.
  - Test: On 1,000 crawled documentation pages, count lines above 20 percent document frequency, their token share, and hand-review 50 removals for lost content.
- **#385** ★ [M] _medium_ **Cascade a question-conditioned sentence pruner before token pruning** — Run an off-the-shelf query-aware pruner first, such as RECOMP's extractive compressor (Xu et al., 2023) or Provence (reportedly Naver Labs Europe, 2025), then ctxprune on survivors. Whole irrelevant sentences go before word-level scoring. Different from training our own pair-input model.
  - Test: On 200 retrieved passages with questions, compare pruner then ctxprune at 0.5 total kept against ctxprune alone at equal tokens; reader exact-match.
- **#386** [M] _small_ **Anchor compression on the retriever's matched span** — In sentence-window and parent-document retrieval, the matched sentence is a free relevance label. Keep it verbatim and compress only the surrounding window harder. LlamaIndex MetadataReplacementPostProcessor and LangChain ParentDocumentRetriever expose the hit; same trick for rg -C context lines.
  - Test: Build a sentence-window index; compare uniform 0.5 against hit-verbatim plus context at 0.3 at equal tokens, on questions answered in or near the hit.
- **#768** [X] `eval` **Chunk size, overlap, top-k and prune-rate Pareto grid** — Unknown whether pruning large chunks beats retrieving small raw ones. Grid chunk size, overlap, k and keep rate; plot reader accuracy against prompt tokens to find dominated settings and whether pruning replaces chunk overlap. Informs integration defaults.
  - Test: One QA set and corpus; 3 sizes x 3 overlaps x k in 3,5,10 x keep 1.0 and 0.5; report the Pareto front with a paired bootstrap.
- **#770** ★ [R] _medium_ **Collapse near-duplicate chunks across document versions at ingest** — Versioned docs, mirrored READMEs and copied sections yield several near-identical chunks that fill top-k. SimHash or MinHash chunks at ingestion, keep one canonical chunk with a version list in payload, and delete the rest from the index. Chunk-level, unlike line-frequency boilerplate stripping.
  - Test: Index a repo with v1 to v3 doc folders; count chunk clusters above Jaccard 0.9, the top-10 slots they occupy for 50 questions, and tokens recovered.
- **#964** [X] _tiny_ **LlamaIndex postprocessor mutates retrieved nodes in place** — _postprocess_nodes calls set_content on the retrieved node objects. With an in-memory docstore these may be the stored nodes, so each later query would compress already pruned text, compounding loss. Return copies instead.
  - Test: Build a small in-memory index, run the same query three times with the postprocessor, and compare node text lengths between runs.
- **#1128** ★ [M] _medium_ **Rank on pruned candidates before LLM listwise rerank** — LLM rerankers (LlamaIndex LLMRerank, RankGPTRerank; LangChain LLMListwiseRerank; names from memory) read every candidate in full. Show them a 0.3-kept view to order candidates, then pass the chosen few at higher fidelity.
  - Test: On 100 HotpotQA queries with 30 BM25 candidates, run a listwise LLM rerank on raw versus 0.3-pruned text; compare NDCG@10 and rerank prompt tokens.
- **#1130** [R] _small_ **Drop LlamaIndex _node_content JSON blob from raw vector DB reads** — LlamaIndex stores each whole node as a JSON string in payload metadata (verified in llama-index-core 0.12.0 source), plus doc_id, document_id and ref_doc_id copies; Qdrant may keep the text inside too. Agents reading the DB directly pay per hit. Keep text, file, offsets.
  - Test: Repeat the synthetic check (571 metadata vs 169 chunk tokens) on a real LlamaIndex Chroma or Qdrant collection; strip the blob; confirm text, file and offsets remain.
- **#1132** [R] _small_ `MCP` **Slim RAG-server MCP envelopes: Context7 snippets, qdrant entries** — Context7-style servers reportedly return snippet blocks (title, description, source URL, code) split by long dash rules; mcp-server-qdrant wraps hits as <entry><content/><metadata/></entry>. Drop rules, fold description equal to title, dedupe identical code, hoist URL prefixes and shared metadata keys.
  - Test: Verify the formats: capture 10 Context7 and 8 qdrant find results; count tokens in separators, wrappers, repeated prefixes; apply the rewrite; check every code block and metadata value survives.
- **#1135** [M] _small_ **Mean pruner score as a chunk-quality prior for filtering** — Mean atom keep-probability per chunk is a query-free density signal; low-percentile chunks may be boilerplate or filler (to be verified). Exclude them at ingest or demote them in top-k, before spending any prune budget.
  - Test: Score 2,000 chunks of a docs crawl; hand-label the lowest 10 percent as filler or content; then compare recall@8 on 50 questions with those chunks excluded.
- **#1136** [M] _tiny_ **Embed pruned chunks to cut embedding-API tokens** — Idea #383 embeds originals and serves pruned. The opposite saves embedding-API tokens at ingest: embed a 0.7-kept version of each chunk and serve that same text. Risk: query words deleted from the embedded text lower recall.
  - Test: On a BEIR subset, embed raw versus 0.7- and 0.5-pruned chunks with one embedding model; compare recall@10 and total embedded tokens.

## Output tokens and thinking

- **#449** ★ [R] _medium_ `MCP` **Line-range edit tool with content-hash guard, no old_string** — An MCP replace_lines(path, start, end, sha, new) drops old_string from every edit; the sha of the range, taken at the last Read, rejects stale edits. Anchors currently cost output tokens on every Edit; some other harnesses use line- or hash-addressed edit formats.
  - Test: Measure old_string share of Edit input tokens in transcripts; count stale-range rejects when files changed between Read and Edit; 20 edit tasks comparing success rate and output tokens.
- **#668** [R] _tiny_ **Abort generation on degenerate repetition loops mid-stream** — Proxy watches SSE text_delta and tool-input deltas for a repeating n-gram, then closes upstream and ends the message cleanly. Output tokens stop accruing at disconnect (to be confirmed). Targets runaway loops that otherwise run to max_tokens.
  - Test: Replay a mock SSE with a looping text block; assert the client gets a valid message_stop. Then cancel a real streamed call midway and compare billed output_tokens.
- **#829** [X] _small_ `eval` **Generated-comment tax in model-written code** — Comment and docstring lines in Write content and Edit new_string cost output tokens once and input tokens on every later Read. Compare their share with the repo's own comment density; if higher, add a steering line.
  - Test: Tokenise comment lines in new_string and content per language across transcripts; compare with comment ratio of files at HEAD; report tokens above baseline.
- **#1054** [R] _small_ **Symbol-addressed edit tool: replace a function by name** — Serena-style replace_symbol_body(path, symbol, new_body) resolves the span with tree-sitter, so the edit never emits old_string. Unlike the hash-guarded line-range tool (#449), nothing goes stale when lines shift; failure means only a missing or ambiguous symbol.
  - Test: Measure old_string tokens in whole-function Edits across transcripts; run 15 function-rewrite tasks with the tool versus Edit; compare output tokens, failed edits and wrong-span rate.
- **#1185** [X] _tiny_ `eval` **Assistant prose redundancy: echoed tool results and repeated drafts** — Agents restate output before the next call ('The output shows...') and re-emit whole tables, drafts or plans with small changes. Measure n-gram overlap with the preceding tool_result and 70%+ line overlap with earlier assistant blocks; sizes don't-restate and changed-lines-only rules.
  - Test: Script over ~/.claude/projects jsonl: 5-gram overlap per text block plus difflib against earlier blocks; sum o200k tokens of overlapped spans as a share of assistant text.
- **#1190** [R] _tiny_ **Short alias keys for built-in tool parameters via proxy** — A proxy renames file_path, old_string, new_string, run_in_background and output_mode to shorter aliases in the tools array and translates tool_use back in the stream. Keys recur in every call and history copy; the per-key saving may be only a token or two.
  - Test: Tokenise parameter keys across transcript tool_use blocks and sum the possible saving; run a small tool-call accuracy suite with aliases.

## Private corpus & telemetry

- **#465** [X] `eval` **Shape-preserving pseudonymizer for private training and eval corpora** — Replace secrets, UUIDs, hashes, IPs, emails, usernames and paths with random strings of the same class and length, consistent per document. Typed placeholders skew measurement: on synthetic logs (tiktoken cl100k) they cut tokens 54% and zlib size 98%; same-shape fill moved tokens under 1%.
  - Test: Scrub 200 real outputs both ways; compare count_tokens, zlib ratio and classifier keep-probabilities against raw; accept the scrubber only if drift stays under 2%.
- **#466** [X] `eval` **k-anonymous line-template export for reviewable cross-user sharing** — Mask variable fields Drain-style, then export only templates seen in at least k distinct sessions or repos, with counts and token mass, filtered against a public-vocabulary allowlist. The user reviews the file before posting it in an issue. Feeds pack discovery without raw text.
  - Test: Run on 20 local sessions with k=3; grep the export for home paths, repo names and planted canary secrets; count how many top-50 token-mass templates survive.
- **#467** [X] `eval` **Content-free deletion-class counters per command family** — Hook increments counters of kept and dropped atoms by class (digit, path, URL, identifier, stopword, negator, punctuation) per command family; no text stored. Shows what the model systematically deletes on real traffic, safe to paste in a bug report.
  - Test: Run in shadow mode for a week; compare each class's drop rate per family with the training-corpus mix; flag classes dropped far more often than in training.
- **#468** [X] `eval` **Stratified reservoir sampler with per-family quotas for local corpus** — Raw shadow logs would grow unbounded and skew toward frequent commands. Keep a fixed byte cap using weighted reservoir sampling keyed by command family, favoring rare families and evicting oldest-within-family, so the private corpus stays small and covers the long tail.
  - Test: Replay transcript tool results through the sampler at a 5 MB cap; compare family and format coverage against FIFO and uniform sampling at the same cap.
- **#469** [X] `eval` **Drift monitor: local template histogram versus shipped golden corpus** — Weekly, compare the user's command-family and line-template histograms, plus mean keep-probability entropy, against the shipped golden corpus using Jensen-Shannon divergence. A high score means the model and noise rules are stale for this user, so raise the pass-through threshold or flag relabeling.
  - Test: Compute divergence per week for 10 transcript sets; check whether high-divergence weeks show lower rule fire rates or more regret-log refetches.
- **#471** [X] `eval` **Forward-chaining holdout for rules mined from private logs** — Pack discovery would fit rules on the same sessions that score them. Mine on the earliest weeks or repos A, freeze, then score on later weeks or repos B; report savings and false-fire decay. Guards against rules that only fit one project.
  - Test: Split transcripts by month and by repo; mine templates on the early split; compare token savings and rule fire rates on early versus late holdout.
- **#472** [X] `eval` **Near-duplicate clusters before splitting private corpus rows** — Public sources split by group, but private sessions repeat: reruns, forks, vendored dependencies and shared CI templates put near-identical outputs on both sides. MinHash-cluster rows first, split by cluster, and weight clusters once so reruns do not dominate training.
  - Test: On 1,000 captured outputs, report the share of test rows with a Jaccard-0.8 neighbor in train under group versus cluster splits; compare AUC on full versus de-leaked test.
- **#473** [X] `eval` **Default-deny corpus admission with per-row provenance** — Corpus builder admits a repo only with an opt-in marker file and a license file, after a secret/entropy scan; rejects everything else. Each row records ctxprune version, pre-pass and rule versions, and a salted content hash, so results are reproducible without sharing text.
  - Test: Dry-run over ~/.claude/projects; list admitted and rejected repos with reasons; assert rejected repos contribute zero bytes and manifests contain no raw text.
- **#845** [X] `eval` **Contamination guard: corpus miner skips already-compressed results** — If hooked sessions store ctxprune output in the transcript (open question, #295), a miner trains on the model's own deletions. Detect the note marker, then restore the original from the saved path or drop the row.
  - Test: Install the hook in a scratch session, run one large command, mine its JSONL; assert the row is flagged and the original restored byte for byte.
- **#849** [X] `eval` **Re-identification red team on exported templates and counters** — Grepping for names misses quasi-identifiers: rare package-version combinations, internal hostnames and ticket prefixes can fingerprint a project. Test exports by asking a model to guess repo, employer or stack, and by counting tokens absent from public package and word lists.
  - Test: Export from 20 local sessions; run the guess prompt and the public-vocabulary miss count; report uniquely identifying lines per export and what the user-review step catches.
- **#850** [X] `eval` **Row egress classes restrict which teacher or judge sees it** — Rows already sent to Claude in a session add no new recipient when Claude labels or judges them (retention terms may differ); a third-party model or hosted service does. Tag rows claude-seen, local-only or public; route teacher, judge and consensus calls by tag.
  - Test: Add the tag to the corpus manifest; assert a mocked non-Anthropic client refuses non-public rows; report what share of the private corpus each downstream use may touch.
- **#852** [X] `eval` **Lineage-tracked purge with per-session encrypted shards** — Store shadow rows in per-session shards encrypted under a key kept outside the cache directory; purging a session deletes its key. Derived templates, counters and sketches carry lineage ids, so a rebuild drops them and recomputes k counts.
  - Test: Purge one canary session; assert its shard is unreadable, the rebuilt template export lacks the canary lines, and affected k counts drop.
- **#853** [X] `eval` **Capture-recapture estimate of scrubber miss rate** — Run two or more independent secret and PII scanners over the corpus. Lincoln-Petersen estimates findings all scanners missed from their overlap, a recall estimate needing no labels. Scanners correlate on easy cases, so treat it as optimistic.
  - Test: Run gitleaks and detect-secrets on 500 outputs with 30 planted secrets; compare the estimated total with the known count and report the bias.
- **#854** [X] `eval` **Synthetic outputs regenerated from shared templates for evals** — Templates plus per-slot type distributions (path, hex, integer, word) can sample new outputs shaped like a user's real ones. Share or evaluate on those instead of rows; only template constants survive. Differs from scrubbing real rows (#465).
  - Test: From 20 sessions regenerate 200 outputs; compare token counts, zlib ratio and classifier keep-rate distributions with real rows; accept if drift is under 5 percent.
- **#1205** [X] `eval` **Per-install HMAC pseudonyms for cross-session path and repo joins** — Analytics like the orientation re-read census (#689) must recognise the same path across sessions. Store HMAC(install key, path, host or repo) instead of names; deleting the key unlinks all history. Differs from per-document random fill (#465).
  - Test: Compute the re-read census with raw paths and with HMAC ids and assert identical counts; grep the store for path substrings; rotate the key and confirm joins break.
- **#1206** [X] `eval` **Hash-assigned train/dev/test splits stable under growth and purge** — Seeded random splits reshuffle whenever rows are added or a session is purged, leaking old test rows into train. Assign each near-duplicate cluster (#472) to a split by HMAC(cluster id) modulo 100, so membership never changes across rebuilds.
  - Test: Build the corpus at week 1 and week 4 with 10 percent of sessions purged; count surviving rows that changed split, seeded shuffle versus hash assignment.
- **#1207** [X] `eval` **Exclude corpus rows by tool-call path before content scan** — Secret regexes (#370) miss confidential prose. Gate on the call instead: Read or cat of .env, *.pem, id_rsa, .aws/credentials, .npmrc, kubeconfig, or repos with a private remote are never admitted, whatever the content scan says.
  - Test: Over local transcripts count Bash and Read calls matching the denylist; run a gitleaks-style scan on their outputs; report rows only the path rule caught, plus false exclusions.
- **#1208** [X] `eval` **Scrub the conditioning side: commands and prompts in corpus rows** — Command-conditioned training (#166) and query boosts store the command and last user prompt beside the output. Commands carry tokens in curl headers, connection strings and -p flags; prompts carry names. Keep argv shape (flags, value classes) and keyword-overlap features, not text.
  - Test: Scan local Bash commands and user prompts with the secret scanner; count hits absent from outputs; compare classifier AUC under raw versus argv-shape conditioning.
